--- SOURCE: python_samples/compiler_lexer.py ---
"""Minnow language -- part 1: the lexer.

Self-contained: tokenises Minnow source into tokens with line/column
positions.  Run it to see the demo:  python3 compiler_lexer.py

Part of the Minnow compiler series (compiler_lexer / compiler_parser /
compiler_bytecode / compiler_vm / compiler_treewalk / compiler_repl).
All code in this series is original work written for the START project;
it uses only the Python standard library.
"""




"""Lexer for the Minnow language.

Minnow is a small, original, dynamically typed language created for
this project.  Its syntax is deliberately familiar (C-like blocks,
``let`` bindings, ``fn`` functions); every line here is original work.

The lexer turns source text into tokens.  Each token records its type,
its literal value, and the line/column where it starts, so later stages
can report precise errors.  Supported input:

* line comments starting with ``#`` or ``//``
* integers (``42``) and floats (``3.14``, ``1e3``)
* double-quoted and single-quoted strings with ``\\n \\t \\r \\\\ \\" \\'``
  escapes (unknown escapes are kept literally)
* identifiers (``[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*``)
* keywords: ``let fn if else while return true false nil and or not``
* operators: ``+ - * / % == != < <= > >= =``
* punctuation: ``( ) { } [ ] , ;``
"""

KEYWORDS = {
    "let", "fn", "if", "else", "while", "return",
    "true", "false", "nil", "and", "or", "not",
}

# Single-character tokens: character -> token type.
_SINGLE = {
    "(": "LPAREN", ")": "RPAREN",
    "{": "LBRACE", "}": "RBRACE",
    "[": "LBRACKET", "]": "RBRACKET",
    ",": "COMMA", ";": "SEMICOLON",
    "+": "PLUS", "-": "MINUS", "*": "STAR",
    "/": "SLASH", "%": "PERCENT",
}

# Two-character operators, checked before the single-character ones.
_DOUBLE = {
    "==": "EQ", "!=": "NEQ",
    "<=": "LE", ">=": "GE",
}

_ESCAPES = {
    "n": "\n", "t": "\t", "r": "\r",
    "\\": "\\", '"': '"', "'": "'",
    "0": "\0",
}


class LexError(Exception):
    """Raised when the source text cannot be tokenised."""

    def __init__(self, message, line, column):
        super().__init__("line %d, column %d: %s" % (line, column, message))
        self.message = message
        self.line = line
        self.column = column


class Token:
    """One lexical unit of Minnow source code."""

    __slots__ = ("kind", "value", "line", "column")

    def __init__(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.kind = kind        # e.g. "INT", "STRING", "IDENT", "LET"
        self.value = value      # Python value: int/float/str/bool/None
        self.line = line        # 1-based line where the token starts
        self.column = column    # 1-based column where the token starts

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Token(%s, %r, %d:%d)" % (
            self.kind, self.value, self.line, self.column)


class Lexer:
    """A hand-written scanner for Minnow source text."""

    def __init__(self, source):
        self.source = source
        self.length = len(source)
        self.pos = 0
        self.line = 1
        self.column = 1
        self.tokens = []

    # -- low level cursor helpers --------------------------------------

    def _peek(self, offset=0):
        index = self.pos + offset
        if index < self.length:
            return self.source[index]
        return ""

    def _advance(self):
        ch = self.source[self.pos]
        self.pos += 1
        if ch == "\n":
            self.line += 1
            self.column = 1
        else:
            self.column += 1
        return ch

    def _error(self, message):
        raise LexError(message, self.line, self.column)

    def _emit(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.tokens.append(Token(kind, value, line, column))

    # -- public entry point ---------------------------------------------

    def tokenize(self):
        """Scan the whole source and return the list of tokens."""
        while self.pos < self.length:
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch in " \t\r":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "\n":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "#" or (ch == "/" and self._peek(1) == "/"):
                self._skip_comment()
            elif ch.isdigit():
                self._lex_number()
            elif ch == '"' or ch == "'":
                self._lex_string()
            elif ch.isalpha() or ch == "_":
                self._lex_identifier()
            elif ch == "=" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("==")
            elif ch == "!" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("!=")
            elif ch == "<" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("<=")
            elif ch == ">" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double(">=")
            elif ch in _SINGLE:
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit(_SINGLE[ch], ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "<":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("LT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == ">":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("GT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "=":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("ASSIGN", ch, line, column)
            else:
                self._error("unexpected character %r" % ch)
        self.tokens.append(Token("EOF", None, self.line, self.column))
        return self.tokens

    # -- individual token kinds -----------------------------------------

    def _skip_comment(self):
        while self.pos < self.length and self._peek() != "\n":
            self._advance()

    def _lex_double(self, text):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        self._advance()
        self._advance()
        self._emit(_DOUBLE[text], text, line, column)

    def _lex_number(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isdigit():
            self._advance()
        is_float = False
        if self._peek() == "." and self._peek(1).isdigit():
            is_float = True
            self._advance()
            while self._peek().isdigit():
                self._advance()
        if self._peek() in "eE":
            nxt = self._peek(1)
            nxt2 = self._peek(2)
            if nxt.isdigit() or (nxt in "+-" and nxt2.isdigit()):
                is_float = True
                self._advance()
                if self._peek() in "+-":
                    self._advance()
                while self._peek().isdigit():
                    self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if is_float:
            self._emit("FLOAT", float(text), line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("INT", int(text), line, column)

    def _lex_string(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        quote = self._advance()
        chars = []
        while True:
            if self.pos >= self.length:
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch == quote:
                self._advance()
                break
            if ch == "\n":
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            if ch == "\\":
                self._advance()
                esc = self._peek()
                if esc == "":
                    self._error("unterminated string literal")
                self._advance()
                if esc in _ESCAPES:
                    chars.append(_ESCAPES[esc])
                else:
                    # Unknown escapes are kept literally (backslash + char),
                    # which is friendlier than failing on e.g. "\d".
                    chars.append("\\")
                    chars.append(esc)
            else:
                chars.append(self._advance())
        self._emit("STRING", "".join(chars), line, column)

    def _lex_identifier(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isalnum() or self._peek() == "_":
            self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if text in KEYWORDS:
            self._emit(text.upper(), text, line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("IDENT", text, line, column)


def tokenize(source):
    """Convenience wrapper: return the token list for *source*."""
    return Lexer(source).tokenize()



# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test: tokenise a sample program, check token kinds,
# values and positions, and check that bad input fails cleanly.
# Run:  python3 compiler_lexer.py
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

_LEXER_SAMPLE = '''# a comment line
// and a second comment style
let answer = 42;
let pi = 3.14;
let big = 1e3;
fn greet(name) {
  return "hi, " + name + "!";
}
print(greet("world"));
let xs = [1, 2, 3];
if (answer >= 42 and not false) { print("yes"); }
'''


def main():
    tokens = tokenize(_LEXER_SAMPLE)
    print("tokens for the sample program:")
    for token in tokens:
        print("  %-10s %-12r %d:%d" % (
            token.kind, token.value, token.line, token.column))

    kinds = [t.kind for t in tokens]
    assert kinds.count("LET") == 4, kinds
    assert kinds.count("FN") == 1
    assert kinds.count("IF") == 1
    assert kinds.count("STRING") == 4, kinds
    assert tokens[-1].kind == "EOF"

    # Numbers: ints, floats and exponents.
    numbers = tokenize("1 2.5 1e3 0.5 100")
    values = [t.value for t in numbers if t.kind in ("INT", "FLOAT")]
    assert values == [1, 2.5, 1000.0, 0.5, 100], values
    assert [t.kind for t in numbers if t.kind in ("INT", "FLOAT")] == [
        "INT", "FLOAT", "FLOAT", "FLOAT", "INT"]

    # String escapes.
    escaped = tokenize(r'"a\nb\t\"q\"\\\'"')[0]
    assert escaped.value == 'a\nb\t"q"\\\'', repr(escaped.value)
    single = tokenize("'it\\'s'")[0]
    assert single.value == "it's", repr(single.value)

    # Positions are tracked across lines.
    positioned = tokenize("let x = 1;\nlet y = 2;")
    second_let = [t for t in positioned if t.kind == "LET"][1]
    assert (second_let.line, second_let.column) == (2, 1), second_let
    ident_y = [t for t in positioned if t.value == "y"][0]
    assert (ident_y.line, ident_y.column) == (2, 5), ident_y

    # Comments (both styles) produce no tokens.
    commented = tokenize("# only a comment\n// and another\n")
    assert [t.kind for t in commented] == ["EOF"], commented

    # Keywords are distinct from identifiers.
    assert tokenize("letter")[0].kind == "IDENT"
    assert tokenize("let")[0].kind == "LET"
    assert tokenize("true false nil")[0].kind == "TRUE"

    # Bad input fails with a positioned LexError.
    for bad in ('"unterminated', "let x = 1 @ 2;", "let x = 'oops;"):
        try:
            tokenize(bad)
        except LexError as exc:
            print("caught expected lexer error: %s" % exc)
        else:
            raise AssertionError("bad input was accepted: %r" % bad)

    print("lexer demo OK: %d tokens in the sample program" % len(tokens))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

--- SOURCE: python_samples/compiler_parser.py ---
"""Minnow language -- part 2: lexer + AST + recursive-descent parser.

Self-contained: lexes Minnow source and parses it into an abstract
syntax tree.  Run it to see the demo:  python3 compiler_parser.py

Part of the Minnow compiler series (compiler_lexer / compiler_parser /
compiler_bytecode / compiler_vm / compiler_treewalk / compiler_repl).
All code in this series is original work written for the START project;
it uses only the Python standard library.
"""




"""Lexer for the Minnow language.

Minnow is a small, original, dynamically typed language created for
this project.  Its syntax is deliberately familiar (C-like blocks,
``let`` bindings, ``fn`` functions); every line here is original work.

The lexer turns source text into tokens.  Each token records its type,
its literal value, and the line/column where it starts, so later stages
can report precise errors.  Supported input:

* line comments starting with ``#`` or ``//``
* integers (``42``) and floats (``3.14``, ``1e3``)
* double-quoted and single-quoted strings with ``\\n \\t \\r \\\\ \\" \\'``
  escapes (unknown escapes are kept literally)
* identifiers (``[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*``)
* keywords: ``let fn if else while return true false nil and or not``
* operators: ``+ - * / % == != < <= > >= =``
* punctuation: ``( ) { } [ ] , ;``
"""

KEYWORDS = {
    "let", "fn", "if", "else", "while", "return",
    "true", "false", "nil", "and", "or", "not",
}

# Single-character tokens: character -> token type.
_SINGLE = {
    "(": "LPAREN", ")": "RPAREN",
    "{": "LBRACE", "}": "RBRACE",
    "[": "LBRACKET", "]": "RBRACKET",
    ",": "COMMA", ";": "SEMICOLON",
    "+": "PLUS", "-": "MINUS", "*": "STAR",
    "/": "SLASH", "%": "PERCENT",
}

# Two-character operators, checked before the single-character ones.
_DOUBLE = {
    "==": "EQ", "!=": "NEQ",
    "<=": "LE", ">=": "GE",
}

_ESCAPES = {
    "n": "\n", "t": "\t", "r": "\r",
    "\\": "\\", '"': '"', "'": "'",
    "0": "\0",
}


class LexError(Exception):
    """Raised when the source text cannot be tokenised."""

    def __init__(self, message, line, column):
        super().__init__("line %d, column %d: %s" % (line, column, message))
        self.message = message
        self.line = line
        self.column = column


class Token:
    """One lexical unit of Minnow source code."""

    __slots__ = ("kind", "value", "line", "column")

    def __init__(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.kind = kind        # e.g. "INT", "STRING", "IDENT", "LET"
        self.value = value      # Python value: int/float/str/bool/None
        self.line = line        # 1-based line where the token starts
        self.column = column    # 1-based column where the token starts

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Token(%s, %r, %d:%d)" % (
            self.kind, self.value, self.line, self.column)


class Lexer:
    """A hand-written scanner for Minnow source text."""

    def __init__(self, source):
        self.source = source
        self.length = len(source)
        self.pos = 0
        self.line = 1
        self.column = 1
        self.tokens = []

    # -- low level cursor helpers --------------------------------------

    def _peek(self, offset=0):
        index = self.pos + offset
        if index < self.length:
            return self.source[index]
        return ""

    def _advance(self):
        ch = self.source[self.pos]
        self.pos += 1
        if ch == "\n":
            self.line += 1
            self.column = 1
        else:
            self.column += 1
        return ch

    def _error(self, message):
        raise LexError(message, self.line, self.column)

    def _emit(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.tokens.append(Token(kind, value, line, column))

    # -- public entry point ---------------------------------------------

    def tokenize(self):
        """Scan the whole source and return the list of tokens."""
        while self.pos < self.length:
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch in " \t\r":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "\n":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "#" or (ch == "/" and self._peek(1) == "/"):
                self._skip_comment()
            elif ch.isdigit():
                self._lex_number()
            elif ch == '"' or ch == "'":
                self._lex_string()
            elif ch.isalpha() or ch == "_":
                self._lex_identifier()
            elif ch == "=" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("==")
            elif ch == "!" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("!=")
            elif ch == "<" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("<=")
            elif ch == ">" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double(">=")
            elif ch in _SINGLE:
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit(_SINGLE[ch], ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "<":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("LT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == ">":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("GT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "=":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("ASSIGN", ch, line, column)
            else:
                self._error("unexpected character %r" % ch)
        self.tokens.append(Token("EOF", None, self.line, self.column))
        return self.tokens

    # -- individual token kinds -----------------------------------------

    def _skip_comment(self):
        while self.pos < self.length and self._peek() != "\n":
            self._advance()

    def _lex_double(self, text):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        self._advance()
        self._advance()
        self._emit(_DOUBLE[text], text, line, column)

    def _lex_number(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isdigit():
            self._advance()
        is_float = False
        if self._peek() == "." and self._peek(1).isdigit():
            is_float = True
            self._advance()
            while self._peek().isdigit():
                self._advance()
        if self._peek() in "eE":
            nxt = self._peek(1)
            nxt2 = self._peek(2)
            if nxt.isdigit() or (nxt in "+-" and nxt2.isdigit()):
                is_float = True
                self._advance()
                if self._peek() in "+-":
                    self._advance()
                while self._peek().isdigit():
                    self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if is_float:
            self._emit("FLOAT", float(text), line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("INT", int(text), line, column)

    def _lex_string(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        quote = self._advance()
        chars = []
        while True:
            if self.pos >= self.length:
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch == quote:
                self._advance()
                break
            if ch == "\n":
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            if ch == "\\":
                self._advance()
                esc = self._peek()
                if esc == "":
                    self._error("unterminated string literal")
                self._advance()
                if esc in _ESCAPES:
                    chars.append(_ESCAPES[esc])
                else:
                    # Unknown escapes are kept literally (backslash + char),
                    # which is friendlier than failing on e.g. "\d".
                    chars.append("\\")
                    chars.append(esc)
            else:
                chars.append(self._advance())
        self._emit("STRING", "".join(chars), line, column)

    def _lex_identifier(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isalnum() or self._peek() == "_":
            self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if text in KEYWORDS:
            self._emit(text.upper(), text, line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("IDENT", text, line, column)


def tokenize(source):
    """Convenience wrapper: return the token list for *source*."""
    return Lexer(source).tokenize()


"""Abstract syntax tree and recursive-descent parser for Minnow.

Grammar (precedence from loosest to tightest):

    program     := statement*
    statement   := let-stmt | fn-decl | if-stmt | while-stmt | return-stmt
                 | block | expr-stmt
    let-stmt    := "let" IDENT "=" expr ";"
    fn-decl     := "fn" IDENT "(" params? ")" block
    if-stmt     := "if" "(" expr ")" block ("else" (if-stmt | block))?
    while-stmt  := "while" "(" expr ")" block
    return-stmt := "return" expr? ";"
    block       := "{" statement* "}"
    expr-stmt   := expr ("=" expr)? ";"        (assignment form)
    expr        := or-expr
    or-expr     := and-expr ("or" and-expr)*
    and-expr    := equality ("and" equality)*
    equality    := comparison (("==" | "!=") comparison)*
    comparison  := additive (("<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=") additive)*
    additive    := multiplicative (("+" | "-") multiplicative)*
    multiplicative := unary (("*" | "/" | "%") unary)*
    unary       := ("-" | "not") unary | postfix
    postfix     := primary ( "(" args? ")" | "[" expr "]" )*
    primary     := INT | FLOAT | STRING | "true" | "false" | "nil"
                 | IDENT | "(" expr ")" | "[" elements? "]"

Scoping rule (shared by both interpreters): ``let`` declares a variable
in the *enclosing function* (or at top level, in the global scope).
Blocks do not introduce a new scope; this matches JavaScript ``var``
and keeps the bytecode compiler and the tree-walking interpreter in
exact agreement.
"""


class ParseError(Exception):
    """Raised when the token stream does not match the grammar."""

    def __init__(self, message, token):
        super().__init__("line %d, column %d: %s" % (
            token.line, token.column, message))
        self.message = message
        self.token = token


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# AST nodes.  Each node is a small plain class so the tree is easy to
# inspect, print, compile and walk.  ``children()`` yields the child
# nodes, which the free-variable analysis and the pretty printer use.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

class Node:
    def children(self):
        return ()

    def __repr__(self):
        fields = ", ".join(
            "%s=%r" % (k, v) for k, v in vars(self).items())
        return "%s(%s)" % (type(self).__name__, fields)


class Program(Node):
    def __init__(self, statements):
        self.statements = statements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.statements)


class Block(Node):
    def __init__(self, statements):
        self.statements = statements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.statements)


class Let(Node):
    def __init__(self, name, value):
        self.name = name
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return (self.value,)


class Assign(Node):
    """Assignment to a variable or an indexed element: ``x = v`` / ``a[i] = v``."""

    def __init__(self, target, value):
        self.target = target
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return (self.target, self.value)


class FnDecl(Node):
    def __init__(self, name, params, body):
        self.name = name
        self.params = params
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.body,)


class FnExpr(Node):
    """An anonymous function literal (the value side of ``let f = fn(...)``)."""

    def __init__(self, params, body):
        self.params = params
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.body,)


class If(Node):
    def __init__(self, condition, then_branch, else_branch):
        self.condition = condition
        self.then_branch = then_branch
        self.else_branch = else_branch

    def children(self):
        kids = [self.condition, self.then_branch]
        if self.else_branch is not None:
            kids.append(self.else_branch)
        return tuple(kids)


class While(Node):
    def __init__(self, condition, body):
        self.condition = condition
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.condition, self.body)


class Return(Node):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return () if self.value is None else (self.value,)


class ExprStmt(Node):
    def __init__(self, expr):
        self.expr = expr

    def children(self):
        return (self.expr,)


class Binary(Node):
    def __init__(self, op, left, right):
        self.op = op
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def children(self):
        return (self.left, self.right)


class Unary(Node):
    def __init__(self, op, operand):
        self.op = op
        self.operand = operand

    def children(self):
        return (self.operand,)


class Call(Node):
    def __init__(self, func, args):
        self.func = func
        self.args = args

    def children(self):
        return (self.func,) + tuple(self.args)


class Index(Node):
    def __init__(self, obj, index):
        self.obj = obj
        self.index = index

    def children(self):
        return (self.obj, self.index)


class Var(Node):
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name

    def children(self):
        return ()


class Literal(Node):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return ()


class ListLit(Node):
    def __init__(self, elements):
        self.elements = elements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.elements)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Parser
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

class Parser:
    """Recursive-descent parser: one method per grammar rule."""

    def __init__(self, tokens):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.pos = 0

    # -- cursor helpers -------------------------------------------------

    def _peek(self):
        return self.tokens[self.pos]

    def _peek_kind(self):
        return self.tokens[self.pos].kind

    def _advance(self):
        token = self.tokens[self.pos]
        if self.pos < len(self.tokens) - 1:
            self.pos += 1
        return token

    def _check(self, kind):
        return self._peek_kind() == kind

    def _match(self, *kinds):
        if self._peek_kind() in kinds:
            return self._advance()
        return None

    def _expect(self, kind, what=""):
        if self._check(kind):
            return self._advance()
        token = self._peek()
        raise ParseError(
            "expected %s but found %s" % (what or kind, token.kind), token)

    def _skip_semicolons(self):
        while self._check("SEMICOLON"):
            self._advance()

    # -- entry point ----------------------------------------------------

    def parse_program(self):
        statements = []
        while not self._check("EOF"):
            self._skip_semicolons()
            if self._check("EOF"):
                break
            statements.append(self._parse_statement())
        return Program(statements)

    # -- statements -----------------------------------------------------

    def _parse_statement(self):
        kind = self._peek_kind()
        if kind == "LET":
            return self._parse_let()
        if kind == "FN":
            return self._parse_fn_decl()
        if kind == "IF":
            return self._parse_if()
        if kind == "WHILE":
            return self._parse_while()
        if kind == "RETURN":
            return self._parse_return()
        if kind == "LBRACE":
            return self._parse_block()
        return self._parse_expr_statement()

    def _parse_let(self):
        self._advance()  # let
        name = self._expect("IDENT", "a variable name").value
        self._expect("ASSIGN", "'='")
        value = self._parse_expr()
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return Let(name, value)

    def _parse_fn_decl(self):
        self._advance()  # fn
        name = self._expect("IDENT", "a function name").value
        params, body = self._parse_fn_tail()
        return FnDecl(name, params, body)

    def _parse_fn_tail(self):
        """Parse ``(params) block`` after ``fn`` or ``fn name``."""
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        params = []
        if not self._check("RPAREN"):
            while True:
                params.append(self._expect("IDENT", "a parameter name").value)
                if not self._match("COMMA"):
                    break
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        body = self._parse_block()
        return params, body

    def _parse_if(self):
        self._advance()  # if
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        condition = self._parse_expr()
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        then_branch = self._parse_block()
        else_branch = None
        if self._match("ELSE"):
            if self._check("IF"):
                else_branch = self._parse_if()
            else:
                else_branch = self._parse_block()
        return If(condition, then_branch, else_branch)

    def _parse_while(self):
        self._advance()  # while
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        condition = self._parse_expr()
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        body = self._parse_block()
        return While(condition, body)

    def _parse_return(self):
        self._advance()  # return
        value = None
        if not self._check("SEMICOLON") and not self._check("RBRACE") \
                and not self._check("EOF"):
            value = self._parse_expr()
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return Return(value)

    def _parse_block(self):
        self._expect("LBRACE", "'{'")
        statements = []
        while not self._check("RBRACE"):
            if self._check("EOF"):
                raise ParseError("unterminated block", self._peek())
            self._skip_semicolons()
            if self._check("RBRACE"):
                break
            statements.append(self._parse_statement())
        self._expect("RBRACE", "'}'")
        return Block(statements)

    def _parse_expr_statement(self):
        expr = self._parse_expr()
        if self._check("ASSIGN"):
            if not isinstance(expr, (Var, Index)):
                raise ParseError(
                    "invalid assignment target", self._peek())
            self._advance()  # =
            value = self._parse_expr()
            self._skip_semicolons()
            return Assign(expr, value)
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return ExprStmt(expr)

    # -- expressions (precedence climbing by method call chain) ---------

    def _parse_expr(self):
        return self._parse_or()

    def _parse_or(self):
        left = self._parse_and()
        while self._check("OR"):
            self._advance()
            left = Binary("or", left, self._parse_and())
        return left

    def _parse_and(self):
        left = self._parse_equality()
        while self._check("AND"):
            self._advance()
            left = Binary("and", left, self._parse_equality())
        return left

    def _parse_equality(self):
        left = self._parse_comparison()
        while self._check("EQ") or self._check("NEQ"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_comparison())
        return left

    def _parse_comparison(self):
        left = self._parse_additive()
        while self._peek_kind() in ("LT", "LE", "GT", "GE"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_additive())
        return left

    def _parse_additive(self):
        left = self._parse_multiplicative()
        while self._check("PLUS") or self._check("MINUS"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_multiplicative())
        return left

    def _parse_multiplicative(self):
        left = self._parse_unary()
        while self._peek_kind() in ("STAR", "SLASH", "PERCENT"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_unary())
        return left

    def _parse_unary(self):
        if self._check("MINUS"):
            self._advance()
            return Unary("-", self._parse_unary())
        if self._check("NOT"):
            self._advance()
            return Unary("not", self._parse_unary())
        return self._parse_postfix()

    def _parse_postfix(self):
        expr = self._parse_primary()
        while True:
            if self._check("LPAREN"):
                self._advance()
                args = []
                if not self._check("RPAREN"):
                    while True:
                        args.append(self._parse_expr())
                        if not self._match("COMMA"):
                            break
                self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
                expr = Call(expr, args)
            elif self._check("LBRACKET"):
                self._advance()
                index = self._parse_expr()
                self._expect("RBRACKET", "']'")
                expr = Index(expr, index)
            else:
                break
        return expr

    def _parse_primary(self):
        token = self._peek()
        kind = token.kind
        if kind == "INT" or kind == "FLOAT" or kind == "STRING":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(token.value)
        if kind == "TRUE":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(True)
        if kind == "FALSE":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(False)
        if kind == "NIL":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(None)
        if kind == "IDENT":
            self._advance()
            return Var(token.value)
        if kind == "FN":
            self._advance()
            params, body = self._parse_fn_tail()
            return FnExpr(params, body)
        if kind == "LPAREN":
            self._advance()
            expr = self._parse_expr()
            self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
            return expr
        if kind == "LBRACKET":
            self._advance()
            elements = []
            if not self._check("RBRACKET"):
                while True:
                    elements.append(self._parse_expr())
                    if not self._match("COMMA"):
                        break
            self._expect("RBRACKET", "']'")
            return ListLit(elements)
        raise ParseError("unexpected token %s" % kind, token)


def parse(source):
    """Convenience wrapper: lex *source* and return its Program AST."""
    return Parser(tokenize(source)).parse_program()


def dump(node, indent=0):
    """Pretty-print an AST node (and its children) as an indented tree."""
    pad = "  " * indent
    if isinstance(node, Literal):
        return "%sLiteral(%r)" % (pad, node.value)
    if isinstance(node, Var):
        return "%sVar(%s)" % (pad, node.name)
    if isinstance(node, (Binary, Unary)):
        lines = ["%s%s(%s)" % (pad, type(node).__name__, node.op)]
        for child in node.children():
            lines.append(dump(child, indent + 1))
        return "\n".join(lines)
    if isinstance(node, (FnDecl, FnExpr)):
        header = "%s%s(%s, params=%s)" % (
            pad, type(node).__name__,
            getattr(node, "name", ""), ", ".join(node.params))
        return header + "\n" + dump(node.body, indent + 1)
    if isinstance(node, Let):
        return "%sLet(%s)\n%s" % (
            pad, node.name, dump(node.value, indent + 1))
    lines = ["%s%s" % (pad, type(node).__name__)]
    for child in node.children():
        lines.append(dump(child, indent + 1))
    return "\n".join(lines)



# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test: parse sample programs, print their ASTs, and check
# precedence, structure and error reporting.
# Run:  python3 compiler_parser.py
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

_PARSER_SAMPLE = """
fn fact(n) {
  if (n <= 1) { return 1; }
  return n * fact(n - 1);
}
let xs = [3, 1, 2];
let total = 0;
let i = 0;
while (i < len(xs)) {
  total = total + xs[i];
  i = i + 1;
}
print(fact(5) + total);
"""


def main():
    program = parse(_PARSER_SAMPLE)
    print("AST of the sample program:")
    print(dump(program))
    assert isinstance(program, Program)
    assert len(program.statements) == 6, len(program.statements)

    fact = program.statements[0]
    assert isinstance(fact, FnDecl)
    assert fact.name == "fact" and fact.params == ["n"]
    assert isinstance(fact.body, Block)

    # Operator precedence: 1 + 2 * 3 is 1 + (2 * 3), not (1 + 2) * 3.
    expr = parse("1 + 2 * 3;").statements[0].expr
    assert isinstance(expr, Binary) and expr.op == "+"
    assert isinstance(expr.right, Binary) and expr.right.op == "*"
    assert expr.right.left.value == 2 and expr.right.right.value == 3

    # Comparison binds looser than addition; equality looser still.
    expr = parse("1 + 2 < 4 == true;").statements[0].expr
    assert expr.op == "==" and expr.left.op == "<"

    # "and" binds tighter than "or"; unary minus binds tightest.
    expr = parse("true or false and not true;").statements[0].expr
    assert expr.op == "or" and expr.right.op == "and"
    expr = parse("-2 * 3;").statements[0].expr
    assert expr.op == "*" and isinstance(expr.left, Unary)

    # Calls and indexing chain: f(x)[0](y) parses as nested postfix.
    expr = parse("f(x)[0](y);").statements[0].expr
    assert isinstance(expr, Call)
    assert isinstance(expr.func, Index)
    assert isinstance(expr.func.obj, Call)

    # Anonymous functions, list literals, else-if chains.
    fn_expr = parse("let f = fn(x) { return x * 2; };").statements[0]
    assert isinstance(fn_expr, Let) and isinstance(fn_expr.value, FnExpr)
    assert fn_expr.value.params == ["x"]
    lst = parse("[1, 2, 3];").statements[0].expr
    assert isinstance(lst, ListLit) and len(lst.elements) == 3
    if_node = parse("if (a) { print(1); } else if (b) { print(2); } "
                   "else { print(3); };").statements[0]
    assert isinstance(if_node, If)
    assert isinstance(if_node.else_branch, If)
    assert isinstance(if_node.else_branch.else_branch, Block)

    # Assignment targets: plain variables and indexed elements.
    assign = parse("xs[0] = 9;").statements[0]
    assert isinstance(assign, Assign) and isinstance(assign.target, Index)
    assign = parse("x = 1;").statements[0]
    assert isinstance(assign, Assign) and isinstance(assign.target, Var)

    # Free-standing expressions and empty blocks are fine.
    assert parse("print(1); 42;").statements[1].expr.value == 42
    assert parse("fn f() {}").statements[0].body.statements == []

    # Broken programs fail with a positioned ParseError.
    for bad in ("let x = ;", "fn f( { }", "if (true) { print(1);",
                "1 + ;", "let 3x = 1;"):
        try:
            parse(bad)
        except ParseError as exc:
            print("caught expected parse error: %s" % exc)
        else:
            raise AssertionError("bad program was accepted: %r" % bad)

    print("parser demo OK")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

--- SOURCE: python_samples/compiler_bytecode.py ---
"""Minnow language -- part 3: lexer + parser + bytecode compiler.

Self-contained: compiles Minnow source to bytecode for the stack VM
(see compiler_vm.py) and can disassemble it.  The compiler resolves
names to locals, captured upvalues (closures) or globals, and emits
jumps for if/while and short-circuit logic.
Run it to see the demo:  python3 compiler_bytecode.py

Part of the Minnow compiler series (compiler_lexer / compiler_parser /
compiler_bytecode / compiler_vm / compiler_treewalk / compiler_repl).
All code in this series is original work written for the START project;
it uses only the Python standard library.
"""




"""Lexer for the Minnow language.

Minnow is a small, original, dynamically typed language created for
this project.  Its syntax is deliberately familiar (C-like blocks,
``let`` bindings, ``fn`` functions); every line here is original work.

The lexer turns source text into tokens.  Each token records its type,
its literal value, and the line/column where it starts, so later stages
can report precise errors.  Supported input:

* line comments starting with ``#`` or ``//``
* integers (``42``) and floats (``3.14``, ``1e3``)
* double-quoted and single-quoted strings with ``\\n \\t \\r \\\\ \\" \\'``
  escapes (unknown escapes are kept literally)
* identifiers (``[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*``)
* keywords: ``let fn if else while return true false nil and or not``
* operators: ``+ - * / % == != < <= > >= =``
* punctuation: ``( ) { } [ ] , ;``
"""

KEYWORDS = {
    "let", "fn", "if", "else", "while", "return",
    "true", "false", "nil", "and", "or", "not",
}

# Single-character tokens: character -> token type.
_SINGLE = {
    "(": "LPAREN", ")": "RPAREN",
    "{": "LBRACE", "}": "RBRACE",
    "[": "LBRACKET", "]": "RBRACKET",
    ",": "COMMA", ";": "SEMICOLON",
    "+": "PLUS", "-": "MINUS", "*": "STAR",
    "/": "SLASH", "%": "PERCENT",
}

# Two-character operators, checked before the single-character ones.
_DOUBLE = {
    "==": "EQ", "!=": "NEQ",
    "<=": "LE", ">=": "GE",
}

_ESCAPES = {
    "n": "\n", "t": "\t", "r": "\r",
    "\\": "\\", '"': '"', "'": "'",
    "0": "\0",
}


class LexError(Exception):
    """Raised when the source text cannot be tokenised."""

    def __init__(self, message, line, column):
        super().__init__("line %d, column %d: %s" % (line, column, message))
        self.message = message
        self.line = line
        self.column = column


class Token:
    """One lexical unit of Minnow source code."""

    __slots__ = ("kind", "value", "line", "column")

    def __init__(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.kind = kind        # e.g. "INT", "STRING", "IDENT", "LET"
        self.value = value      # Python value: int/float/str/bool/None
        self.line = line        # 1-based line where the token starts
        self.column = column    # 1-based column where the token starts

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Token(%s, %r, %d:%d)" % (
            self.kind, self.value, self.line, self.column)


class Lexer:
    """A hand-written scanner for Minnow source text."""

    def __init__(self, source):
        self.source = source
        self.length = len(source)
        self.pos = 0
        self.line = 1
        self.column = 1
        self.tokens = []

    # -- low level cursor helpers --------------------------------------

    def _peek(self, offset=0):
        index = self.pos + offset
        if index < self.length:
            return self.source[index]
        return ""

    def _advance(self):
        ch = self.source[self.pos]
        self.pos += 1
        if ch == "\n":
            self.line += 1
            self.column = 1
        else:
            self.column += 1
        return ch

    def _error(self, message):
        raise LexError(message, self.line, self.column)

    def _emit(self, kind, value, line, column):
        self.tokens.append(Token(kind, value, line, column))

    # -- public entry point ---------------------------------------------

    def tokenize(self):
        """Scan the whole source and return the list of tokens."""
        while self.pos < self.length:
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch in " \t\r":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "\n":
                self._advance()
            elif ch == "#" or (ch == "/" and self._peek(1) == "/"):
                self._skip_comment()
            elif ch.isdigit():
                self._lex_number()
            elif ch == '"' or ch == "'":
                self._lex_string()
            elif ch.isalpha() or ch == "_":
                self._lex_identifier()
            elif ch == "=" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("==")
            elif ch == "!" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("!=")
            elif ch == "<" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double("<=")
            elif ch == ">" and self._peek(1) == "=":
                self._lex_double(">=")
            elif ch in _SINGLE:
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit(_SINGLE[ch], ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "<":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("LT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == ">":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("GT", ch, line, column)
            elif ch == "=":
                line, column = self.line, self.column
                self._advance()
                self._emit("ASSIGN", ch, line, column)
            else:
                self._error("unexpected character %r" % ch)
        self.tokens.append(Token("EOF", None, self.line, self.column))
        return self.tokens

    # -- individual token kinds -----------------------------------------

    def _skip_comment(self):
        while self.pos < self.length and self._peek() != "\n":
            self._advance()

    def _lex_double(self, text):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        self._advance()
        self._advance()
        self._emit(_DOUBLE[text], text, line, column)

    def _lex_number(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isdigit():
            self._advance()
        is_float = False
        if self._peek() == "." and self._peek(1).isdigit():
            is_float = True
            self._advance()
            while self._peek().isdigit():
                self._advance()
        if self._peek() in "eE":
            nxt = self._peek(1)
            nxt2 = self._peek(2)
            if nxt.isdigit() or (nxt in "+-" and nxt2.isdigit()):
                is_float = True
                self._advance()
                if self._peek() in "+-":
                    self._advance()
                while self._peek().isdigit():
                    self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if is_float:
            self._emit("FLOAT", float(text), line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("INT", int(text), line, column)

    def _lex_string(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        quote = self._advance()
        chars = []
        while True:
            if self.pos >= self.length:
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            ch = self._peek()
            if ch == quote:
                self._advance()
                break
            if ch == "\n":
                self._error("unterminated string literal")
            if ch == "\\":
                self._advance()
                esc = self._peek()
                if esc == "":
                    self._error("unterminated string literal")
                self._advance()
                if esc in _ESCAPES:
                    chars.append(_ESCAPES[esc])
                else:
                    # Unknown escapes are kept literally (backslash + char),
                    # which is friendlier than failing on e.g. "\d".
                    chars.append("\\")
                    chars.append(esc)
            else:
                chars.append(self._advance())
        self._emit("STRING", "".join(chars), line, column)

    def _lex_identifier(self):
        line, column = self.line, self.column
        start = self.pos
        while self._peek().isalnum() or self._peek() == "_":
            self._advance()
        text = self.source[start:self.pos]
        if text in KEYWORDS:
            self._emit(text.upper(), text, line, column)
        else:
            self._emit("IDENT", text, line, column)


def tokenize(source):
    """Convenience wrapper: return the token list for *source*."""
    return Lexer(source).tokenize()


"""Abstract syntax tree and recursive-descent parser for Minnow.

Grammar (precedence from loosest to tightest):

    program     := statement*
    statement   := let-stmt | fn-decl | if-stmt | while-stmt | return-stmt
                 | block | expr-stmt
    let-stmt    := "let" IDENT "=" expr ";"
    fn-decl     := "fn" IDENT "(" params? ")" block
    if-stmt     := "if" "(" expr ")" block ("else" (if-stmt | block))?
    while-stmt  := "while" "(" expr ")" block
    return-stmt := "return" expr? ";"
    block       := "{" statement* "}"
    expr-stmt   := expr ("=" expr)? ";"        (assignment form)
    expr        := or-expr
    or-expr     := and-expr ("or" and-expr)*
    and-expr    := equality ("and" equality)*
    equality    := comparison (("==" | "!=") comparison)*
    comparison  := additive (("<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=") additive)*
    additive    := multiplicative (("+" | "-") multiplicative)*
    multiplicative := unary (("*" | "/" | "%") unary)*
    unary       := ("-" | "not") unary | postfix
    postfix     := primary ( "(" args? ")" | "[" expr "]" )*
    primary     := INT | FLOAT | STRING | "true" | "false" | "nil"
                 | IDENT | "(" expr ")" | "[" elements? "]"

Scoping rule (shared by both interpreters): ``let`` declares a variable
in the *enclosing function* (or at top level, in the global scope).
Blocks do not introduce a new scope; this matches JavaScript ``var``
and keeps the bytecode compiler and the tree-walking interpreter in
exact agreement.
"""


class ParseError(Exception):
    """Raised when the token stream does not match the grammar."""

    def __init__(self, message, token):
        super().__init__("line %d, column %d: %s" % (
            token.line, token.column, message))
        self.message = message
        self.token = token


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# AST nodes.  Each node is a small plain class so the tree is easy to
# inspect, print, compile and walk.  ``children()`` yields the child
# nodes, which the free-variable analysis and the pretty printer use.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

class Node:
    def children(self):
        return ()

    def __repr__(self):
        fields = ", ".join(
            "%s=%r" % (k, v) for k, v in vars(self).items())
        return "%s(%s)" % (type(self).__name__, fields)


class Program(Node):
    def __init__(self, statements):
        self.statements = statements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.statements)


class Block(Node):
    def __init__(self, statements):
        self.statements = statements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.statements)


class Let(Node):
    def __init__(self, name, value):
        self.name = name
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return (self.value,)


class Assign(Node):
    """Assignment to a variable or an indexed element: ``x = v`` / ``a[i] = v``."""

    def __init__(self, target, value):
        self.target = target
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return (self.target, self.value)


class FnDecl(Node):
    def __init__(self, name, params, body):
        self.name = name
        self.params = params
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.body,)


class FnExpr(Node):
    """An anonymous function literal (the value side of ``let f = fn(...)``)."""

    def __init__(self, params, body):
        self.params = params
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.body,)


class If(Node):
    def __init__(self, condition, then_branch, else_branch):
        self.condition = condition
        self.then_branch = then_branch
        self.else_branch = else_branch

    def children(self):
        kids = [self.condition, self.then_branch]
        if self.else_branch is not None:
            kids.append(self.else_branch)
        return tuple(kids)


class While(Node):
    def __init__(self, condition, body):
        self.condition = condition
        self.body = body

    def children(self):
        return (self.condition, self.body)


class Return(Node):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return () if self.value is None else (self.value,)


class ExprStmt(Node):
    def __init__(self, expr):
        self.expr = expr

    def children(self):
        return (self.expr,)


class Binary(Node):
    def __init__(self, op, left, right):
        self.op = op
        self.left = left
        self.right = right

    def children(self):
        return (self.left, self.right)


class Unary(Node):
    def __init__(self, op, operand):
        self.op = op
        self.operand = operand

    def children(self):
        return (self.operand,)


class Call(Node):
    def __init__(self, func, args):
        self.func = func
        self.args = args

    def children(self):
        return (self.func,) + tuple(self.args)


class Index(Node):
    def __init__(self, obj, index):
        self.obj = obj
        self.index = index

    def children(self):
        return (self.obj, self.index)


class Var(Node):
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name

    def children(self):
        return ()


class Literal(Node):
    def __init__(self, value):
        self.value = value

    def children(self):
        return ()


class ListLit(Node):
    def __init__(self, elements):
        self.elements = elements

    def children(self):
        return tuple(self.elements)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Parser
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

class Parser:
    """Recursive-descent parser: one method per grammar rule."""

    def __init__(self, tokens):
        self.tokens = tokens
        self.pos = 0

    # -- cursor helpers -------------------------------------------------

    def _peek(self):
        return self.tokens[self.pos]

    def _peek_kind(self):
        return self.tokens[self.pos].kind

    def _advance(self):
        token = self.tokens[self.pos]
        if self.pos < len(self.tokens) - 1:
            self.pos += 1
        return token

    def _check(self, kind):
        return self._peek_kind() == kind

    def _match(self, *kinds):
        if self._peek_kind() in kinds:
            return self._advance()
        return None

    def _expect(self, kind, what=""):
        if self._check(kind):
            return self._advance()
        token = self._peek()
        raise ParseError(
            "expected %s but found %s" % (what or kind, token.kind), token)

    def _skip_semicolons(self):
        while self._check("SEMICOLON"):
            self._advance()

    # -- entry point ----------------------------------------------------

    def parse_program(self):
        statements = []
        while not self._check("EOF"):
            self._skip_semicolons()
            if self._check("EOF"):
                break
            statements.append(self._parse_statement())
        return Program(statements)

    # -- statements -----------------------------------------------------

    def _parse_statement(self):
        kind = self._peek_kind()
        if kind == "LET":
            return self._parse_let()
        if kind == "FN":
            return self._parse_fn_decl()
        if kind == "IF":
            return self._parse_if()
        if kind == "WHILE":
            return self._parse_while()
        if kind == "RETURN":
            return self._parse_return()
        if kind == "LBRACE":
            return self._parse_block()
        return self._parse_expr_statement()

    def _parse_let(self):
        self._advance()  # let
        name = self._expect("IDENT", "a variable name").value
        self._expect("ASSIGN", "'='")
        value = self._parse_expr()
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return Let(name, value)

    def _parse_fn_decl(self):
        self._advance()  # fn
        name = self._expect("IDENT", "a function name").value
        params, body = self._parse_fn_tail()
        return FnDecl(name, params, body)

    def _parse_fn_tail(self):
        """Parse ``(params) block`` after ``fn`` or ``fn name``."""
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        params = []
        if not self._check("RPAREN"):
            while True:
                params.append(self._expect("IDENT", "a parameter name").value)
                if not self._match("COMMA"):
                    break
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        body = self._parse_block()
        return params, body

    def _parse_if(self):
        self._advance()  # if
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        condition = self._parse_expr()
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        then_branch = self._parse_block()
        else_branch = None
        if self._match("ELSE"):
            if self._check("IF"):
                else_branch = self._parse_if()
            else:
                else_branch = self._parse_block()
        return If(condition, then_branch, else_branch)

    def _parse_while(self):
        self._advance()  # while
        self._expect("LPAREN", "'('")
        condition = self._parse_expr()
        self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
        body = self._parse_block()
        return While(condition, body)

    def _parse_return(self):
        self._advance()  # return
        value = None
        if not self._check("SEMICOLON") and not self._check("RBRACE") \
                and not self._check("EOF"):
            value = self._parse_expr()
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return Return(value)

    def _parse_block(self):
        self._expect("LBRACE", "'{'")
        statements = []
        while not self._check("RBRACE"):
            if self._check("EOF"):
                raise ParseError("unterminated block", self._peek())
            self._skip_semicolons()
            if self._check("RBRACE"):
                break
            statements.append(self._parse_statement())
        self._expect("RBRACE", "'}'")
        return Block(statements)

    def _parse_expr_statement(self):
        expr = self._parse_expr()
        if self._check("ASSIGN"):
            if not isinstance(expr, (Var, Index)):
                raise ParseError(
                    "invalid assignment target", self._peek())
            self._advance()  # =
            value = self._parse_expr()
            self._skip_semicolons()
            return Assign(expr, value)
        self._skip_semicolons()
        return ExprStmt(expr)

    # -- expressions (precedence climbing by method call chain) ---------

    def _parse_expr(self):
        return self._parse_or()

    def _parse_or(self):
        left = self._parse_and()
        while self._check("OR"):
            self._advance()
            left = Binary("or", left, self._parse_and())
        return left

    def _parse_and(self):
        left = self._parse_equality()
        while self._check("AND"):
            self._advance()
            left = Binary("and", left, self._parse_equality())
        return left

    def _parse_equality(self):
        left = self._parse_comparison()
        while self._check("EQ") or self._check("NEQ"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_comparison())
        return left

    def _parse_comparison(self):
        left = self._parse_additive()
        while self._peek_kind() in ("LT", "LE", "GT", "GE"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_additive())
        return left

    def _parse_additive(self):
        left = self._parse_multiplicative()
        while self._check("PLUS") or self._check("MINUS"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_multiplicative())
        return left

    def _parse_multiplicative(self):
        left = self._parse_unary()
        while self._peek_kind() in ("STAR", "SLASH", "PERCENT"):
            op = self._advance().value
            left = Binary(op, left, self._parse_unary())
        return left

    def _parse_unary(self):
        if self._check("MINUS"):
            self._advance()
            return Unary("-", self._parse_unary())
        if self._check("NOT"):
            self._advance()
            return Unary("not", self._parse_unary())
        return self._parse_postfix()

    def _parse_postfix(self):
        expr = self._parse_primary()
        while True:
            if self._check("LPAREN"):
                self._advance()
                args = []
                if not self._check("RPAREN"):
                    while True:
                        args.append(self._parse_expr())
                        if not self._match("COMMA"):
                            break
                self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
                expr = Call(expr, args)
            elif self._check("LBRACKET"):
                self._advance()
                index = self._parse_expr()
                self._expect("RBRACKET", "']'")
                expr = Index(expr, index)
            else:
                break
        return expr

    def _parse_primary(self):
        token = self._peek()
        kind = token.kind
        if kind == "INT" or kind == "FLOAT" or kind == "STRING":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(token.value)
        if kind == "TRUE":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(True)
        if kind == "FALSE":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(False)
        if kind == "NIL":
            self._advance()
            return Literal(None)
        if kind == "IDENT":
            self._advance()
            return Var(token.value)
        if kind == "FN":
            self._advance()
            params, body = self._parse_fn_tail()
            return FnExpr(params, body)
        if kind == "LPAREN":
            self._advance()
            expr = self._parse_expr()
            self._expect("RPAREN", "')'")
            return expr
        if kind == "LBRACKET":
            self._advance()
            elements = []
            if not self._check("RBRACKET"):
                while True:
                    elements.append(self._parse_expr())
                    if not self._match("COMMA"):
                        break
            self._expect("RBRACKET", "']'")
            return ListLit(elements)
        raise ParseError("unexpected token %s" % kind, token)


def parse(source):
    """Convenience wrapper: lex *source* and return its Program AST."""
    return Parser(tokenize(source)).parse_program()


def dump(node, indent=0):
    """Pretty-print an AST node (and its children) as an indented tree."""
    pad = "  " * indent
    if isinstance(node, Literal):
        return "%sLiteral(%r)" % (pad, node.value)
    if isinstance(node, Var):
        return "%sVar(%s)" % (pad, node.name)
    if isinstance(node, (Binary, Unary)):
        lines = ["%s%s(%s)" % (pad, type(node).__name__, node.op)]
        for child in node.children():
            lines.append(dump(child, indent + 1))
        return "\n".join(lines)
    if isinstance(node, (FnDecl, FnExpr)):
        header = "%s%s(%s, params=%s)" % (
            pad, type(node).__name__,
            getattr(node, "name", ""), ", ".join(node.params))
        return header + "\n" + dump(node.body, indent + 1)
    if isinstance(node, Let):
        return "%sLet(%s)\n%s" % (
            pad, node.name, dump(node.value, indent + 1))
    lines = ["%s%s" % (pad, type(node).__name__)]
    for child in node.children():
        lines.append(dump(child, indent + 1))
    return "\n".join(lines)


"""Bytecode compiler for Minnow.

The compiler translates the AST into instructions for a small stack-based
virtual machine (see compiler_vm.py).  It owns the instruction set:

    LOAD_CONST n     push constants[n]
    LOAD_GLOBAL n    push globals[names[n]] (falling back to builtins)
    STORE_GLOBAL n   pop into globals[names[n]]
    LOAD_LOCAL s     push frame.cells[s].value
    STORE_LOCAL s    pop into frame.cells[s].value
    LOAD_UPVALUE u   push frame.upvalues[u].value
    STORE_UPVALUE u  pop into frame.upvalues[u].value
    POP              discard the top of the stack
    DUP              duplicate the top of the stack
    BUILD_LIST n     pop n values, push a list of them
    INDEX            pop index and object, push object[index]
    STORE_INDEX      pop value, index, object; object[index] = value
    ADD SUB MUL DIV MOD
                     pop right and left, push left <op> right
    NEG              pop a value, push its negation
    NOT              pop a value, push its logical negation
    EQ NEQ LT LE GT GE
                     pop right and left, push the comparison result
    JUMP a           set ip = a
    JUMP_IF_FALSE a  pop a value; if falsy, set ip = a
    JUMP_IF_TRUE a   pop a value; if truthy, set ip = a
    CALL n           pop n arguments and a callable, push the result
    MAKE_FUNCTION c  push a closure over code object constants[c]
    RETURN           return the top of the stack to the caller

Locals are cells, so a nested function can capture a variable from an
enclosing function by sharing the same cell object: assignment through
a closure is visible outside it and vice versa.  The compiler resolves
each name to (local | upvalue | global) by walking the enclosing
function scopes; a name found in an outer function is recorded as an
upvalue in every scope between the use and the definition, like
CPython's free-variable handling.

Scoping rule: ``let`` declares in the enclosing function (or globally),
never in a bare block.  A ``let`` whose value is a function literal
pre-declares its own name first, so a local function can call itself.
"""

# -- opcode numbers (kept stable: the disassembler and VM share them) ---

LOAD_CONST = 0
LOAD_GLOBAL = 1
STORE_GLOBAL = 2
LOAD_LOCAL = 3
STORE_LOCAL = 4
LOAD_UPVALUE = 5
STORE_UPVALUE = 6
POP = 7
DUP = 8
BUILD_LIST = 9
INDEX = 10
STORE_INDEX = 11
ADD = 12
SUB = 13
MUL = 14
DIV = 15
MOD = 16
NEG = 17
NOT = 18
EQ = 19
NEQ = 20
LT = 21
LE = 22
GT = 23
GE = 24
JUMP = 25
JUMP_IF_FALSE = 26
JUMP_IF_TRUE = 27
CALL = 28
MAKE_FUNCTION = 29
RETURN = 30

OPNAMES = {
    LOAD_CONST: "LOAD_CONST", LOAD_GLOBAL: "LOAD_GLOBAL",
    STORE_GLOBAL: "STORE_GLOBAL", LOAD_LOCAL: "LOAD_LOCAL",
    STORE_LOCAL: "STORE_LOCAL", LOAD_UPVALUE: "LOAD_UPVALUE",
    STORE_UPVALUE: "STORE_UPVALUE", POP: "POP", DUP: "DUP",
    BUILD_LIST: "BUILD_LIST", INDEX: "INDEX",
    STORE_INDEX: "STORE_INDEX", ADD: "ADD", SUB: "SUB", MUL: "MUL",
    DIV: "DIV", MOD: "MOD", NEG: "NEG", NOT: "NOT",
    EQ: "EQ", NEQ: "NEQ", LT: "LT", LE: "LE", GT: "GT", GE: "GE",
    JUMP: "JUMP", JUMP_IF_FALSE: "JUMP_IF_FALSE",
    JUMP_IF_TRUE: "JUMP_IF_TRUE", CALL: "CALL",
    MAKE_FUNCTION: "MAKE_FUNCTION", RETURN: "RETURN",
}

_BINOP = {
    "+": ADD, "-": SUB, "*": MUL, "/": DIV, "%": MOD,
    "==": EQ, "!=": NEQ, "<": LT, "<=": LE, ">": GT, ">=": GE,
}


class CompileError(Exception):
    """Raised when a program cannot be compiled (e.g. too many locals)."""


class Code:
    """A compiled unit of Minnow bytecode: a script or one function."""

    def __init__(self, name, arity):
        self.name = name
        self.arity = arity
        self.instructions = []   # list of [opcode, arg]
        self.constants = []      # literals and nested Code objects
        self.names = []          # global names used by this unit
        self.varnames = []       # local slot names, in slot order
        self.upvalue_sources = []  # ("local"|"upvalue", index) per upvalue
        self.upvalue_names = []    # name of each upvalue, for debugging

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Code(%s, arity=%d, %d instrs)" % (
            self.name, self.arity, len(self.instructions))


class Scope:
    """One function scope (the script counts as the outermost scope)."""

    def __init__(self, parent, is_function):
        self.parent = parent
        self.is_function = is_function
        self.locals = {}          # name -> slot index
        self.varnames = []        # slot index -> name
        self.upvalues = {}        # name -> upvalue index
        self.upvalue_sources = []  # upvalue index -> ("local"|"upvalue", idx)
        self.upvalue_names = []    # upvalue index -> name


class Compiler:
    """Walks the AST and emits bytecode into Code objects."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.code = None
        self.scope = None

    # -- code object helpers --------------------------------------------

    def _add_constant(self, value):
        # Deduplicate constants, but only within the same type: in
        # Python True == 1 and 1 == 1.0, and merging those would silently
        # change a program's meaning (``n - 1`` loading True, for one).
        for i, existing in enumerate(self.code.constants):
            if existing is value:
                return i
            if type(existing) is type(value) and existing == value:
                return i
        self.code.constants.append(value)
        return len(self.code.constants) - 1

    def _add_name(self, name):
        if name in self.code.names:
            return self.code.names.index(name)
        self.code.names.append(name)
        return len(self.code.names) - 1

    def _emit(self, op, arg=0):
        self.code.instructions.append([op, arg])
        return len(self.code.instructions) - 1

    def _patch(self, addr, target):
        self.code.instructions[addr][1] = target

    # -- name resolution --------------------------------------------------

    def _declare(self, name):
        """Reserve a local slot for *name* in the current function scope."""
        scope = self.scope
        if not scope.is_function:
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.locals:
            return ("local", scope.locals[name])
        slot = len(scope.varnames)
        scope.locals[name] = slot
        scope.varnames.append(name)
        return ("local", slot)

    def _resolve_from(self, scope, name):
        """Resolve *name* starting at an enclosing scope.

        Returns ("local", slot), ("upvalue", index) or ("global", name).
        Every scope between the use and the definition gains an upvalue
        entry so the cell can be threaded through at runtime.
        """
        if scope is None:
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.locals:
            return ("local", scope.locals[name])
        kind, index = self._resolve_from(scope.parent, name)
        if kind == "global":
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.upvalues:
            return ("upvalue", scope.upvalues[name])
        new_index = len(scope.upvalue_sources)
        scope.upvalues[name] = new_index
        scope.upvalue_sources.append((kind, index))
        scope.upvalue_names.append(name)
        return ("upvalue", new_index)

    def _resolve(self, name):
        """Resolve *name* from the current scope outward.

        Returns ("local", slot) for a local of the current function,
        ("upvalue", index) for a variable captured from an enclosing
        function, or ("global", name).  The walk starts at the current
        scope so that a name found further out is recorded as an
        upvalue in every scope between the use and the definition.
        """
        return self._resolve_from(self.scope, name)

    def _emit_load(self, name):
        kind, index = self._resolve(name)
        if kind == "local":
            self._emit(LOAD_LOCAL, index)
        elif kind == "upvalue":
            self._emit(LOAD_UPVALUE, index)
        else:
            self._emit(LOAD_GLOBAL, self._add_name(name))

    def _emit_store(self, name):
        kind, index = self._resolve(name)
        if kind == "local":
            self._emit(STORE_LOCAL, index)
        elif kind == "upvalue":
            self._emit(STORE_UPVALUE, index)
        else:
            self._emit(STORE_GLOBAL, self._add_name(name))

    # -- entry points -----------------------------------------------------

    def compile_program(self, program):
        """Compile a whole program; returns the top-level Code object."""
        self.code = Code("<script>", 0)
        self.scope = Scope(None, is_function=False)
        for stmt in program.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        self._emit(RETURN)
        self.code.varnames = list(self.scope.varnames)
        return self.code

    def _compile_function(self, name, params, body):
        """Compile one function body into a fresh Code object."""
        saved_code, saved_scope = self.code, self.scope
        self.code = Code(name, len(params))
        self.scope = Scope(saved_scope, is_function=True)
        for param in params:
            self._declare(param)
        for stmt in body.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        self._emit(RETURN)
        code = self.code
        code.varnames = list(self.scope.varnames)
        code.upvalue_sources = list(self.scope.upvalue_sources)
        code.upvalue_names = list(self.scope.upvalue_names)
        self.code, self.scope = saved_code, saved_scope
        return code

    # -- statements ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _compile_stmt(self, node):
        method = getattr(self, "_stmt_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise CompileError("cannot compile statement %r" % node)
        method(node)

    def _stmt_Let(self, node):
        # A function literal must see its own name (for recursion), so
        # declare the slot before compiling the value.
        if isinstance(node.value, FnExpr):
            self._declare(node.name)
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._emit_store(node.name)
        else:
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._declare(node.name)
            self._emit_store(node.name)

    def _stmt_Assign(self, node):
        if isinstance(node.target, Var):
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            # Assigning to a name that no scope knows yet creates a global.
            kind, index = self._resolve(node.target.name)
            if kind == "local":
                self._emit(STORE_LOCAL, index)
            elif kind == "upvalue":
                self._emit(STORE_UPVALUE, index)
            else:
                self._emit(STORE_GLOBAL, self._add_name(node.target.name))
        else:  # Index target: obj[index] = value
            self._compile_expr(node.target.obj)
            self._compile_expr(node.target.index)
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._emit(STORE_INDEX)

    def _stmt_FnDecl(self, node):
        self._declare(node.name)
        code = self._compile_function(node.name, node.params, node.body)
        const_index = self._add_constant(code)
        self._emit(MAKE_FUNCTION, const_index)
        self._emit_store(node.name)

    def _stmt_If(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.condition)
        jump_false = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
        self._compile_stmt(node.then_branch)
        if node.else_branch is None:
            self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))
        else:
            jump_end = self._emit(JUMP)
            self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))
            self._compile_stmt(node.else_branch)
            self._patch(jump_end, len(self.code.instructions))

    def _stmt_While(self, node):
        loop_start = len(self.code.instructions)
        self._compile_expr(node.condition)
        jump_false = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
        self._compile_stmt(node.body)
        self._emit(JUMP, loop_start)
        self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))

    def _stmt_Return(self, node):
        if node.value is None:
            self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        else:
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
        self._emit(RETURN)

    def _stmt_Block(self, node):
        for stmt in node.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)

    def _stmt_ExprStmt(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.expr)
        self._emit(POP)

    # -- expressions ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _compile_expr(self, node):
        method = getattr(self, "_expr_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise CompileError("cannot compile expression %r" % node)
        method(node)

    def _expr_Literal(self, node):
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(node.value))

    def _expr_Var(self, node):
        self._emit_load(node.name)

    def _expr_ListLit(self, node):
        for element in node.elements:
            self._compile_expr(element)
        self._emit(BUILD_LIST, len(node.elements))

    def _expr_Index(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.obj)
        self._compile_expr(node.index)
        self._emit(INDEX)

    def _expr_Unary(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.operand)
        if node.op == "-":
            self._emit(NEG)
        elif node.op == "not":
            self._emit(NOT)
        else:
            raise CompileError("unknown unary operator %r" % node.op)

    def _expr_Binary(self, node):
        if node.op == "and":
            self._compile_expr(node.left)
            self._emit(DUP)
            jump = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
            self._emit(POP)
            self._compile_expr(node.right)
            self._patch(jump, len(self.code.instructions))
            return
        if node.op == "or":
            self._compile_expr(node.left)
            self._emit(DUP)
            jump = self._emit(JUMP_IF_TRUE)
            self._emit(POP)
            self._compile_expr(node.right)
            self._patch(jump, len(self.code.instructions))
            return
        self._compile_expr(node.left)
        self._compile_expr(node.right)
        self._emit(_BINOP[node.op])

    def _expr_Call(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.func)
        for arg in node.args:
            self._compile_expr(arg)
        self._emit(CALL, len(node.args))

    def _expr_FnExpr(self, node):
        code = self._compile_function("<fn>", node.params, node.body)
        const_index = self._add_constant(code)
        self._emit(MAKE_FUNCTION, const_index)


def compile_source(source):
    """Lex, parse and compile *source*; returns the top-level Code object."""
    return Compiler().compile_program(parse(source))


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Disassembler: prints bytecode in a readable form, used by the demos
# and by the REPL's :dis command.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def disassemble(code, indent=0):
    """Return a multi-line string disassembling *code* (recursively)."""
    pad = "  " * indent
    lines = ["%s%s/%d vars=%s upvalues=%s" % (
        pad, code.name, code.arity, code.varnames, code.upvalue_names)]
    for addr, (op, arg) in enumerate(code.instructions):
        name = OPNAMES.get(op, "?%d" % op)
        detail = ""
        if op == LOAD_CONST:
            value = code.constants[arg]
            if isinstance(value, Code):
                detail = "<code %s>" % value.name
            else:
                detail = repr(value)
        elif op in (LOAD_GLOBAL, STORE_GLOBAL):
            detail = code.names[arg]
        elif op in (LOAD_LOCAL, STORE_LOCAL):
            if arg < len(code.varnames):
                detail = code.varnames[arg]
        elif op in (LOAD_UPVALUE, STORE_UPVALUE):
            if arg < len(code.upvalue_names):
                detail = code.upvalue_names[arg]
        elif op == MAKE_FUNCTION:
            value = code.constants[arg]
