        self.names = []          # global names used by this unit
        self.varnames = []       # local slot names, in slot order
        self.upvalue_sources = []  # ("local"|"upvalue", index) per upvalue
        self.upvalue_names = []    # name of each upvalue, for debugging

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Code(%s, arity=%d, %d instrs)" % (
            self.name, self.arity, len(self.instructions))


class Scope:
    """One function scope (the script counts as the outermost scope)."""

    def __init__(self, parent, is_function):
        self.parent = parent
        self.is_function = is_function
        self.locals = {}          # name -> slot index
        self.varnames = []        # slot index -> name
        self.upvalues = {}        # name -> upvalue index
        self.upvalue_sources = []  # upvalue index -> ("local"|"upvalue", idx)
        self.upvalue_names = []    # upvalue index -> name


class Compiler:
    """Walks the AST and emits bytecode into Code objects."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.code = None
        self.scope = None

    # -- code object helpers --------------------------------------------

    def _add_constant(self, value):
        # Deduplicate constants, but only within the same type: in
        # Python True == 1 and 1 == 1.0, and merging those would silently
        # change a program's meaning (``n - 1`` loading True, for one).
        for i, existing in enumerate(self.code.constants):
            if existing is value:
                return i
            if type(existing) is type(value) and existing == value:
                return i
        self.code.constants.append(value)
        return len(self.code.constants) - 1

    def _add_name(self, name):
        if name in self.code.names:
            return self.code.names.index(name)
        self.code.names.append(name)
        return len(self.code.names) - 1

    def _emit(self, op, arg=0):
        self.code.instructions.append([op, arg])
        return len(self.code.instructions) - 1

    def _patch(self, addr, target):
        self.code.instructions[addr][1] = target

    # -- name resolution --------------------------------------------------

    def _declare(self, name):
        """Reserve a local slot for *name* in the current function scope."""
        scope = self.scope
        if not scope.is_function:
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.locals:
            return ("local", scope.locals[name])
        slot = len(scope.varnames)
        scope.locals[name] = slot
        scope.varnames.append(name)
        return ("local", slot)

    def _resolve_from(self, scope, name):
        """Resolve *name* starting at an enclosing scope.

        Returns ("local", slot), ("upvalue", index) or ("global", name).
        Every scope between the use and the definition gains an upvalue
        entry so the cell can be threaded through at runtime.
        """
        if scope is None:
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.locals:
            return ("local", scope.locals[name])
        kind, index = self._resolve_from(scope.parent, name)
        if kind == "global":
            return ("global", name)
        if name in scope.upvalues:
            return ("upvalue", scope.upvalues[name])
        new_index = len(scope.upvalue_sources)
        scope.upvalues[name] = new_index
        scope.upvalue_sources.append((kind, index))
        scope.upvalue_names.append(name)
        return ("upvalue", new_index)

    def _resolve(self, name):
        """Resolve *name* from the current scope outward.

        Returns ("local", slot) for a local of the current function,
        ("upvalue", index) for a variable captured from an enclosing
        function, or ("global", name).  The walk starts at the current
        scope so that a name found further out is recorded as an
        upvalue in every scope between the use and the definition.
        """
        return self._resolve_from(self.scope, name)

    def _emit_load(self, name):
        kind, index = self._resolve(name)
        if kind == "local":
            self._emit(LOAD_LOCAL, index)
        elif kind == "upvalue":
            self._emit(LOAD_UPVALUE, index)
        else:
            self._emit(LOAD_GLOBAL, self._add_name(name))

    def _emit_store(self, name):
        kind, index = self._resolve(name)
        if kind == "local":
            self._emit(STORE_LOCAL, index)
        elif kind == "upvalue":
            self._emit(STORE_UPVALUE, index)
        else:
            self._emit(STORE_GLOBAL, self._add_name(name))

    # -- entry points -----------------------------------------------------

    def compile_program(self, program):
        """Compile a whole program; returns the top-level Code object."""
        self.code = Code("<script>", 0)
        self.scope = Scope(None, is_function=False)
        for stmt in program.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        self._emit(RETURN)
        self.code.varnames = list(self.scope.varnames)
        return self.code

    def _compile_function(self, name, params, body):
        """Compile one function body into a fresh Code object."""
        saved_code, saved_scope = self.code, self.scope
        self.code = Code(name, len(params))
        self.scope = Scope(saved_scope, is_function=True)
        for param in params:
            self._declare(param)
        for stmt in body.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        self._emit(RETURN)
        code = self.code
        code.varnames = list(self.scope.varnames)
        code.upvalue_sources = list(self.scope.upvalue_sources)
        code.upvalue_names = list(self.scope.upvalue_names)
        self.code, self.scope = saved_code, saved_scope
        return code

    # -- statements ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _compile_stmt(self, node):
        method = getattr(self, "_stmt_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise CompileError("cannot compile statement %r" % node)
        method(node)

    def _stmt_Let(self, node):
        # A function literal must see its own name (for recursion), so
        # declare the slot before compiling the value.
        if isinstance(node.value, FnExpr):
            self._declare(node.name)
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._emit_store(node.name)
        else:
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._declare(node.name)
            self._emit_store(node.name)

    def _stmt_Assign(self, node):
        if isinstance(node.target, Var):
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            # Assigning to a name that no scope knows yet creates a global.
            kind, index = self._resolve(node.target.name)
            if kind == "local":
                self._emit(STORE_LOCAL, index)
            elif kind == "upvalue":
                self._emit(STORE_UPVALUE, index)
            else:
                self._emit(STORE_GLOBAL, self._add_name(node.target.name))
        else:  # Index target: obj[index] = value
            self._compile_expr(node.target.obj)
            self._compile_expr(node.target.index)
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
            self._emit(STORE_INDEX)

    def _stmt_FnDecl(self, node):
        self._declare(node.name)
        code = self._compile_function(node.name, node.params, node.body)
        const_index = self._add_constant(code)
        self._emit(MAKE_FUNCTION, const_index)
        self._emit_store(node.name)

    def _stmt_If(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.condition)
        jump_false = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
        self._compile_stmt(node.then_branch)
        if node.else_branch is None:
            self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))
        else:
            jump_end = self._emit(JUMP)
            self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))
            self._compile_stmt(node.else_branch)
            self._patch(jump_end, len(self.code.instructions))

    def _stmt_While(self, node):
        loop_start = len(self.code.instructions)
        self._compile_expr(node.condition)
        jump_false = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
        self._compile_stmt(node.body)
        self._emit(JUMP, loop_start)
        self._patch(jump_false, len(self.code.instructions))

    def _stmt_Return(self, node):
        if node.value is None:
            self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(None))
        else:
            self._compile_expr(node.value)
        self._emit(RETURN)

    def _stmt_Block(self, node):
        for stmt in node.statements:
            self._compile_stmt(stmt)

    def _stmt_ExprStmt(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.expr)
        self._emit(POP)

    # -- expressions ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _compile_expr(self, node):
        method = getattr(self, "_expr_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise CompileError("cannot compile expression %r" % node)
        method(node)

    def _expr_Literal(self, node):
        self._emit(LOAD_CONST, self._add_constant(node.value))

    def _expr_Var(self, node):
        self._emit_load(node.name)

    def _expr_ListLit(self, node):
        for element in node.elements:
            self._compile_expr(element)
        self._emit(BUILD_LIST, len(node.elements))

    def _expr_Index(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.obj)
        self._compile_expr(node.index)
        self._emit(INDEX)

    def _expr_Unary(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.operand)
        if node.op == "-":
            self._emit(NEG)
        elif node.op == "not":
            self._emit(NOT)
        else:
            raise CompileError("unknown unary operator %r" % node.op)

    def _expr_Binary(self, node):
        if node.op == "and":
            self._compile_expr(node.left)
            self._emit(DUP)
            jump = self._emit(JUMP_IF_FALSE)
            self._emit(POP)
            self._compile_expr(node.right)
            self._patch(jump, len(self.code.instructions))
            return
        if node.op == "or":
            self._compile_expr(node.left)
            self._emit(DUP)
            jump = self._emit(JUMP_IF_TRUE)
            self._emit(POP)
            self._compile_expr(node.right)
            self._patch(jump, len(self.code.instructions))
            return
        self._compile_expr(node.left)
        self._compile_expr(node.right)
        self._emit(_BINOP[node.op])

    def _expr_Call(self, node):
        self._compile_expr(node.func)
        for arg in node.args:
            self._compile_expr(arg)
        self._emit(CALL, len(node.args))

    def _expr_FnExpr(self, node):
        code = self._compile_function("<fn>", node.params, node.body)
        const_index = self._add_constant(code)
        self._emit(MAKE_FUNCTION, const_index)


def compile_source(source):
    """Lex, parse and compile *source*; returns the top-level Code object."""
    return Compiler().compile_program(parse(source))


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Disassembler: prints bytecode in a readable form, used by the demos
# and by the REPL's :dis command.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

def disassemble(code, indent=0):
    """Return a multi-line string disassembling *code* (recursively)."""
    pad = "  " * indent
    lines = ["%s%s/%d vars=%s upvalues=%s" % (
        pad, code.name, code.arity, code.varnames, code.upvalue_names)]
    for addr, (op, arg) in enumerate(code.instructions):
        name = OPNAMES.get(op, "?%d" % op)
        detail = ""
        if op == LOAD_CONST:
            value = code.constants[arg]
            if isinstance(value, Code):
                detail = "<code %s>" % value.name
            else:
                detail = repr(value)
        elif op in (LOAD_GLOBAL, STORE_GLOBAL):
            detail = code.names[arg]
        elif op in (LOAD_LOCAL, STORE_LOCAL):
            if arg < len(code.varnames):
                detail = code.varnames[arg]
        elif op in (LOAD_UPVALUE, STORE_UPVALUE):
            if arg < len(code.upvalue_names):
                detail = code.upvalue_names[arg]
        elif op == MAKE_FUNCTION:
            value = code.constants[arg]
            detail = value.name if isinstance(value, Code) else repr(value)
        lines.append("%s%4d  %-16s %-4d %s" % (pad, addr, name, arg, detail))
    for const in code.constants:
        if isinstance(const, Code):
            lines.append(disassemble(const, indent + 1))
    return "\n".join(lines)


"""Stack-based virtual machine for Minnow bytecode.

The VM executes the instruction set defined in compiler_bytecode.py.  It
is deliberately iterative: frames live in an explicit list, so a deeply
recursive Minnow program (``fib(25)`` makes ~150,000 nested calls) never
touches the Python call stack and never hits Python's recursion limit.

Values are plain Python values: ``int``, ``float``, ``str``, ``bool``,
``None`` (``nil``), ``list``, plus ``Closure`` and ``BuiltinFunction``.
Truthiness: ``nil``, ``false``, ``0``, ``0.0``, ``""`` and ``[]`` are
falsy, everything else is truthy.  A frame holds a code object, an
instruction pointer, an operand stack, a list of local *cells* and a
tuple of captured upvalue cells; locals are cells (one-element boxes)
so a closure shares variables with its defining frame.

Builtins (``print``, ``len``, ``push``, ``pop``, ``str``, ``int``,
``float``, ``type``, ``range``, ``chr``, ``ord``, ``assert``) are
ordinary callables.  ``print`` writes into the VM's output list instead
of stdout, so test harnesses can capture it.
"""

MAX_STEPS = 20_000_000


class VMError(Exception):
    """A runtime error raised while executing Minnow bytecode."""


class Cell:
    """A one-element box shared between a frame and its closures."""

    __slots__ = ("value",)

    def __init__(self, value=None):
        self.value = value

    def __repr__(self):
        return "Cell(%r)" % (self.value,)


class Closure:
    """A Minnow function value: a code object plus its captured cells."""

    __slots__ = ("code", "cells")

    def __init__(self, code, cells):
        self.code = code
        self.cells = cells  # tuple of Cell, one per upvalue

    def __repr__(self):
        return "<fn %s>" % self.code.name


class BuiltinFunction:
    """A Minnow builtin: a Python callable exposed to Minnow programs."""

    __slots__ = ("name", "func")

    def __init__(self, name, func):
        self.name = name
        self.func = func

    def __repr__(self):
        return "<builtin %s>" % self.name


class Frame:
    __slots__ = ("code", "ip", "stack", "cells", "upvalues")

    def __init__(self, code, cells, upvalues):
        self.code = code
        self.ip = 0
        self.stack = []
        self.cells = cells      # list of Cell, one per local slot
        self.upvalues = upvalues  # tuple of Cell, one per upvalue


def is_truthy(value):
    """Minnow truthiness: nil/false/0/""/[] are falsy, the rest truthy."""
    if value is None or value is False:
        return False
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return value != 0
    if isinstance(value, (str, list)):
        return len(value) != 0
    return True


def stringify(value):
    """How a Minnow value looks when printed or converted with str()."""
    if value is None:
        return "nil"
    if value is True:
        return "true"
    if value is False:
        return "false"
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value
    if isinstance(value, list):
        return "[" + ", ".join(stringify(item) for item in value) + "]"
    if isinstance(value, Closure):
        return "<fn %s>" % value.code.name
    if isinstance(value, BuiltinFunction):
        return "<builtin %s>" % value.name
    return repr(value)


def type_name(value):
    if value is None:
        return "nil"
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return "bool"
    if isinstance(value, int):
        return "int"
    if isinstance(value, float):
        return "float"
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return "string"
    if isinstance(value, list):
        return "list"
    if isinstance(value, (Closure, BuiltinFunction)):
        return "function"
    return "unknown"


class VM:
    """Executes Minnow bytecode.  One VM instance = one global scope."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.globals = {}
        self.output = []  # lines produced by the print builtin
        self.builtins = {}
        self._install_builtins()

    # -- builtins ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _install_builtins(self):
        def _print(*args):
            self.output.append(" ".join(stringify(a) for a in args))

        def _len(value):
            if isinstance(value, (str, list)):
                return len(value)
            raise VMError("len() needs a string or a list, got %s"
                          % type_name(value))

        def _push(lst, value):
            if not isinstance(lst, list):
                raise VMError("push() needs a list, got %s" % type_name(lst))
            lst.append(value)
            return lst

        def _pop(lst):
            if not isinstance(lst, list):
                raise VMError("pop() needs a list, got %s" % type_name(lst))
            if not lst:
                raise VMError("pop() from an empty list")
            return lst.pop()

        def _str(value):
            return stringify(value)

        def _int(value):
            if isinstance(value, bool):
                return 1 if value else 0
            if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                return int(value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                try:
                    return int(value.strip())
                except ValueError:
                    raise VMError("int() cannot parse %r" % value)
            raise VMError("int() cannot convert %s" % type_name(value))

        def _float(value):
            if isinstance(value, bool):
                return 1.0 if value else 0.0
            if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                return float(value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                try:
                    return float(value.strip())
                except ValueError:
                    raise VMError("float() cannot parse %r" % value)
            raise VMError("float() cannot convert %s" % type_name(value))

        def _type(value):
            return type_name(value)

        def _range(*args):
            if len(args) == 1:
                return list(range(args[0]))
            if len(args) == 2:
                return list(range(args[0], args[1]))
            if len(args) == 3:
                return list(range(args[0], args[1], args[2]))
            raise VMError("range() takes 1 to 3 arguments")

        def _chr(value):
            if not isinstance(value, int):
                raise VMError("chr() needs an int")
            return chr(value)

        def _ord(value):
            if not isinstance(value, str) or len(value) != 1:
                raise VMError("ord() needs a one-character string")
            return ord(value)

        def _assert(condition, message="assertion failed"):
            if not is_truthy(condition):
                raise VMError(str(message))

        for name, func in [
            ("print", _print), ("len", _len), ("push", _push),
            ("pop", _pop), ("str", _str), ("int", _int),
            ("float", _float), ("type", _type), ("range", _range),
            ("chr", _chr), ("ord", _ord), ("assert", _assert),
        ]:
            self.builtins[name] = BuiltinFunction(name, func)

    # -- main loop ----------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, code):
        """Execute *code* (a top-level Code object).

        Returns ``(output_lines, result)`` where result is the value the
        script returned (``None`` unless it used a top-level ``return``).
        """
        self.output = []
        initial_cells = [Cell(None) for _ in code.varnames]
        frames = [Frame(code, initial_cells, ())]
        steps = 0
        while frames:
            frame = frames[-1]
            instructions = frame.code.instructions
            if frame.ip >= len(instructions):
                raise VMError("ran off the end of %s" % frame.code.name)
            op, arg = instructions[frame.ip]
            frame.ip += 1
            steps += 1
            if steps > MAX_STEPS:
                raise VMError("step limit exceeded (infinite loop?)")
            stack = frame.stack

            if op == LOAD_CONST:
                stack.append(frame.code.constants[arg])
            elif op == LOAD_GLOBAL:
                name = frame.code.names[arg]
                if name in self.globals:
                    stack.append(self.globals[name])
                elif name in self.builtins:
                    stack.append(self.builtins[name])
                else:
                    raise VMError("undefined variable %r" % name)
            elif op == STORE_GLOBAL:
                self.globals[frame.code.names[arg]] = stack.pop()
            elif op == LOAD_LOCAL:
                stack.append(frame.cells[arg].value)
            elif op == STORE_LOCAL:
                frame.cells[arg].value = stack.pop()
            elif op == LOAD_UPVALUE:
                stack.append(frame.upvalues[arg].value)
            elif op == STORE_UPVALUE:
                frame.upvalues[arg].value = stack.pop()
            elif op == POP:
                stack.pop()
            elif op == DUP:
                stack.append(stack[-1])
            elif op == BUILD_LIST:
                items = stack[len(stack) - arg:] if arg else []
                if arg:
                    del stack[len(stack) - arg:]
                stack.append(items)
            elif op == INDEX:
                index = stack.pop()
                obj = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._index(obj, index))
            elif op == STORE_INDEX:
                value = stack.pop()
                index = stack.pop()
                obj = stack.pop()
                self._store_index(obj, index, value)
            elif op == ADD:
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._binary(ADD, left, right))
            elif op == SUB:
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._binary(SUB, left, right))
            elif op == MUL:
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._binary(MUL, left, right))
            elif op == DIV:
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._binary(DIV, left, right))
            elif op == MOD:
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._binary(MOD, left, right))
            elif op == NEG:
                value = stack.pop()
                if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                    raise VMError("cannot negate %s" % type_name(value))
                stack.append(-value)
            elif op == NOT:
                stack.append(not is_truthy(stack.pop()))
            elif op in (EQ, NEQ, LT, LE, GT, GE):
                right = stack.pop()
                left = stack.pop()
                stack.append(self._compare(op, left, right))
            elif op == JUMP:
                frame.ip = arg
            elif op == JUMP_IF_FALSE:
                if not is_truthy(stack.pop()):
                    frame.ip = arg
            elif op == JUMP_IF_TRUE:
                if is_truthy(stack.pop()):
                    frame.ip = arg
            elif op == CALL:
                self._call(frames, arg)
            elif op == MAKE_FUNCTION:
                func_code = frame.code.constants[arg]
                cells = []
                for kind, index in func_code.upvalue_sources:
                    if kind == "local":
                        cells.append(frame.cells[index])
                    else:
                        cells.append(frame.upvalues[index])
                stack.append(Closure(func_code, tuple(cells)))
            elif op == RETURN:
                result = stack.pop()
                frames.pop()
                if frames:
                    frames[-1].stack.append(result)
                else:
                    return self.output, result
            else:
                raise VMError("unknown opcode %d" % op)
        return self.output, None

    # -- calls ---------------------------------------------------------------

    def _call(self, frames, argc):
        args = frames[-1].stack[len(frames[-1].stack) - argc:] if argc else []
        if argc:
            del frames[-1].stack[len(frames[-1].stack) - argc:]
        callee = frames[-1].stack.pop()
        if isinstance(callee, BuiltinFunction):
            try:
                result = callee.func(*args)
            except VMError:
                raise
            except Exception as exc:
                raise VMError("builtin %s failed: %s" % (callee.name, exc))
            frames[-1].stack.append(result)
        elif isinstance(callee, Closure):
            code = callee.code
            if len(args) != code.arity:
                raise VMError(
                    "%s() takes %d argument(s), got %d"
                    % (code.name, code.arity, len(args)))
            cells = [Cell(None) for _ in code.varnames]
            for i, value in enumerate(args):
                cells[i].value = value
            frames.append(Frame(code, cells, callee.cells))
        else:
            raise VMError("value of type %s is not callable"
                          % type_name(callee))

    # -- operators -------------------------------------------------------------

    def _binary(self, op, left, right):
        if op == ADD:
            if isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, str):
                return left + right
            if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, list):
                return left + right
            if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
                return left + right
            raise VMError("cannot add %s and %s"
                          % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op == MUL:
            if isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, int):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, int) and isinstance(right, str):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, int):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, int) and isinstance(right, list):
                return left * right
            if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
                return left * right
            raise VMError("cannot multiply %s and %s"
                          % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op in (SUB, DIV, MOD):
            if not (self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right)):
                raise VMError("cannot apply arithmetic to %s and %s"
                              % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
            if op == SUB:
                return left - right
            if op == DIV:
                if right == 0:
                    raise VMError("division by zero")
                return left / right
            if right == 0:
                raise VMError("modulo by zero")
            return left % right
        raise VMError("bad binary opcode %d" % op)

    @staticmethod
    def _is_number(value):
        return isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool)

    def _compare(self, op, left, right):
        if op == EQ:
            return self._values_equal(left, right)
        if op == NEQ:
            return not self._values_equal(left, right)
        if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
            pass
        elif isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, str):
            pass
        else:
            raise VMError("cannot compare %s and %s"
                          % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op == LT:
            return left < right
        if op == LE:
            return left <= right
        if op == GT:
            return left > right
        return left >= right

    @staticmethod
    def _values_equal(left, right):
        if isinstance(left, bool) or isinstance(right, bool):
            return left is right
        if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, list):
            return len(left) == len(right) and all(
                VM._values_equal(a, b) for a, b in zip(left, right))
        if isinstance(left, (Closure, BuiltinFunction)) or \
                isinstance(right, (Closure, BuiltinFunction)):
            return left is right
        try:
            return left == right
        except Exception:
            return False

    @staticmethod
    def _index(obj, index):
        if isinstance(obj, (list, str)):
            if not isinstance(index, int) or isinstance(index, bool):
                raise VMError("index must be an int, got %s"
                              % type_name(index))
            try:
                return obj[index]
            except IndexError:
                raise VMError("index %d out of range" % index)
        raise VMError("value of type %s is not indexable" % type_name(obj))

    @staticmethod
    def _store_index(obj, index, value):
        if isinstance(obj, list):
            if not isinstance(index, int) or isinstance(index, bool):
                raise VMError("index must be an int, got %s"
                              % type_name(index))
            try:
                obj[index] = value
            except IndexError:
                raise VMError("index %d out of range" % index)
            return
        raise VMError("value of type %s does not support item assignment"
                      % type_name(obj))


def run_source_vm(source):
    """Compile *source* and run it on the VM.

    Returns the captured print output as one string; a runtime error is
    reported as a final ``Error: ...`` line instead of raising.
    """
    vm = VM()
    try:
        code = compile_source(source)
        output, _result = vm.run(code)
        return "\n".join(output)
    except (LexError, ParseError, CompileError, VMError) as exc:
        lines = list(vm.output)
        lines.append("Error: %s" % exc)
        return "\n".join(lines)


"""Tree-walking interpreter for Minnow.

The second execution path for the same language: instead of compiling
the AST to bytecode (see compiler_bytecode.py / compiler_vm.py), the
interpreter walks the tree directly, evaluating each node in an
environment.  Both engines share the lexer and parser, run the same
test programs, and must agree on every result -- the REPL's test suite
checks that agreement explicitly.

Environments follow the same scoping rule as the compiler: ``let``
declares in the enclosing *function* (or globally); blocks do not create
a scope.  A function value captures the environment it was defined in,
so closures work naturally: the captured environment object is shared,
and assignments through it are visible on both sides.

Control flow uses exceptions internally: ``return`` raises
``ReturnSignal`` carrying the value, which the nearest function call
(or the top level) catches.  A step budget keeps an accidental infinite
loop from hanging the interpreter.
"""

import sys

MAX_STEPS = 5_000_000


class TreewalkError(Exception):
    """A runtime error raised while interpreting Minnow source."""


class ReturnSignal(Exception):
    """Carries a function's return value up through the tree walk."""

    def __init__(self, value):
        super().__init__("return")
        self.value = value


class Environment:
    """A scope: a dict of names plus a link to the enclosing scope."""

    def __init__(self, parent=None):
        self.vars = {}
        self.parent = parent
        # True on the global scope and on each call frame: a ``let`` binds
        # here, never in a bare block (see the scoping rule in the parser).
        self.is_function_boundary = False

    def define(self, name, value):
        self.vars[name] = value

    def lookup(self, name):
        env = self
        while env is not None:
            if name in env.vars:
                return env.vars[name]
            env = env.parent
        raise TreewalkError("undefined variable %r" % name)

    def assign(self, name, value):
        env = self
        while env is not None:
            if name in env.vars:
                env.vars[name] = value
                return
            env = env.parent
        # Assigning to an unknown name creates a global, matching the VM.
        root = self
        while root.parent is not None:
            root = root.parent
        root.vars[name] = value

    def function_scope(self):
        """The nearest function (or global) environment for a ``let``."""
        env = self
        while env.parent is not None and not env.is_function_boundary:
            env = env.parent
        return env


class MinnowFunction:
    """A Minnow function value: parameters, body and defining env."""

    def __init__(self, name, params, body, env):
        self.name = name
        self.params = params
        self.body = body
        self.env = env

    def __repr__(self):
        return "<fn %s>" % self.name


class BuiltinFunction:
    """A Minnow builtin: a Python callable exposed to Minnow programs."""

    def __init__(self, name, func):
        self.name = name
        self.func = func

    def __repr__(self):
        return "<builtin %s>" % self.name


def is_truthy(value):
    """Minnow truthiness: nil/false/0/""/[] are falsy, the rest truthy."""
    if value is None or value is False:
        return False
    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        return value != 0
    if isinstance(value, (str, list)):
        return len(value) != 0
    return True


def stringify(value):
    """How a Minnow value looks when printed or converted with str()."""
    if value is None:
        return "nil"
    if value is True:
        return "true"
    if value is False:
        return "false"
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value
    if isinstance(value, list):
        return "[" + ", ".join(stringify(item) for item in value) + "]"
    if isinstance(value, MinnowFunction):
        return "<fn %s>" % value.name
    if isinstance(value, BuiltinFunction):
        return "<builtin %s>" % value.name
    return repr(value)


def type_name(value):
    if value is None:
        return "nil"
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return "bool"
    if isinstance(value, int):
        return "int"
    if isinstance(value, float):
        return "float"
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return "string"
    if isinstance(value, list):
        return "list"
    if isinstance(value, (MinnowFunction, BuiltinFunction)):
        return "function"
    return "unknown"


class Interpreter:
    """Walks the AST and evaluates it.  One instance = one global scope."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.globals = Environment()
        self.globals.is_function_boundary = True
        self.output = []
        self.steps = 0
        self._install_builtins()

    # -- builtins (same set and semantics as the VM) -----------------------

    def _install_builtins(self):
        def _print(*args):
            self.output.append(" ".join(stringify(a) for a in args))

        def _len(value):
            if isinstance(value, (str, list)):
                return len(value)
            raise TreewalkError("len() needs a string or a list, got %s"
                                % type_name(value))

        def _push(lst, value):
            if not isinstance(lst, list):
                raise TreewalkError("push() needs a list, got %s"
                                    % type_name(lst))
            lst.append(value)
            return lst

        def _pop(lst):
            if not isinstance(lst, list):
                raise TreewalkError("pop() needs a list, got %s"
                                    % type_name(lst))
            if not lst:
                raise TreewalkError("pop() from an empty list")
            return lst.pop()

        def _str(value):
            return stringify(value)

        def _int(value):
            if isinstance(value, bool):
                return 1 if value else 0
            if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                return int(value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                try:
                    return int(value.strip())
                except ValueError:
                    raise TreewalkError("int() cannot parse %r" % value)
            raise TreewalkError("int() cannot convert %s" % type_name(value))

        def _float(value):
            if isinstance(value, bool):
                return 1.0 if value else 0.0
            if isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                return float(value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                try:
                    return float(value.strip())
                except ValueError:
                    raise TreewalkError("float() cannot parse %r" % value)
            raise TreewalkError("float() cannot convert %s"
                                % type_name(value))

        def _type(value):
            return type_name(value)

        def _range(*args):
            if len(args) == 1:
                return list(range(args[0]))
            if len(args) == 2:
                return list(range(args[0], args[1]))
            if len(args) == 3:
                return list(range(args[0], args[1], args[2]))
            raise TreewalkError("range() takes 1 to 3 arguments")

        def _chr(value):
            if not isinstance(value, int):
                raise TreewalkError("chr() needs an int")
            return chr(value)

        def _ord(value):
            if not isinstance(value, str) or len(value) != 1:
                raise TreewalkError("ord() needs a one-character string")
            return ord(value)

        def _assert(condition, message="assertion failed"):
            if not is_truthy(condition):
                raise TreewalkError(str(message))

        for name, func in [
            ("print", _print), ("len", _len), ("push", _push),
            ("pop", _pop), ("str", _str), ("int", _int),
            ("float", _float), ("type", _type), ("range", _range),
            ("chr", _chr), ("ord", _ord), ("assert", _assert),
        ]:
            self.globals.define(name, BuiltinFunction(name, func))

    # -- entry point ---------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, program):
        """Execute a parsed Program node.

        Returns ``(output_lines, result)``; result is the value of a
        top-level ``return`` or ``None``.
        """
        if sys.getrecursionlimit() < 20000:
            sys.setrecursionlimit(20000)
        self.output = []
        self.steps = 0
        try:
            self._exec_block(program.statements, self.globals)
        except ReturnSignal as ret:
            return self.output, ret.value
        return self.output, None

    def _tick(self):
        self.steps += 1
        if self.steps > MAX_STEPS:
            raise TreewalkError("step limit exceeded (infinite loop?)")

    # -- statements ------------------------------------------------------------

    def _exec_block(self, statements, env):
        for stmt in statements:
            self._exec_stmt(stmt, env)

    def _exec_stmt(self, node, env):
        self._tick()
        method = getattr(self, "_stmt_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise TreewalkError("cannot execute %r" % node)
        method(node, env)

    def _stmt_Let(self, node, env):
        target = env.function_scope()
        if isinstance(node.value, FnExpr):
            # Pre-bind the name so the function can call itself.
            func = self._eval_expr(node.value, env)
            target.define(node.name, func)
        else:
            target.define(node.name, self._eval_expr(node.value, env))

    def _stmt_Assign(self, node, env):
        if isinstance(node.target, Var):
            env.assign(node.target.name, self._eval_expr(node.value, env))
        else:
            obj = self._eval_expr(node.target.obj, env)
            index = self._eval_expr(node.target.index, env)
            value = self._eval_expr(node.value, env)
            self._store_index(obj, index, value)

    def _stmt_FnDecl(self, node, env):
        func = MinnowFunction(node.name, node.params, node.body, env)
        env.function_scope().define(node.name, func)

    def _stmt_If(self, node, env):
        if is_truthy(self._eval_expr(node.condition, env)):
            self._exec_stmt(node.then_branch, env)
        elif node.else_branch is not None:
            self._exec_stmt(node.else_branch, env)

    def _stmt_While(self, node, env):
        while is_truthy(self._eval_expr(node.condition, env)):
            self._exec_stmt(node.body, env)

    def _stmt_Return(self, node, env):
        value = None
        if node.value is not None:
            value = self._eval_expr(node.value, env)
        raise ReturnSignal(value)

    def _stmt_Block(self, node, env):
        self._exec_block(node.statements, env)

    def _stmt_ExprStmt(self, node, env):
        self._eval_expr(node.expr, env)

    # -- expressions -------------------------------------------------------------

    def _eval_expr(self, node, env):
        self._tick()
        method = getattr(self, "_expr_" + type(node).__name__, None)
        if method is None:
            raise TreewalkError("cannot evaluate %r" % node)
        return method(node, env)

    def _expr_Literal(self, node, env):
        return node.value

    def _expr_Var(self, node, env):
        return env.lookup(node.name)

    def _expr_ListLit(self, node, env):
        return [self._eval_expr(item, env) for item in node.elements]

    def _expr_Index(self, node, env):
        obj = self._eval_expr(node.obj, env)
        index = self._eval_expr(node.index, env)
        return self._index(obj, index)

    def _expr_Unary(self, node, env):
        value = self._eval_expr(node.operand, env)
        if node.op == "-":
            if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                raise TreewalkError("cannot negate %s" % type_name(value))
            return -value
        if node.op == "not":
            return not is_truthy(value)
        raise TreewalkError("unknown unary operator %r" % node.op)

    def _expr_Binary(self, node, env):
        if node.op == "and":
            left = self._eval_expr(node.left, env)
            if not is_truthy(left):
                return left
            return self._eval_expr(node.right, env)
        if node.op == "or":
            left = self._eval_expr(node.left, env)
            if is_truthy(left):
                return left
            return self._eval_expr(node.right, env)
        left = self._eval_expr(node.left, env)
        right = self._eval_expr(node.right, env)
        return self._binary(node.op, left, right)

    def _expr_Call(self, node, env):
        func = self._eval_expr(node.func, env)
        args = [self._eval_expr(arg, env) for arg in node.args]
        return self._call(func, args)

    def _expr_FnExpr(self, node, env):
        return MinnowFunction("<fn>", node.params, node.body, env)

    # -- calls -------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _call(self, func, args):
        if isinstance(func, BuiltinFunction):
            try:
                return func.func(*args)
            except TreewalkError:
                raise
            except Exception as exc:
                raise TreewalkError("builtin %s failed: %s"
                                    % (func.name, exc))
        if isinstance(func, MinnowFunction):
            if len(args) != len(func.params):
                raise TreewalkError(
                    "%s() takes %d argument(s), got %d"
                    % (func.name, len(func.params), len(args)))
            call_env = Environment(func.env)
            call_env.is_function_boundary = True
            for name, value in zip(func.params, args):
                call_env.define(name, value)
            try:
                self._exec_block(func.body.statements, call_env)
            except ReturnSignal as ret:
                return ret.value
            return None
        raise TreewalkError("value of type %s is not callable"
                            % type_name(func))

    # -- operators -----------------------------------------------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _is_number(value):
        return isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool)

    def _binary(self, op, left, right):
        if op == "+":
            if isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, str):
                return left + right
            if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, list):
                return left + right
            if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
                return left + right
            raise TreewalkError("cannot add %s and %s"
                                % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op == "*":
            if isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, int):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, int) and isinstance(right, str):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, int):
                return left * right
            if isinstance(left, int) and isinstance(right, list):
                return left * right
            if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
                return left * right
            raise TreewalkError("cannot multiply %s and %s"
                                % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op in ("-", "/", "%"):
            if not (self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right)):
                raise TreewalkError("cannot apply arithmetic to %s and %s"
                                    % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
            if op == "-":
                return left - right
            if op == "/":
                if right == 0:
                    raise TreewalkError("division by zero")
                return left / right
            if right == 0:
                raise TreewalkError("modulo by zero")
            return left % right
        if op in ("==", "!=", "<", "<=", ">", ">="):
            return self._compare(op, left, right)
        raise TreewalkError("unknown operator %r" % op)

    def _compare(self, op, left, right):
        if op == "==":
            return self._values_equal(left, right)
        if op == "!=":
            return not self._values_equal(left, right)
        if self._is_number(left) and self._is_number(right):
            pass
        elif isinstance(left, str) and isinstance(right, str):
            pass
        else:
            raise TreewalkError("cannot compare %s and %s"
                                % (type_name(left), type_name(right)))
        if op == "<":
            return left < right
        if op == "<=":
            return left <= right
        if op == ">":
            return left > right
        return left >= right

    @staticmethod
    def _values_equal(left, right):
        if isinstance(left, bool) or isinstance(right, bool):
            return left is right
        if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, list):
            return len(left) == len(right) and all(
                Interpreter._values_equal(a, b)
                for a, b in zip(left, right))
        if isinstance(left, (MinnowFunction, BuiltinFunction)) or \
                isinstance(right, (MinnowFunction, BuiltinFunction)):
            return left is right
        try:
            return left == right
        except Exception:
            return False

    @staticmethod
    def _index(obj, index):
        if isinstance(obj, (list, str)):
            if not isinstance(index, int) or isinstance(index, bool):
                raise TreewalkError("index must be an int, got %s"
                                    % type_name(index))
            try:
                return obj[index]
            except IndexError:
                raise TreewalkError("index %d out of range" % index)
        raise TreewalkError("value of type %s is not indexable"
                            % type_name(obj))

    @staticmethod
    def _store_index(obj, index, value):
        if isinstance(obj, list):
            if not isinstance(index, int) or isinstance(index, bool):
                raise TreewalkError("index must be an int, got %s"
                                    % type_name(index))
            try:
                obj[index] = value
            except IndexError:
                raise TreewalkError("index %d out of range" % index)
            return
        raise TreewalkError(
            "value of type %s does not support item assignment"
            % type_name(obj))


def run_source_tree(source):
    """Parse *source* and run it with the tree-walking interpreter.

    Returns the captured print output as one string; a runtime error is
    reported as a final ``Error: ...`` line instead of raising.
    """
    interp = Interpreter()
    try:
        program = parse(source)
        output, _result = interp.run(program)
        return "\n".join(output)
    except (LexError, ParseError, TreewalkError) as exc:
        lines = list(interp.output)
        lines.append("Error: %s" % exc)
        return "\n".join(lines)


"""Shared test programs for the Minnow engines.

Every entry is ``(name, source, expected)`` where *expected* is the exact
captured print output (lines joined by ``\\n``), or ``None`` when the
program must fail with an error (the runner's output must then start
with ``"Error:"``).

The same programs run on the bytecode VM and on the tree-walking
interpreter, and both engines must produce identical output.  They cover
recursion, sorting, closures, higher-order functions, string building,
nested loops, short-circuit logic and error handling.
"""

     """

       if (n <= 1) { return 1; }
       return n * fact(n - 1);

     print(fib(0));
     print(fib(1));
     """,
     "610\n0\n1"),

    ("fibonacci_iterative",

         b = t;

     """,
     "6765\n12586269025"),

     fn bubble(xs) {
       let n = len(xs);
       let i = 0;
       while (i < n) {
         let j = 0;
         while (j < n - i - 1) {
           if (xs[j] > xs[j + 1]) {
             let t = xs[j];
             xs[j] = xs[j + 1];
             xs[j + 1] = t;
           }
           j = j + 1;
         }
         i = i + 1;
       }
       return xs;
     }
     print(bubble([5, 2, 9, 1, 7, 3]));
     print(bubble([1]));
     print(bubble([]));
     """,
     "[1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 9]\n[1]\n[]"),

    ("quicksort",
     """
     fn qsort(xs) {
       if (len(xs) <= 1) { return xs; }
       let pivot = xs[0];
       let lo = [];
       let hi = [];
       let i = 1;
       while (i < len(xs)) {
         if (xs[i] < pivot) { push(lo, xs[i]); } else { push(hi, xs[i]); }
         i = i + 1;
       }
       return qsort(lo) + [pivot] + qsort(hi);
     }
     print(qsort([5, 2, 9, 1, 7, 3, 8, 4, 6]));
     print(qsort([3, 3, 1, 2, 3]));
     """,
     "[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9]\n[1, 2, 3, 3, 3]"),

    ("binary_search",
     """
     fn bsearch(xs, target) {
       let lo = 0;
       let hi = len(xs) - 1;
       while (lo <= hi) {
         let mid = int((lo + hi) / 2);
         if (xs[mid] == target) { return mid; }
         if (xs[mid] < target) { lo = mid + 1; } else { hi = mid - 1; }
       }
       return -1;
     }
     let xs = [1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13];
     print(bsearch(xs, 7));
     print(bsearch(xs, 4));
     print(bsearch(xs, 13));
     """,
     "3\n-1\n6"),

    ("gcd",

     fn gcd(a, b) {
       while (b != 0) {
         let t = b;
         b = a % b;
         a = t;
       }
       return a;
     }
     print(gcd(1071, 462));
     print(gcd(48, 18));
     print(gcd(17, 5));
     """,

    ("primes_sieve",
     """
     fn primes_upto(n) {
       let sieve = [];
       let i = 0;
       while (i <= n) { push(sieve, true); i = i + 1; }
       sieve[0] = false;
       sieve[1] = false;
       let p = 2;
       while (p * p <= n) {
         if (sieve[p]) {
           let m = p * p;
           while (m <= n) { sieve[m] = false; m = m + p; }
         }
         p = p + 1;
       }
       let out = [];
       i = 2;
       while (i <= n) {
         if (sieve[i]) { push(out, i); }
         i = i + 1;
       }
       return out;
     }
     print(primes_upto(30));
     """,

       }
       hanoi(n - 1, from, via, to);

         steps = steps + 1;

     """,

    ("higher_order_map",

     fn map(f, xs) {
       let out = [];

       while (i < len(xs)) {
         push(out, f(xs[i]));
         i = i + 1;
       }
       return out;
     }
     fn double(x) { return x * 2; }
     fn square(x) { return x * x; }
     print(map(double, [1, 2, 3, 4]));
     print(map(square, [1, 2, 3, 4]));

       return is_odd(n - 1);
     }

       return is_even(n - 1);
     }
     print(is_even(10));

def run_all_tests(runner, label):
    """Run every program in TEST_PROGRAMS through *runner*.

    *runner* is a function ``source -> output string``.  Returns
    ``(passed, failed)`` and prints one PASS/FAIL line per program, with
    the expected and actual output shown for failures.
    """
    passed = 0
    failed = 0
    print("== %s: %d test programs ==" % (label, len(TEST_PROGRAMS)))
    for name, source, expected in TEST_PROGRAMS:
        actual = runner(source)
        if expected is None:
            ok = actual.startswith("Error:")
        else:
            ok = actual == expected
        if ok:
            passed += 1
            print("PASS  %s" % name)
        else:
            failed += 1
            print("FAIL  %s" % name)
            print("  expected: %r" % expected)
            print("  actual:   %r" % actual)
    print("-- %s: %d passed, %d failed --" % (label, passed, failed))
    return passed, failed


"""Interactive REPL for Minnow.

The REPL keeps one VM and one tree-walking interpreter alive across
lines, so definitions persist between inputs:

    minnow> let x = 6;
    minnow> fn square(n) { return n * n; }
    minnow> print(square(x));
    36

Chunks spanning several lines are supported: input is accumulated until
braces, brackets and parentheses balance.  Commands (lines starting
with ``:``):

    :help          show this help
    :quit          leave the REPL
    :mode vm|tree  switch the execution engine (default vm); definitions
                   are preserved by replaying the session history into
                   the new engine
    :dis           toggle printing bytecode disassembly (vm mode)
    :ast           toggle printing the parsed AST
    :test          run the full test suite on both engines and compare

When a chunk is a bare expression its value is shown as ``=> value``.
When run non-interactively (stdin is not a terminal, or a list of lines
is supplied) the same code drives a scripted session, which is how the
demo at the bottom of this file exercises the REPL.
"""

import sys


def _balance_depth(text):
    """Net open-bracket depth of *text*, ignoring brackets in strings."""
    depth = 0
    quote = None
    escaped = False
    for ch in text:
        if quote is not None:
            if escaped:
                escaped = False
            elif ch == "\\":
                escaped = True
            elif ch == quote:
                quote = None
            continue
        if ch == '"' or ch == "'":
            quote = ch
        elif ch in "([{":
            depth += 1
        elif ch in ")]}":
            depth -= 1
    return depth


class Repl:
    """One interactive session, with persistent state in both engines."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.vm = VM()
        self.interp = Interpreter()
        self.mode = "vm"
        self.show_dis = False
        self.show_ast = False
        self.history = []  # accepted chunks, replayed on a mode switch

    # -- one chunk ---------------------------------------------------------

    def _run_chunk(self, chunk):
        """Parse and run one chunk in the current mode.

        Returns ``(display_lines, ok)``.  This does not touch the
        history; :meth:`execute` records successful chunks, and the
        mode switch replays them through this same method.
        """
        lines = []
        try:
            program = parse(chunk)
        except (LexError, ParseError) as exc:
            return ["Error: %s" % exc], False
        # A chunk that is a single bare expression is run as a top-level
        # ``return`` so its value can be shown as ``=> value``.
        if len(program.statements) == 1 and isinstance(
                program.statements[0], ExprStmt):
            program = Program([Return(program.statements[0].expr)])
        if self.show_ast:
            lines.append(dump(program))
        try:
            if self.mode == "vm":
                code = Compiler().compile_program(program)
                if self.show_dis:
                    lines.append(disassemble(code))
                output, result = self.vm.run(code)
            else:
                output, result = self.interp.run(program)
        except (CompileError, VMError, TreewalkError) as exc:
            lines.extend(self._drain_output())
            lines.append("Error: %s" % exc)
            return lines, False
        lines.extend(output)
        if result is not None:
            lines.append("=> %s" % stringify(result))
        return lines, True

    def execute(self, chunk):
        """Run one complete chunk; returns the text to display."""
        lines, ok = self._run_chunk(chunk)
        if ok:
            self.history.append(chunk)
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def _rebuild(self, mode):
        """Switch engines, replaying the accepted history into the new
        one so definitions survive a mode switch.  Returns how many
        replayed chunks failed (normally zero)."""
        self.mode = mode
        if mode == "vm":
            self.vm = VM()
        else:
            self.interp = Interpreter()
        saved_dis, saved_ast = self.show_dis, self.show_ast
        self.show_dis = self.show_ast = False
        failed = 0
        for chunk in self.history:
            _lines, ok = self._run_chunk(chunk)
            if not ok:
                failed += 1
        self.show_dis, self.show_ast = saved_dis, saved_ast
        return failed

    def _drain_output(self):
        if self.mode == "vm":
            output, self.vm.output = self.vm.output, []
            return output
        output, self.interp.output = self.interp.output, []
        return output

    # -- commands ------------------------------------------------------------

    def handle_command(self, line):
        """Handle a ``:command`` line.  Returns (text, keep_going)."""
        parts = line[1:].split()
        command = parts[0] if parts else ""
        if command == "quit":
            return "bye.", False
        if command == "help":
            return (
                "Minnow REPL -- type Minnow code, one chunk at a time.\n"
                "  :help          show this help\n"
                "  :quit          leave the REPL\n"
                "  :mode vm|tree  switch execution engine (now: %s)\n"
                "  :dis           toggle bytecode disassembly\n"
                "  :ast           toggle AST printing\n"
                "  :test          run the test suite on both engines"
                % self.mode), True
        if command == "mode":
            if len(parts) == 2 and parts[1] in ("vm", "tree"):
                if parts[1] == self.mode:
                    return "mode is already %s." % self.mode, True
                failed = self._rebuild(parts[1])
                message = "mode is now %s." % self.mode
                if self.history:
                    message += " Replayed %d earlier chunk(s)%s." % (
                        len(self.history),
                        " (%d failed)" % failed if failed else "")
                return message, True
            return "usage: :mode vm|tree", True
        if command == "dis":
            self.show_dis = not self.show_dis
            return "disassembly %s." % ("on" if self.show_dis else "off"), True
        if command == "ast":
            self.show_ast = not self.show_ast
            return "AST printing %s." % ("on" if self.show_ast else "off"), True
        if command == "test":
            return self._run_test_command(), True
        return "unknown command %r -- try :help" % line, True

    def _run_test_command(self):
        """Run the shared suite on both engines and compare the outputs."""
        lines = []
        vm_pass, vm_fail = run_all_tests(run_source_vm, "VM")
        tree_pass, tree_fail = run_all_tests(run_source_tree, "tree-walk")
        mismatches = 0
        for name, source, _expected in TEST_PROGRAMS:
            vm_out = run_source_vm(source)
            tree_out = run_source_tree(source)
            if vm_out != tree_out:
                mismatches += 1
                lines.append("MISMATCH %s:\n  vm:   %r\n  tree: %r"
                             % (name, vm_out, tree_out))
        lines.append("agreement: %d/%d programs identical "
                     "(%d mismatches)" % (
                         len(TEST_PROGRAMS) - mismatches,
                         len(TEST_PROGRAMS), mismatches))
        lines.append("totals: VM %d/%d passed, tree-walk %d/%d passed" % (
            vm_pass, vm_pass + vm_fail, tree_pass, tree_pass + tree_fail))
        return "\n".join(lines)

    # -- main loop -------------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, input_lines=None):
        """Drive the REPL.

        With *input_lines* given (a list of strings), the session is
        scripted: lines are echoed with their prompts and the session
        ends at the end of the list or at ``:quit``.  Otherwise lines
        are read from stdin interactively.
        """
        scripted = input_lines is not None
        lines_iter = iter(input_lines) if scripted else None
        print("Minnow REPL -- :help for commands, :quit to leave.")
        buffer = ""
        while True:
            prompt = "... " if buffer else "minnow> "
            if scripted:
                try:
                    line = next(lines_iter)
                except StopIteration:
                    break
                print(prompt + line)
            else:
                try:
                    line = input(prompt)
                except EOFError:
                    print()
                    break
                except KeyboardInterrupt:
                    print()
                    buffer = ""
                    continue
            stripped = line.strip()
            if not buffer and stripped.startswith(":"):
                text, keep_going = self.handle_command(stripped)
                print(text)
                if not keep_going:
                    break
                continue
            buffer += line + "\n"
            if _balance_depth(buffer) > 0:
                continue
            if buffer.strip():
                print(self.execute(buffer))
            buffer = ""


def run_scripted_session(lines):
    """Run a scripted REPL session (used by the demo and by tests)."""
    Repl().run(list(lines))



# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test: run the shared suite on both engines, check they
# agree program-for-program, then drive a scripted REPL session.
# Run:  python3 compiler_repl.py
# (Run it with a terminal for a live session: the REPL reads stdin
# interactively when no scripted lines are supplied.)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------

import sys

_SCRIPTED_SESSION = [
    "let x = 6;",
    "fn square(n) { return n * n; }",
    "print(square(x));",
    "square(9)",
    ":mode tree",
    "print(square(7));",
    "square(3) + square(4)",
    ":mode vm",
    ":dis",
    "fn fact(n) { if (n <= 1) { return 1; } return n * fact(n - 1); }",
    "print(fact(6));",
    ":dis",
    ":ast",
    "let xs = [3, 1, 2];",
    ":ast",
    "print(xs);",
    ":test",
    ":quit",
]


def main():
    # 1. Both engines must pass the whole suite...
    vm_pass, vm_fail = run_all_tests(run_source_vm, "VM")
    tree_pass, tree_fail = run_all_tests(run_source_tree, "tree-walk")

    # 2. ...and agree with each other on every program, byte for byte
    # (including the wording of error messages).
    mismatches = 0
    for name, source, _expected in TEST_PROGRAMS:
        vm_out = run_source_vm(source)
        tree_out = run_source_tree(source)
        if vm_out != tree_out:
            mismatches += 1
            print("MISMATCH %s:\n  vm:   %r\n  tree: %r"
                  % (name, vm_out, tree_out))
    print("engine agreement: %d/%d programs identical"
          % (len(TEST_PROGRAMS) - mismatches, len(TEST_PROGRAMS)))
    if vm_fail or tree_fail or mismatches:
        print("REPL demo FAILED")
        sys.exit(1)

    # 3. Scripted REPL session exercising definitions, both engines,
    # expression results, :dis, :ast and the :test command.
    print()
    print("== scripted REPL session ==")
    run_scripted_session(_SCRIPTED_SESSION)
    print("REPL demo OK")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

--- SOURCE: python_samples/reversing_pyc.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
reversing_pyc.py -- A .pyc reader and recursive code-object walker.

A .pyc file is the serialised form of a compiled Python module: a small
header followed by a marshal-encoded code object.  This module parses the
header by hand, unmarshals the code object, and then walks it recursively
to produce a readable structural report: constants, names, variable names,
nested code objects, flags, and a fingerprint of the whole tree.

Everything here uses only the standard library.  The file is
self-contained: run it directly and its demo compiles a sample module to
.pyc, reads it back, and asserts concrete facts about what it finds.

Usage:
    python3 reversing_pyc.py                 # run the built-in demo
    python3 reversing_pyc.py file.pyc        # report on a .pyc file
    python3 reversing_pyc.py file.py         # compile to .pyc first, then report
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import importlib.util
import marshal
import struct
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path
from types import CodeType
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# .pyc header layout
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CPython .pyc files (3.7+) start with:
#   offset  size  field
#   0       4     magic number (identifies the bytecode version)
#   4       4     bit field (flags; bit 0 set => hash-based pyc)
#   8       4     source mtime (timestamp-based pyc) OR 0
#   12      4     source size  (timestamp-based pyc) OR source hash
#   16      ...   marshal-encoded code object
# For hash-based pycs (PEP 552) bytes 8..16 hold the first 8 bytes of the
# source hash instead of mtime/size.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

MAGIC_NUMBER = importlib.util.MAGIC_NUMBER
HEADER_SIZE = 16
FLAG_HASH_BASED = 0x01


class PycError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a file is not a readable .pyc file."""


class PycHeader:
    """Parsed .pyc header."""

    def __init__(self, magic: bytes, flags: int, mtime: int, size: int,
                 source_hash: bytes = b"") -> None:
        self.magic = magic
        self.flags = flags
        self.mtime = mtime
        self.size = size
        self.source_hash = source_hash

    @property
    def hash_based(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.flags & FLAG_HASH_BASED)

    @property
    def magic_matches(self) -> bool:
        return self.magic == MAGIC_NUMBER

    def describe(self) -> List[str]:
        lines = [
            "magic:          %s (%s)" % (
                self.magic.hex(),
                "matches this interpreter" if self.magic_matches
                else "DIFFERS from this interpreter -- unmarshalling may fail"),
            "flags:          0x%08x (%s)" % (
                self.flags,
                "hash-based (PEP 552)" if self.hash_based
                else "timestamp-based"),
        ]
        if self.hash_based:
            lines.append("source hash:    %s" % self.source_hash.hex())
        else:
            lines.append("source mtime:   %d (%s)" % (
                self.mtime,
                time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S", time.localtime(self.mtime))
                if self.mtime else "unset"))
            lines.append("source size:    %d bytes" % self.size)
        return lines


def parse_pyc_header(data: bytes) -> PycHeader:
    """Parse the first 16 bytes of a .pyc file into a PycHeader."""
    if len(data) < HEADER_SIZE:
        raise PycError(
            "file too short for a .pyc header: %d bytes" % len(data))
    magic, flags, field_a, field_b = struct.unpack("<IIII", data[:HEADER_SIZE])
    magic_bytes = struct.pack("<I", magic)
    if flags & FLAG_HASH_BASED:
        source_hash = data[8:16]
        return PycHeader(magic_bytes, flags, 0, 0, source_hash)
    return PycHeader(magic_bytes, flags, field_a, field_b)


def read_pyc(path: str | Path) -> Tuple[PycHeader, CodeType]:
    """Read a .pyc file, returning (header, code object)."""
    path = Path(path)
    data = path.read_bytes()
    header = parse_pyc_header(data)
    if not header.magic_matches:
        raise PycError(
            "magic number %s does not match this interpreter (%s); "
            "the .pyc was compiled by a different CPython version"
            % (header.magic.hex(), MAGIC_NUMBER.hex()))
    try:
        code = marshal.loads(data[HEADER_SIZE:])
    except (ValueError, EOFError, TypeError) as exc:
        raise PycError("marshal failed to decode code object: %s" % exc) from exc
    if not isinstance(code, CodeType):
        raise PycError("marshalled payload is %s, not a code object"
                       % type(code).__name__)
    return header, code


def write_pyc(code: CodeType, path: str | Path, source_mtime: int = 0,
              source_size: int = 0) -> Path:
    """Serialise a code object to a timestamp-based .pyc file."""
    path = Path(path)
    header = struct.pack("<IIII", struct.unpack("<I", MAGIC_NUMBER)[0],
                         0, source_mtime, source_size)
    path.write_bytes(header + marshal.dumps(code))
    return path


def compile_source_to_pyc(source_path: str | Path, pyc_path: str | Path,
                          ) -> Tuple[PycHeader, CodeType]:
    """Compile a .py file and write a .pyc for it, then read it back."""
    source_path = Path(source_path)
    source = source_path.read_bytes()
    code = compile(source, str(source_path), "exec")
    stat = source_path.stat()
    write_pyc(code, pyc_path, int(stat.st_mtime), len(source))
    return read_pyc(pyc_path)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Code-object walking
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# co_flags bits (from CPython's Include/code.h, stable across versions).
FLAG_OPTIMIZED = 0x0001
FLAG_NEWLOCALS = 0x0002
FLAG_VARARGS = 0x0004
FLAG_VARKEYWORDS = 0x0008
FLAG_NESTED = 0x0010
FLAG_GENERATOR = 0x0020
FLAG_NOFREE = 0x0040
FLAG_COROUTINE = 0x0080
FLAG_ITERABLE_COROUTINE = 0x0100
FLAG_ASYNC_GENERATOR = 0x0200

# __future__ flags (from Lib/__future__.py; these live in co_flags too).
FLAG_FUTURE_DIVISION = 0x20000
FLAG_FUTURE_ABSOLUTE_IMPORT = 0x40000
FLAG_FUTURE_WITH_STATEMENT = 0x80000
FLAG_FUTURE_PRINT_FUNCTION = 0x100000
FLAG_FUTURE_UNICODE_LITERALS = 0x200000
FLAG_FUTURE_BARRY_AS_BDFL = 0x400000
FLAG_FUTURE_GENERATOR_STOP = 0x800000
FLAG_FUTURE_ANNOTATIONS = 0x1000000

FLAG_NAMES = [
    (FLAG_OPTIMIZED, "OPTIMIZED"),
    (FLAG_NEWLOCALS, "NEWLOCALS"),
    (FLAG_VARARGS, "VARARGS"),
    (FLAG_VARKEYWORDS, "VARKEYWORDS"),
    (FLAG_NESTED, "NESTED"),
    (FLAG_GENERATOR, "GENERATOR"),
    (FLAG_NOFREE, "NOFREE"),
    (FLAG_COROUTINE, "COROUTINE"),
    (FLAG_ITERABLE_COROUTINE, "ITERABLE_COROUTINE"),
    (FLAG_ASYNC_GENERATOR, "ASYNC_GENERATOR"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_DIVISION, "FUTURE_DIVISION"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_ABSOLUTE_IMPORT, "FUTURE_ABSOLUTE_IMPORT"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_WITH_STATEMENT, "FUTURE_WITH_STATEMENT"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_PRINT_FUNCTION, "FUTURE_PRINT_FUNCTION"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_UNICODE_LITERALS, "FUTURE_UNICODE_LITERALS"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_BARRY_AS_BDFL, "FUTURE_BARRY_AS_BDFL"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_GENERATOR_STOP, "FUTURE_GENERATOR_STOP"),
    (FLAG_FUTURE_ANNOTATIONS, "FUTURE_ANNOTATIONS"),
]


def decode_flags(flags: int) -> List[str]:
    """Turn a co_flags bitmask into a list of human-readable flag names."""
    names = [name for bit, name in FLAG_NAMES if flags & bit]
    unknown = flags & ~sum(bit for bit, _ in FLAG_NAMES)
    if unknown:
        names.append("UNKNOWN(0x%x)" % unknown)
    return names


def summarise_constant(value: Any, depth: int = 0) -> str:
    """Render one constant in a short, readable form."""
    if value is None:
        return "None"
    if isinstance(value, CodeType):
        return "<code object %s>" % value.co_qualname
    if isinstance(value, (bytes, bytearray)):
        preview = bytes(value[:24])
        suffix = "..." if len(value) > 24 else ""
        return "bytes[%d] %r%s" % (len(value), preview, suffix)
    if isinstance(value, str):
        if len(value) > 60:
            return "str[%d] %r..." % (len(value), value[:57])
        return "str %r" % value
    if isinstance(value, (tuple, frozenset)):
        if depth >= 2:
            return "%s[%d items]" % (type(value).__name__, len(value))
        inner = ", ".join(summarise_constant(v, depth + 1) for v in value)
        return "%s(%s)" % (type(value).__name__, inner)
    if isinstance(value, complex):
        return "complex %r" % value
    return "%s %r" % (type(value).__name__, value)


class CodeNode:
    """One code object plus everything discovered by walking it."""

    def __init__(self, code: CodeType, path: str) -> None:
        self.code = code
        self.path = path                      # e.g. "module.<locals>.inner"
        self.children: List["CodeNode"] = []
        self.constants: List[Any] = list(code.co_consts)
        self.names: List[str] = list(code.co_names)
        self.varnames: List[str] = list(code.co_varnames)
        self.freevars: List[str] = list(code.co_freevars)
        self.cellvars: List[str] = list(code.co_cellvars)

    # -- derived facts -----------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def qualified_name(self) -> str:
        return self.code.co_qualname

    @property
    def first_line(self) -> int:
        return self.code.co_firstlineno

    @property
    def argcount(self) -> int:
        return self.code.co_argcount

    @property
    def is_generator(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.code.co_flags & FLAG_GENERATOR)

    @property
    def is_coroutine(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.code.co_flags
                    & (FLAG_COROUTINE | FLAG_ASYNC_GENERATOR))

    @property
    def instruction_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.code.co_code) // 2

    def fingerprint(self) -> str:
        """SHA-256 over the bytecode and constants of this node only."""
        h = hashlib.sha256()
        h.update(self.code.co_code)
        h.update(repr(self.code.co_consts).encode("utf-8", "replace"))
        h.update(repr(self.code.co_names).encode("utf-8", "replace"))
        return h.hexdigest()[:16]

    def all_nodes(self) -> List["CodeNode"]:
        """This node plus every descendant, depth-first."""
        out = [self]
        for child in self.children:
            out.extend(child.all_nodes())
        return out

    def find_by_name(self, qualname: str) -> Optional["CodeNode"]:
        for node in self.all_nodes():
            if node.qualified_name == qualname:
                return node
        return None

    def constants_of_type(self, kind: type) -> List[Any]:
        return [c for c in self.constants if isinstance(c, kind)]

    def string_constants(self) -> List[str]:
        """All string constants in this node and its descendants."""
        out: List[str] = []
        for node in self.all_nodes():
            out.extend(c for c in node.constants if isinstance(c, str))
        return out

    def total_instructions(self) -> int:
        return sum(n.instruction_count for n in self.all_nodes())


def walk_code(code: CodeType, path: str = "<module>") -> CodeNode:
    """Recursively walk a code object into a CodeNode tree."""
    node = CodeNode(code, path)
    for const in code.co_consts:
        if isinstance(const, CodeType):
            child_path = "%s.%s" % (path, const.co_qualname)
            node.children.append(walk_code(const, child_path))
    return node


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Reporting
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def report_node(node: CodeNode, indent: int = 0) -> List[str]:
    """Render one node (and its children) as readable report lines."""
    pad = "  " * indent
    code = node.code
    lines = [
        "%s%s  (line %d, %d instructions, %d args, stack %d)" % (
            pad, node.qualified_name, node.first_line,
            node.instruction_count, node.argcount, code.co_stacksize),
        "%s  flags: %s" % (pad, ", ".join(decode_flags(code.co_flags)) or "none"),
        "%s  fingerprint: %s" % (pad, node.fingerprint()),
    ]
    if node.varnames:
        lines.append("%s  varnames: %s" % (pad, ", ".join(node.varnames)))
    if node.freevars:
        lines.append("%s  freevars: %s" % (pad, ", ".join(node.freevars)))
    if node.cellvars:
        lines.append("%s  cellvars: %s" % (pad, ", ".join(node.cellvars)))
    if node.names:
        lines.append("%s  names: %s" % (pad, ", ".join(node.names)))
    if node.constants:
        lines.append("%s  constants:" % pad)
        for const in node.constants:
            lines.append("%s    - %s" % (pad, summarise_constant(const)))
    for child in node.children:
        lines.append("%s  nested:" % pad)
        lines.extend(report_node(child, indent + 2))
    return lines


def report_pyc(path: str | Path) -> str:
    """Full text report for a .pyc file: header plus code-object tree."""
    header, code = read_pyc(path)
    root = walk_code(code)
    nodes = root.all_nodes()
    lines = ["== %s ==" % Path(path).name, ""]
    lines.append("-- header --")
    lines.extend(header.describe())
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("-- summary --")
    lines.append("code objects:      %d" % len(nodes))
    lines.append("total instructions:%d" % root.total_instructions())
    lines.append("string constants:  %d" % len(root.string_constants()))
    lines.append("all names used:    %s" % ", ".join(
        sorted({n for node in nodes for n in node.names})))
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("-- code objects --")
