    lines.extend(report_node(root))
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


def compare_pyc(path_a: str | Path, path_b: str | Path) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Compare two .pyc files structurally and report the differences."""
    _, code_a = read_pyc(path_a)
    _, code_b = read_pyc(path_b)
    root_a, root_b = walk_code(code_a), walk_code(code_b)
    names_a = {n.qualified_name for n in root_a.all_nodes()}
    names_b = {n.qualified_name for n in root_b.all_nodes()}
    consts_a = {summarise_constant(c) for n in root_a.all_nodes()
                for c in n.constants}
    consts_b = {summarise_constant(c) for n in root_b.all_nodes()
                for c in n.constants}
    return {
        "only_in_a": sorted(names_a - names_b),
        "only_in_b": sorted(names_b - names_a),
        "common": sorted(names_a & names_b),
        "constants_only_in_a": sorted(consts_a - consts_b),
        "constants_only_in_b": sorted(consts_b - consts_a),
        "instructions_a": root_a.total_instructions(),
        "instructions_b": root_b.total_instructions(),
    }


def find_pyc_for_source(source_path: str | Path) -> Path:
    """The __pycache__ .pyc path CPython would use for a source file
    (importlib.util.cache_from_source).  The file may not exist yet."""
    return Path(importlib.util.cache_from_source(str(source_path)))


def extract_docstrings(root: CodeNode) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    """Every docstring in the tree: (qualified name, docstring).
    A docstring is the first constant of a code object, when it is a
    string."""
    out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
    for node in root.all_nodes():
        if (node.constants and isinstance(node.constants[0], str)
                and node.constants[0]):
            out.append((node.qualified_name, node.constants[0]))
    return out


def string_table(root: CodeNode) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    """Every string constant in the tree, with the code object that
    owns it: (qualified name, string)."""
    out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
    for node in root.all_nodes():
        for const in node.constants:
            if isinstance(const, str):
                out.append((node.qualified_name, const))
    return out


def size_report(root: CodeNode) -> List[Tuple[str, int, int]]:
    """Per-code-object size report, largest first: (qualified name,
    bytecode bytes, instruction count)."""
    rows = [(node.qualified_name, len(node.code.co_code),
             node.instruction_count) for node in root.all_nodes()]
    rows.sort(key=lambda row: -row[1])
    return rows


def function_index(root: CodeNode) -> Dict[str, CodeNode]:
    """Map qualified name -> node, for quick lookup by name."""
    return {node.qualified_name: node for node in root.all_nodes()}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

SAMPLE_SOURCE = '''\
"""Sample module used to exercise the .pyc reader."""

GREETING = "hello, reverser"
ANSWER = 42
RATIOS = (1.5, 2.5, 3.5)


def add(a, b):
    """Return the sum of a and b."""
    return a + b


def make_counter(start=0):
    count = start

    def bump(step=1):
        nonlocal count
        count += step
        return count

    return bump


def gen_squares(n):
    for i in range(n):
        yield i * i


class Widget:
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name

    def describe(self):
        return "Widget(%s)" % self.name
'''


def _run_demo() -> int:
    """Compile the sample, read it back, and assert concrete findings."""
    import tempfile

    failures: List[str] = []

    def check(condition: bool, message: str) -> None:
        print(("  ok  " if condition else " FAIL ") + message)
        if not condition:
            failures.append(message)

    print("== reversing_pyc demo ==")
    print("interpreter magic: %s" % MAGIC_NUMBER.hex())

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        tmpdir = Path(tmp)
        src = tmpdir / "sample_mod.py"
        src.write_text(SAMPLE_SOURCE)
        pyc = tmpdir / "sample_mod.pyc"

        header, code = compile_source_to_pyc(src, pyc)
        print("-- header --")
        for line in header.describe():
            print("  " + line)
        check(header.magic_matches, "magic matches this interpreter")
        check(not header.hash_based, "timestamp-based pyc")
        check(header.size == len(SAMPLE_SOURCE.encode()),
              "header records source size %d" % len(SAMPLE_SOURCE.encode()))

        root = walk_code(code)
        nodes = root.all_nodes()
        print("-- tree: %d code objects, %d instructions --"
              % (len(nodes), root.total_instructions()))
        check(len(nodes) == 8,
              "found 8 code objects (module, add, make_counter, bump, "
              "gen_squares, Widget body, __init__, describe) "
              "-> got %d: %s" % (len(nodes),
                                 [n.qualified_name for n in nodes])
              if len(nodes) != 8 else
              "found 8 code objects: %s"
              % [n.qualified_name for n in nodes])

        add = root.find_by_name("add")
        check(add is not None, "found function add")
        if add is not None:
            check(add.argcount == 2, "add takes 2 args")
            check("a" in add.varnames and "b" in add.varnames,
                  "add varnames are a, b")
            check(add.first_line == 8, "add starts at line 8")

        bump = root.find_by_name("make_counter.<locals>.bump")
        check(bump is not None, "found nested closure bump")
        if bump is not None:
            check("count" in bump.freevars, "bump closes over count")

        gen = root.find_by_name("gen_squares")
        check(gen is not None and gen.is_generator,
              "gen_squares detected as a generator")

        strings = root.string_constants()
        check("hello, reverser" in strings,
              "string constant 'hello, reverser' recovered")
        check(42 in root.constants, "module constant 42 recovered")
        check((1.5, 2.5, 3.5) in root.constants,
              "tuple constant (1.5, 2.5, 3.5) recovered")

        # A second .pyc with one changed constant: compare must spot it.
        src2 = tmpdir / "sample_mod2.py"
        src2.write_text(SAMPLE_SOURCE.replace("ANSWER = 42", "ANSWER = 43"))
        pyc2 = tmpdir / "sample_mod2.pyc"
        compile_source_to_pyc(src2, pyc2)
        diff = compare_pyc(pyc, pyc2)
        check("int 42" in diff["constants_only_in_a"],
              "compare flags 42 as only in first pyc")
        check("int 43" in diff["constants_only_in_b"],
              "compare flags 43 as only in second pyc")
        check(diff["instructions_a"] == diff["instructions_b"],
              "instruction counts identical between the two versions")

        # Re-reading via read_pyc must give the same tree.
        _, code_again = read_pyc(pyc)
        check(walk_code(code_again).total_instructions()
              == root.total_instructions(),
              "re-read produces identical instruction count")

        # Docstrings, string table, and size report.
        docs = extract_docstrings(root)
        check(any(name == "add" and "sum" in text for name, text in docs),
              "docstring of add recovered: 'Return the sum of a and b.'")
        check(any(name == "<module>" for name, _ in docs),
              "module docstring recovered")
        table = string_table(root)
        check(any(owner == "<module>" and s == "hello, reverser"
                  for owner, s in table),
              "string table attributes 'hello, reverser' to <module>")
        check(len(table) >= 6, "string table has >= 6 entries (got %d)"
              % len(table))
        sizes = size_report(root)
        check(sizes[0][1] >= sizes[-1][1],
              "size report is sorted largest first")
        check(all(row[1] == 2 * row[2] for row in sizes),
              "every code object's byte size is 2x its instruction count")
        index = function_index(root)
        check(index["add"] is add, "function_index maps 'add' to its node")
        check("Widget.describe" in index,
              "function_index contains Widget.describe")

        # The __pycache__ path for a source file, and a real round trip
        # through it: write the .pyc there, read it back.
        cached = find_pyc_for_source(src)
        check(str(cached).endswith(".pyc")
              and "__pycache__" in str(cached),
              "cache path is a __pycache__ .pyc: %s" % cached.name)
        cached.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        write_pyc(code, cached, int(src.stat().st_mtime),
                  len(SAMPLE_SOURCE.encode()))
        _, cached_code = read_pyc(cached)
        check(walk_code(cached_code).total_instructions()
              == root.total_instructions(),
              "round trip through __pycache__ path reproduces the tree")

        # A truncated file must raise PycError, not crash.
        bad = tmpdir / "bad.pyc"
        bad.write_bytes(b"\x00\x01\x02")
        try:
            read_pyc(bad)
            check(False, "truncated pyc raises PycError")
        except PycError:
            check(True, "truncated pyc raises PycError")

        # Wrong magic must raise PycError.
        wrong = tmpdir / "wrong.pyc"
        wrong.write_bytes(b"\xde\xad\xbe\xef" + b"\x00" * 12
                          + marshal.dumps(code))
        try:
            read_pyc(wrong)
            check(False, "wrong magic raises PycError")
        except PycError:
            check(True, "wrong magic raises PycError")

        print()
        print(report_pyc(pyc)[:2000])

    if failures:
        print("DEMO FAILED: %d check(s)" % len(failures))
        return 1
    print("DEMO PASSED: all checks green")
    return 0


def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) == 1:
        return _run_demo()
    path = Path(argv[1])
    if path.suffix == ".py":
        pyc = path.with_suffix(".pyc")
        compile_source_to_pyc(path, pyc)
        print("compiled %s -> %s" % (path, pyc))
        path = pyc
    print(report_pyc(path))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

--- SOURCE: python_samples/reversing_disasm.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
reversing_disasm.py -- A disassembly reporter built on the dis module.

Given a function, method, class, module, or code object, this module
produces a readable per-instruction listing (offset, opcode, argument,
argument value, source line), plus the analysis a reverse engineer
actually wants on top of the raw listing:

  * opcode histograms and per-function bytecode statistics,
  * a source-line interleaved listing (source line, then its instructions),
  * a stack-depth simulation that tracks the deepest evaluation stack,
  * jump-target resolution (which instructions are jump targets, and
    which instructions jump where),
  * a search API: find every instruction matching an opcode, a name, or
    a constant, with its source line,
  * a side-by-side diff of two functions' instruction streams.

Only the standard library is used.  The file is self-contained; its
__main__ demo disassembles real functions from this file and from the
START repo (mini_ai modules) and asserts concrete facts about them.

Usage:
    python3 reversing_disasm.py                     # run the demo
    python3 reversing_disasm.py path/to/file.py     # disassemble a module
    python3 reversing_disasm.py path/to/file.py -f name
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import dis
import inspect
import sys
import types
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, Iterable, List, Optional, Tuple

# Sentinel for "no constant filter" in find_instructions.
_MISSING: Any = object()

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Core: one instruction, fully described
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class Instr:
    """One bytecode instruction, enriched with source-line context."""

    __slots__ = ("offset", "opname", "opcode", "arg", "argval", "argrepr",
                 "starts_line", "is_jump_target", "positions")

    def __init__(self, raw: dis.Instruction) -> None:
        self.offset = raw.offset
        self.opname = raw.opname
        self.opcode = raw.opcode
        self.arg = raw.arg
        self.argval = raw.argval
        self.argrepr = raw.argrepr
        self.starts_line = raw.starts_line
        self.is_jump_target = raw.is_jump_target
        self.positions = raw.positions

    def __repr__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover - debugging aid
        return "Instr(%d %s %s)" % (self.offset, self.opname, self.argrepr)

    def short(self) -> str:
        """One-line rendering, in the style of dis.dis output."""
        target = ">> " if self.is_jump_target else "   "
        line = "%4d " % self.starts_line if self.starts_line is not None else "     "
        return "%s%s %4d %-22s %s" % (
            target, line, self.offset, self.opname,
            self.argrepr if self.arg is not None else "")


def get_instructions(obj: Any) -> List[Instr]:
    """Return the enriched instruction list for a function/code object."""
    if isinstance(obj, types.CodeType):
        code = obj
    elif isinstance(obj, (types.FunctionType, types.MethodType)):
        code = obj.__code__
    elif isinstance(obj, type):
        # A class: disassemble its methods one at a time via walk below.
        raise TypeError("pass a function or code object, not a class; "
                        "use disassemble_module for classes")
    else:
        raise TypeError("cannot disassemble %r" % type(obj).__name__)
    return [Instr(raw) for raw in dis.get_instructions(code)]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Statistics and analysis over an instruction list
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def opcode_histogram(instructions: Iterable[Instr]) -> Counter:
    return Counter(ins.opname for ins in instructions)


def jump_map(instructions: List[Instr]) -> Dict[int, List[int]]:
    """Map each jump-target offset to the offsets of instructions that
    jump to it."""
    targets: Dict[int, List[int]] = {}
    for ins in instructions:
        if "JUMP" in ins.opname or ins.opname in (
                "POP_JUMP_IF_TRUE", "POP_JUMP_IF_FALSE",
                "JUMP_IF_TRUE_OR_POP", "JUMP_IF_FALSE_OR_POP",
                "FOR_ITER", "SETUP_FINALLY"):
            if isinstance(ins.argval, int):
                targets.setdefault(ins.argval, []).append(ins.offset)
    return targets


def jump_targets(instructions: List[Instr]) -> List[int]:
    return sorted({ins.offset for ins in instructions if ins.is_jump_target})


def max_stack_depth(instructions: List[Instr]) -> int:
    """Simulate the evaluation stack linearly and report the deepest
    point reached.  This is an approximation: it follows the instruction
    order, not the control-flow graph, so it is an upper bound only when
    a loop body pushes more than it pops.  For straight-line code it is
    exact; see reversing_flow.py for the CFG-based version."""
    depth = 0
    deepest = 0
    for ins in instructions:
        try:
            effect = dis.stack_effect(ins.opcode, ins.arg)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            effect = 0
        depth += effect
        deepest = max(deepest, depth)
        # A jump target may reset the stack in real execution; the linear
        # simulation keeps going, which is why this is an upper bound.
        if depth < 0:
            depth = 0
    return deepest


def line_spans(instructions: List[Instr]) -> Dict[int, List[Instr]]:
    """Group instructions by the source line that starts them."""
    spans: Dict[int, List[Instr]] = {}
    current: Optional[int] = None
    for ins in instructions:
        if ins.starts_line is not None:
            current = ins.starts_line
        if current is not None:
            spans.setdefault(current, []).append(ins)
    return spans


def _call_sites(instructions: List[Instr]
                ) -> List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]]:
    """Origin-tracking stack simulation: for every CALL instruction,
    return (index, dotted callee name, source line).

    The stack holds the *origin* of each value: LOAD_GLOBAL os pushes
    "os", LOAD_ATTR system turns the top origin into "os.system".
    A CALL with n arguments finds its callable at stack position
    -(n+1), because CPython 3.12 lays the call out as
    [NULL, callable, arg1, ..., argn] (the NULL comes from LOAD_GLOBAL
    with its null-bit set, from PUSH_NULL, or from LOAD_ATTR with its
    null-bit set).  The simulation is linear: at any jump target the
    stack is reset, so calls that span a branch are missed rather than
    misattributed.
    """
    stack: List[str] = []
    out: List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]] = []
    current_line: Optional[int] = None
    for i, ins in enumerate(instructions):
        if getattr(ins, "starts_line", None) is not None:
            # CALL itself often carries no line; remember the line of
            # the statement it belongs to.
            current_line = ins.starts_line
        if ins.is_jump_target and i != 0:
            stack = []
        op = ins.opname
        arg = ins.arg if ins.arg is not None else 0
        if op == "PUSH_NULL":
            stack.append("<null>")
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_GLOBAL":
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_FAST", "LOAD_DEREF", "LOAD_NAME", "LOAD_CLOSURE"):
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_CONST":
            stack.append("<const>")
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_ATTR", "LOAD_METHOD"):
            base = stack.pop() if stack else "<obj>"
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_SUPER_ATTR":
            base = "<super>"
            if stack:
                stack.pop()
            if stack:
                base = stack.pop()
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "COPY":
            if stack and 1 <= arg <= len(stack):
                stack.append(stack[-arg])
            else:
                stack.append("<expr>")
            continue
        if op == "SWAP":
            if len(stack) >= arg and arg >= 2:
                stack[-1], stack[-arg] = stack[-arg], stack[-1]
            continue
        if op.startswith("CALL"):
            if op == "CALL_FUNCTION_EX":
                popped = 4 if (arg & 1) else 3
                callee = stack[-3] if len(stack) >= 3 else None
                if callee is not None:
                    out.append((i, callee, current_line))
                stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
                stack.append("<call>")
                continue
            argc = arg
            callee = None
            if len(stack) >= argc + 1:
                callee = stack[-argc - 1]
            if callee is not None and callee not in ("<null>", "<const>",
                                                     "<expr>", "<call>"):
                out.append((i, callee, current_line))
            popped = argc + 2
            stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
            stack.append("<call>")
            continue
        try:
            effect = dis.stack_effect(ins.opcode, ins.arg)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            stack = []
            continue
        if effect < 0:
            pops = -effect
            stack = stack[:-pops] if pops <= len(stack) else []
        elif effect > 0:
            stack.extend("<expr>" for _ in range(effect))
    return out


def find_instructions(instructions: List[Instr], *,
                      opname: Optional[str] = None,
                      name: Optional[str] = None,
                      constant: Any = _MISSING) -> List[Instr]:
    """Find instructions matching an opcode name, a LOAD name, or a
    constant value.  Pass constant=_MISSING to skip the constant test;
    any other value (including None) is matched exactly."""
    out = []
    for ins in instructions:
        if opname is not None and ins.opname != opname:
            continue
        if name is not None and ins.argval != name:
            continue
        if constant is not _MISSING and ins.argval != constant:
            continue
        out.append(ins)
    return out


class FunctionReport:
    """Everything the reporter knows about one function."""

    def __init__(self, obj: Any, label: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
        if isinstance(obj, types.CodeType):
            self.code = obj
            self.name = obj.co_qualname
        else:
            self.code = obj.__code__
            self.name = label or getattr(obj, "__qualname__", str(obj))
        self.instructions = get_instructions(self.code)
        self.histogram = opcode_histogram(self.instructions)
        self.jumps = jump_map(self.instructions)
        self.targets = jump_targets(self.instructions)
        self.spans = line_spans(self.instructions)
        self.max_depth = max_stack_depth(self.instructions)

    @property
    def instruction_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.instructions)

    @property
    def byte_size(self) -> int:
        return len(self.code.co_code)

    @property
    def distinct_opcodes(self) -> int:
        return len(self.histogram)

    @property
    def first_line(self) -> int:
        return self.code.co_firstlineno

    @property
    def source_lines_covered(self) -> int:
        return len(self.spans)

    def most_common_opcodes(self, n: int = 5) -> List[Tuple[str, int]]:
        return self.histogram.most_common(n)

    def loads_of(self, name: str) -> List[Instr]:
        return find_instructions(self.instructions, name=name)

    def calls(self) -> List[Instr]:
        """Every CALL / CALL_FUNCTION_EX instruction."""
        return [ins for ins in self.instructions
                if ins.opname.startswith("CALL")]

    def called_names(self) -> List[str]:
        """Names of the callables this function calls, via an
        origin-tracking stack simulation (see _call_sites): the stack
        holds the origin of each value, so a CALL finds its callable
        at stack position -(argc+1), below its arguments.  This is
        exact for direct calls like os.system(x) or math.sqrt(16);
        calls through computed values are skipped, not guessed."""
        return [name for _, name, _ in _call_sites(self.instructions)]

    def listing(self) -> str:
        lines = ["%s  (%d instructions, %d bytes, first line %d)"
                 % (self.name, self.instruction_count, self.byte_size,
                    self.first_line)]
        for ins in self.instructions:
            lines.append(ins.short())
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def interleaved_listing(self, source_lines: List[str]) -> str:
        """Source lines with the instructions that implement them."""
        out = ["%s  (source-interleaved)" % self.name]
        for lineno in sorted(self.spans):
            text = ""
            if 1 <= lineno <= len(source_lines):
                text = source_lines[lineno - 1].rstrip()
            out.append("  %4d  %s" % (lineno, text))
            for ins in self.spans[lineno]:
                out.append("        " + ins.short())
        return "\n".join(out)

    def summary(self) -> str:
        top = ", ".join("%s x%d" % (op, n)
                        for op, n in self.most_common_opcodes())
        return (
            "%s: %d instructions, %d distinct opcodes, %d jump targets, "
            "max stack depth %d, %d source lines covered\n"
            "  most common: %s\n"
            "  calls: %s"
            % (self.name, self.instruction_count, self.distinct_opcodes,
               len(self.targets), self.max_depth,
               self.source_lines_covered, top,
               ", ".join(self.called_names()) or "none"))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-level disassembly
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def walk_code_objects(code: types.CodeType):
    """Yield a code object and every nested code object, depth-first."""
    yield code
    for const in code.co_consts:
        if isinstance(const, types.CodeType):
            yield from walk_code_objects(const)


def disassemble_module(path: str | Path) -> Dict[str, FunctionReport]:
    """Disassemble every code object in a .py source file."""
    path = Path(path)
    code = compile(path.read_text(), str(path), "exec")
    reports: Dict[str, FunctionReport] = {}
    for obj in walk_code_objects(code):
        reports[obj.co_qualname] = FunctionReport(obj)
    return reports


def module_summary(path: str | Path) -> str:
    reports = disassemble_module(path)
    total = sum(r.instruction_count for r in reports.values())
    all_hist: Counter = Counter()
    for r in reports.values():
        all_hist.update(r.histogram)
    lines = ["== %s ==" % Path(path).name,
             "code objects: %d, total instructions: %d"
             % (len(reports), total),
             "module opcode histogram (top 10):"]
    for op, n in all_hist.most_common(10):
        lines.append("  %-24s %d" % (op, n))
    lines.append("")
    for name in sorted(reports):
        lines.append("  " + reports[name].summary().replace("\n", "\n  "))
    return "\n".join(lines)


def opcode_bigrams(instructions: List[Instr]) -> Counter:
    """Counts of adjacent opcode pairs: the idioms a function uses,
    e.g. LOAD_FAST -> BINARY_OP, or COMPARE_OP -> POP_JUMP_IF_FALSE."""
    return Counter(
        (a.opname, b.opname)
        for a, b in zip(instructions, instructions[1:]))


def detect_constructs(report: "FunctionReport") -> Set[str]:
    """Name the source-level constructs a function's bytecode shows:
    comprehensions, with-statements, f-strings, exception handling,
    async/await, decorators (via MAKE_FUNCTION with a closure), and
    attribute assignment.  Useful for a quick read of what a function
    does without reading its source."""
    ops = set(report.histogram)
    found: Set[str] = set()
    if ops & {"LIST_APPEND", "SET_ADD", "MAP_ADD"}:
        found.add("comprehension")
    if ops & {"SETUP_WITH", "BEFORE_WITH"}:
        found.add("with-statement")
    if "FORMAT_VALUE" in ops or "FORMAT" in ops:
        found.add("f-string")
    if ops & {"SETUP_FINALLY", "SETUP_CLEANUP", "PUSH_EXC_INFO",
              "CHECK_EXC_MATCH", "RERAISE"}:
        found.add("exception-handling")
    if ops & {"GET_AWAITABLE", "GET_AITER", "GET_ANEXT", "SEND"}:
        found.add("async")
    if "MAKE_FUNCTION" in ops:
        found.add("nested-function-or-decorator")
    if "LOAD_ASSERTION_ERROR" in ops:
        found.add("assert")
    if ops & {"BUILD_LIST", "BUILD_MAP", "BUILD_SET", "BUILD_TUPLE",
              "BUILD_SLICE", "BUILD_STRING"}:
        found.add("literal-construction")
    if ops & {"IMPORT_NAME", "IMPORT_FROM"}:
        found.add("import")
    if "YIELD_VALUE" in ops:
        found.add("generator")
    return found


def line_density(report: "FunctionReport") -> List[Tuple[int, int]]:
    """Source lines ranked by how many instructions implement them:
    the densest lines are usually the most complex expressions."""
    rows = [(lineno, len(insns))
            for lineno, insns in report.spans.items()]
    rows.sort(key=lambda row: -row[1])
    return rows


def diff_functions(func_a: Any, func_b: Any) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Compare two functions' instruction streams opcode by opcode.
    Instructions are compared as (opname, argrepr) pairs, because in
    CPython 3.11+ the operator of BINARY_OP lives in the argument, not
    in a distinct opcode."""
    a = get_instructions(func_a)
    b = get_instructions(func_b)
    ops_a = [(i.opname, i.argrepr) for i in a]
    ops_b = [(i.opname, i.argrepr) for i in b]
    common_prefix = 0
    for x, y in zip(ops_a, ops_b):
        if x != y:
            break
        common_prefix += 1
    first_diff: Optional[Tuple[int, Optional[str], Optional[str]]] = None
    if common_prefix < min(len(ops_a), len(ops_b)):
        first_diff = (common_prefix, ops_a[common_prefix],
                      ops_b[common_prefix])
    return {
        "len_a": len(ops_a),
        "len_b": len(ops_b),
        "common_prefix": common_prefix,
        "first_diff": first_diff,
        "only_in_a": sorted(set(ops_a) - set(ops_b)),
        "only_in_b": sorted(set(ops_b) - set(ops_a)),
        "identical": ops_a == ops_b,
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo targets (real code, analysed by the demo below)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def sample_add(a, b):
    """Add two numbers."""
    return a + b


def sample_loop(n):
    """Sum squares with a loop and a conditional."""
    total = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if i % 2 == 0:
            total += i * i
        else:
            total += 1
    return total


def sample_conditional(x):
    if x < 0:
        return "negative"
    elif x == 0:
        return "zero"
    return "positive"


def sample_try(x):
    try:
        return 10 // x
    except ZeroDivisionError:
        return None
    finally:
        pass


def sample_callers():
    import math
    return math.sqrt(16) + len("abcd")


def sample_comprehension(items):
    """A list comprehension, a dict comprehension, and an f-string."""
    squares = [x * x for x in items if x > 0]
    lookup = {x: x * x for x in items}
    return "%s -> %s" % (squares, lookup) + f" ({len(squares)} items)"


def sample_with(path):
    """A with-statement plus an assert."""
    with open(path) as handle:
        text = handle.read()
    assert text, "file was empty"
    return text


async def sample_async(items):
    """An async function, so GET_AWAITABLE appears in its bytecode."""
    total = 0
    for item in items:
        total += await item
    return total


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _run_demo() -> int:
    failures: List[str] = []

    def check(condition: bool, message: str) -> None:
        print(("  ok  " if condition else " FAIL ") + message)
        if not condition:
            failures.append(message)

    print("== reversing_disasm demo ==")

    # --- basic listing of a tiny function --------------------------------
    rep = FunctionReport(sample_add)
    print(rep.listing())
    check(rep.instruction_count == 5,
          "sample_add has exactly 5 instructions (got %d)" % rep.instruction_count)
    check([i.opname for i in rep.instructions]
          == ["RESUME", "LOAD_FAST", "LOAD_FAST", "BINARY_OP", "RETURN_VALUE"],
          "sample_add opcode stream is RESUME, LOAD_FAST, LOAD_FAST, "
          "BINARY_OP, RETURN_VALUE")
    check(rep.called_names() == [], "sample_add calls nothing")
    check(rep.max_depth == 2, "sample_add max stack depth is 2")

    # --- constants and names are recoverable from the listing ------------
    loads = find_instructions(rep.instructions, opname="LOAD_FAST")
    check([i.argval for i in loads] == ["a", "b"],
          "LOAD_FAST args are a, b")

    # --- loop function: jumps, targets, histogram ------------------------
    loop = FunctionReport(sample_loop)
    print()
    print(loop.summary())
    check(len(loop.targets) >= 2,
          "sample_loop has >= 2 jump targets (got %d: %s)"
          % (len(loop.targets), loop.targets))
    check("FOR_ITER" in loop.histogram,
          "sample_loop uses FOR_ITER")
    check("POP_JUMP_IF_FALSE" in loop.histogram or
          "POP_JUMP_IF_TRUE" in loop.histogram,
          "sample_loop has a conditional jump")
    check(loop.source_lines_covered >= 5,
          "sample_loop covers >= 5 source lines (got %d)"
          % loop.source_lines_covered)
    check(loop.max_depth >= 2, "sample_loop stack depth >= 2")

    # --- conditional: three return paths, jump map is non-empty ----------
    cond = FunctionReport(sample_conditional)
    check(len(cond.jumps) >= 2,
          "sample_conditional has >= 2 jump sites (got %d)"
          % len(cond.jumps))
    rets = [i for i in cond.instructions
            if i.opname in ("RETURN_VALUE", "RETURN_CONST")]
    check(len(rets) == 3,
          "sample_conditional has 3 returns (RETURN_VALUE/RETURN_CONST)")
    check(find_instructions(cond.instructions, constant="negative"),
          "constant 'negative' found in sample_conditional")

    # --- try/except: SETUP_FINALLY / exception handling opcodes ----------
    tr = FunctionReport(sample_try)
    check(any(op in tr.histogram for op in
              ("SETUP_FINALLY", "SETUP_CLEANUP", "PUSH_EXC_INFO",
               "CHECK_EXC_MATCH")),
          "sample_try has exception-handling opcodes: %s"
          % [op for op in tr.histogram if "EXC" in op or "SETUP" in op])

    # --- calls: the callee-name heuristic --------------------------------
    callers = FunctionReport(sample_callers)
    check("math.sqrt" in callers.called_names(),
          "sample_callers calls math.sqrt (found %s)" % callers.called_names())
    check("len" in callers.called_names(),
          "sample_callers calls len")

    # --- diff: identical functions, and a changed one --------------------
    def twin_add(a, b):
        return a + b

    def other_add(a, b):
        return a - b

    d_same = diff_functions(sample_add, twin_add)
    check(d_same["identical"], "identical functions diff as identical")
    d_diff = diff_functions(sample_add, other_add)
    check(not d_diff["identical"],
          "a + b vs a - b diff as different")
    check(d_diff["common_prefix"] == 3,
          "a + b vs a - b share a 3-instruction prefix (RESUME + two LOAD_FASTs)")

    # --- interleaved source listing --------------------------------------
    # Pass the whole file's lines: spans are keyed by absolute line number.
    src_lines = Path(__file__).read_text().splitlines()
    inter = loop.interleaved_listing(src_lines)
    check("for i in range(n):" in inter,
          "interleaved listing contains the for line")
    check("total += i * i" in inter,
          "interleaved listing contains the total += i * i line")

    # --- real repo code: disassemble this file's own module --------------
    here = Path(__file__)
    reports = disassemble_module(here)
    check(len(reports) >= 10,
          "this file disassembles into >= 10 code objects (got %d)"
          % len(reports))
    check("sample_loop" in reports, "sample_loop found in module walk")
    check("FunctionReport.listing" in reports,
          "method FunctionReport.listing found in module walk")
    total = sum(r.instruction_count for r in reports.values())
    check(total > 500,
          "this file totals > 500 instructions (got %d)" % total)

    # --- real repo code: a mini_ai module --------------------------------
    repo = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    planning = repo / "mini_ai" / "planning.py"
    if planning.exists():
        preports = disassemble_module(planning)
        check(len(preports) > 0, "mini_ai/planning.py disassembles")
        names = sorted(preports)
        print("  mini_ai/planning.py code objects: %s" % names)
        check(any("plan" in n.lower() for n in names),
              "planning.py contains a plan-related code object")
        ptotal = sum(r.instruction_count for r in preports.values())
        print("  mini_ai/planning.py total instructions: %d" % ptotal)
        check(ptotal > 50, "planning.py has > 50 instructions")
        # Every instruction offset must be even and strictly increasing.
        for name, r in preports.items():
            offs = [i.offset for i in r.instructions]
            check(offs == sorted(offs) and all(o % 2 == 0 for o in offs),
                  "offsets in %s are increasing and even" % name)
    else:
        print("  (mini_ai/planning.py not found; repo check skipped)")

    # --- construct detection ------------------------------------------------
    comp = FunctionReport(sample_comprehension)
    constructs = detect_constructs(comp)
    check("comprehension" in constructs,
          "comprehension detected in sample_comprehension")
    check("f-string" in constructs,
          "f-string detected in sample_comprehension")
    with_rep = FunctionReport(sample_with)
    check("with-statement" in detect_constructs(with_rep),
          "with-statement detected in sample_with")
    check("assert" in detect_constructs(with_rep),
          "assert detected in sample_with")
    check("import" not in detect_constructs(with_rep),
          "sample_with has no import (open is a builtin, not an import)")
    async_rep = FunctionReport(sample_async)
    check("async" in detect_constructs(async_rep),
          "async detected in sample_async")
    check("generator" not in detect_constructs(FunctionReport(sample_loop)),
          "sample_loop is not a generator")

    # --- bigrams and line density -------------------------------------------
    bigrams = opcode_bigrams(loop.instructions)
    check(("LOAD_FAST", "LOAD_FAST") in bigrams
          or ("LOAD_FAST", "BINARY_OP") in bigrams,
          "sample_loop has LOAD_FAST bigrams")
    check(sum(bigrams.values()) == loop.instruction_count - 1,
          "bigram count is instruction count - 1")
    density = line_density(loop)
    check(density[0][1] >= 2,
          "densest line of sample_loop has >= 2 instructions")
    check(all(n >= 1 for _, n in density),
          "every covered line has >= 1 instruction")

    # --- error paths ------------------------------------------------------
    try:
        get_instructions(42)
        check(False, "get_instructions(42) raises TypeError")
    except TypeError:
        check(True, "get_instructions(42) raises TypeError")
    try:
        get_instructions(dict)
        check(False, "get_instructions(class) raises TypeError")
    except TypeError:
        check(True, "get_instructions(class) raises TypeError")

    if failures:
        print("DEMO FAILED: %d check(s)" % len(failures))
        return 1
    print("DEMO PASSED: all checks green")
    return 0


def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) == 1:
        return _run_demo()
    path = Path(argv[1])
    if len(argv) >= 4 and argv[2] == "-f":
        reports = disassemble_module(path)
        name = argv[3]
        if name not in reports:
            print("no code object named %r; available: %s"
                  % (name, sorted(reports)))
            return 2
        print(reports[name].listing())
        return 0
    print(module_summary(path))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

--- SOURCE: python_samples/reversing_flow.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
reversing_flow.py -- Control-flow analysis for Python bytecode.

Builds, from the dis module's instruction stream, the structures a
reverse engineer draws on paper:

  * basic blocks -- maximal straight-line runs of instructions,
  * the control-flow graph (CFG) -- edges between blocks,
  * dominators -- which blocks must be passed through to reach a block,
  * loops -- natural loops found from back edges in the CFG,
  * unreachable blocks -- code that no edge can reach,
  * cyclomatic complexity -- edges - nodes + 2,
  * a call graph -- which functions call which, across a whole module.

Only the standard library is used.  The file is self-contained; its
__main__ demo analyses real functions (loops, nested branches, try/
except, an early return) and asserts concrete facts: block counts,
loop headers and bodies, dominator sets, complexity numbers, and a
call graph built from this file and from mini_ai modules in the repo.

Usage:
    python3 reversing_flow.py                    # run the demo
    python3 reversing_flow.py path/to/file.py    # CFG report for a module
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import dis
import sys
import types
from collections import defaultdict, deque
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Iterable, List, Optional, Set, Tuple

# Opcodes that end a block by transferring control elsewhere, and
# opcodes that end a block by leaving the function.
UNCONDITIONAL_JUMPS = {"JUMP_FORWARD", "JUMP_BACKWARD", "JUMP"}
CONDITIONAL_JUMPS = {
    "POP_JUMP_IF_FALSE", "POP_JUMP_IF_TRUE",
    "JUMP_IF_TRUE_OR_POP", "JUMP_IF_FALSE_OR_POP",
    "JUMP_IF_NOT_EXC_MATCH",
}
TERMINATORS = {"RETURN_VALUE", "RETURN_CONST", "RAISE_VARARGS",
               "RERAISE", "RETURN_GENERATOR"}
# Instructions that can transfer control to a handler or iterator end.
BRANCHING = UNCONDITIONAL_JUMPS | CONDITIONAL_JUMPS | {
    "FOR_ITER", "SETUP_FINALLY", "SETUP_CLEANUP", "SETUP_WITH",
    "SEND", "JUMP_IF_NOT_EXC_MATCH",
}


def _is_branch(ins: dis.Instruction) -> bool:
    return ins.opname in BRANCHING


def _is_terminator(ins: dis.Instruction) -> bool:
    return ins.opname in TERMINATORS


def _jump_target(ins: dis.Instruction) -> Optional[int]:
    if _is_branch(ins) and isinstance(ins.argval, int):
        return ins.argval
    return None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Basic blocks
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class BasicBlock:
    """A maximal straight-line run of instructions."""

    def __init__(self, block_id: int, start_offset: int) -> None:
        self.id = block_id
        self.start_offset = start_offset
        self.instructions: List[dis.Instruction] = []
        self.successors: Set[int] = set()
        self.predecessors: Set[int] = set()

    @property
    def end_offset(self) -> int:
        return self.instructions[-1].offset if self.instructions else -1

    @property
    def first_line(self) -> Optional[int]:
        for ins in self.instructions:
            if ins.starts_line is not None:
                return ins.starts_line
        return None

    @property
    def last_opname(self) -> Optional[str]:
        return self.instructions[-1].opname if self.instructions else None

    def __repr__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover - debugging aid
        return "Block(%d, off=%d, %d ins, succ=%s)" % (
            self.id, self.start_offset, len(self.instructions),
            sorted(self.successors))


def split_into_blocks(instructions: List[dis.Instruction]) -> List[BasicBlock]:
    """Split an instruction list into basic blocks.

    A new block starts at every jump target and at every instruction
    that follows a branch or a terminator.
    """
    if not instructions:
        return []
    leaders: Set[int] = {instructions[0].offset}
    for i, ins in enumerate(instructions):
        target = _jump_target(ins)
        if target is not None:
            leaders.add(target)
        if (_is_branch(ins) or _is_terminator(ins)) and i + 1 < len(instructions):
            leaders.add(instructions[i + 1].offset)
    blocks: List[BasicBlock] = []
    current: Optional[BasicBlock] = None
    for ins in instructions:
        if ins.offset in leaders:
            current = BasicBlock(len(blocks), ins.offset)
            blocks.append(current)
        assert current is not None
        current.instructions.append(ins)
    return blocks


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Control-flow graph
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class CFG:
    """Control-flow graph of one function."""

    def __init__(self, obj: Any, label: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
        if isinstance(obj, types.CodeType):
            self.code = obj
            self.name = obj.co_qualname
        else:
            self.code = obj.__code__
            self.name = label or getattr(obj, "__qualname__", str(obj))
        instructions = list(dis.get_instructions(self.code))
        self.instructions = instructions
        self.blocks = split_into_blocks(instructions)
        self._offset_to_block: Dict[int, int] = {}
        for block in self.blocks:
            for ins in block.instructions:
                self._offset_to_block[ins.offset] = block.id
        self._link_blocks()

    # -- construction ------------------------------------------------------
    def _link_blocks(self) -> None:
        for i, block in enumerate(self.blocks):
            if not block.instructions:
                continue
            last = block.instructions[-1]
            target = _jump_target(last)
            if last.opname in UNCONDITIONAL_JUMPS:
                if target is not None and target in self._offset_to_block:
                    block.successors.add(self._offset_to_block[target])
            elif _is_branch(last):
                # Conditional: fall through to the next block AND jump.
                if i + 1 < len(self.blocks):
                    block.successors.add(i + 1)
                if target is not None and target in self._offset_to_block:
                    block.successors.add(self._offset_to_block[target])
            elif _is_terminator(last):
                pass  # leaves the function; no successors
            else:
                if i + 1 < len(self.blocks):
                    block.successors.add(i + 1)
            for succ in block.successors:
                self.blocks[succ].predecessors.add(block.id)
        self._link_exception_edges()

    def _block_containing(self, offset: int) -> Optional[int]:
        """Id of the block that contains a bytecode offset (the block
        with the greatest start offset <= the given offset)."""
        best: Optional[int] = None
        for block in self.blocks:
            if block.start_offset <= offset:
                best = block.id
            else:
                break
        return best

    def _link_exception_edges(self) -> None:
        """Add edges from the exception table: if an exception can be
        raised anywhere in [start, end), control can jump to `target`.
        CPython 3.11+ stores this table on the code object; dis exposes
        a private parser for it.  Without a parser we simply have no
        exception edges (handlers then look unreachable)."""
        parse = getattr(dis, "_parse_exception_table", None)
        if parse is None:
            return
        try:
            entries = parse(self.code)
        except Exception:
            return
        for entry in entries:
            target_block = self._block_containing(entry.target)
            if target_block is None:
                continue
            for block in self.blocks:
                covered = any(entry.start <= ins.offset < entry.end
                              for ins in block.instructions)
                if covered and target_block not in block.successors:
                    block.successors.add(target_block)
                    self.blocks[target_block].predecessors.add(block.id)

    # -- queries ------------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def entry(self) -> BasicBlock:
        return self.blocks[0]

    @property
    def block_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.blocks)

    @property
    def edge_count(self) -> int:
        return sum(len(b.successors) for b in self.blocks)

    def cyclomatic_complexity(self) -> int:
        """McCabe complexity for a graph with possibly several exits:
        E - N + exits + 1, where exits are blocks with no successors.
        (For a single exit this reduces to the familiar E - N + 2.)"""
        exits = sum(1 for b in self.blocks if not b.successors)
        return self.edge_count - self.block_count + exits + 1

    def reachable_blocks(self) -> Set[int]:
        seen: Set[int] = set()
        stack = [0]
        while stack:
            node = stack.pop()
            if node in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(node)
            stack.extend(self.blocks[node].successors)
        return seen

    def unreachable_blocks(self) -> List[BasicBlock]:
        reachable = self.reachable_blocks()
        return [b for b in self.blocks if b.id not in reachable]

    def dominators(self) -> Dict[int, Set[int]]:
        """Iterative dominator computation (Cooper et al. style, set form).

        dom(entry) = {entry}; dom(b) = {b} u intersection of dom(p) for
        every predecessor p.  Iterated to a fixed point.
        """
        all_ids = set(range(len(self.blocks)))
        dom: Dict[int, Set[int]] = {i: set(all_ids) for i in all_ids}
        dom[0] = {0}
        changed = True
        while changed:
            changed = False
            for block in self.blocks[1:]:
                preds = [p for p in block.predecessors if p in dom]
                if not preds:
                    new = {block.id}
                else:
                    new = set.intersection(*(dom[p] for p in preds))
                    new.add(block.id)
                if new != dom[block.id]:
                    dom[block.id] = new
                    changed = True
        return dom

    def back_edges(self) -> List[Tuple[int, int]]:
        """Edges (source, target) where the target dominates the source:
        these are the loop-closing edges."""
        dom = self.dominators()
        edges = []
        for block in self.blocks:
            for succ in block.successors:
                if succ in dom[block.id]:
                    edges.append((block.id, succ))
        return edges

    def natural_loop(self, back_edge: Tuple[int, int]) -> Set[int]:
        """The natural loop of a back edge (source, header): the header
        plus every block that can reach the source without passing
        through the header."""
        source, header = back_edge
        loop = {header, source}
        stack = [source]
        while stack:
            node = stack.pop()
            for pred in self.blocks[node].predecessors:
                if pred not in loop:
                    loop.add(pred)
                    stack.append(pred)
        return loop

    def loops(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """All natural loops, one per loop header.  A loop often has
        several back edges (one per branch that jumps back to the
        header, e.g. each arm of an if/elif inside a for body), so
        back edges are grouped by header and the body is the union
        of their natural loops."""
        by_header: Dict[int, List[Tuple[int, int]]] = defaultdict(list)
        for edge in self.back_edges():
            by_header[edge[1]].append(edge)
        out = []
        for header_id, edges in sorted(by_header.items()):
            body: Set[int] = set()
            for edge in edges:
                body |= self.natural_loop(edge)
            header = self.blocks[header_id]
            line = header.first_line
            if line is None:
                # The header instruction (e.g. FOR_ITER) often carries
                # no line of its own; fall back to the body's lines.
                body_lines = [self.blocks[b].first_line for b in body]
                body_lines = [ln for ln in body_lines if ln is not None]
                line = min(body_lines) if body_lines else None
            out.append({
                "header_block": header_id,
                "header_line": line,
                "latch_blocks": sorted(e[0] for e in edges),
                "body_blocks": sorted(body),
                "body_size": len(body),
            })
        return out

    def block_for_line(self, lineno: int) -> Optional[BasicBlock]:
        for block in self.blocks:
            if block.first_line == lineno:
                return block
        return None

    def reachable_from(self, block_id: int) -> Set[int]:
        """All blocks reachable from a given block (including itself)."""
        seen: Set[int] = set()
        stack = [block_id]
        while stack:
            node = stack.pop()
            if node in seen or node >= len(self.blocks):
                continue
            seen.add(node)
            stack.extend(self.blocks[node].successors)
        return seen

    def loop_nesting_depth(self) -> int:
        """Deepest loop nesting: a loop header inside another loop's
        body adds one level.  Computed from header containment: a
        loop's header is nested inside every other loop whose body
        contains it."""
        loops = self.loops()
        if not loops:
            return 0
        bodies = [set(loop["body_blocks"]) for loop in loops]
        headers = [loop["header_block"] for loop in loops]
        depth = [1] * len(loops)
        for i, header in enumerate(headers):
            for j, body in enumerate(bodies):
                if i != j and header in body:
                    depth[i] = max(depth[i], depth[j] + 1)
        # Iterate to a fixed point: nesting can chain.
        changed = True
        while changed:
            changed = False
            for i, header in enumerate(headers):
                for j, body in enumerate(bodies):
                    if i != j and header in body and depth[i] < depth[j] + 1:
                        depth[i] = depth[j] + 1
                        changed = True
        return max(depth)

    def to_dot(self) -> str:
        """Graphviz DOT rendering of the CFG, for piping to `dot`."""
        lines = ["digraph %s {" % self.name.replace(".", "_"),
                 '  label="%s (complexity %d)";' % (
                     self.name, self.cyclomatic_complexity()),
                 "  node [shape=box, fontname=monospace];"]
        for block in self.blocks:
            label = "B%d\\nline %s\\n%s" % (
                block.id, block.first_line,
                "\\n".join(i.opname for i in block.instructions[:6]))
            if len(block.instructions) > 6:
                label += "\\n..."
            lines.append('  B%d [label="%s"];' % (block.id, label))
        for block in self.blocks:
            for succ in sorted(block.successors):
                lines.append("  B%d -> B%d;" % (block.id, succ))
        lines.append("}")
        return "\n".join(lines)

    # -- rendering ----------------------------------------------------------
    def render(self) -> str:
        lines = ["CFG of %s: %d blocks, %d edges, complexity %d"
                 % (self.name, self.block_count, self.edge_count,
                    self.cyclomatic_complexity())]
        loops = self.loops()
        if loops:
            for loop in loops:
                lines.append("  loop: header block %d (line %s), latches %s, body blocks %s"
                             % (loop["header_block"], loop["header_line"],
                                loop["latch_blocks"], loop["body_blocks"]))
        unreachable = self.unreachable_blocks()
        if unreachable:
            lines.append("  unreachable blocks: %s"
                         % [b.id for b in unreachable])
        for block in self.blocks:
            opnames = [i.opname for i in block.instructions]
            lines.append("  B%d [line %s, off %d..%d] -> %s : %s"
                         % (block.id, block.first_line, block.start_offset,
                            block.end_offset, sorted(block.successors),
                            ", ".join(opnames)))
        return "\n".join(lines)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Call graph
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _call_sites(instructions: List[dis.Instruction]
                ) -> List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]]:
    """Origin-tracking stack simulation: for every CALL instruction,
    return (index, dotted callee name, source line).  The stack holds
    the origin of each value (LOAD_GLOBAL os pushes "os", LOAD_ATTR
    system makes it "os.system"); a CALL with n arguments finds its
    callable at stack position -(n+1), below the NULL and the args,
    per CPython 3.12's calling convention.  Linear simulation: the
    stack resets at jump targets, so calls spanning a branch are
    missed rather than misattributed."""
    stack: List[str] = []
    out: List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]] = []
    current_line: Optional[int] = None
    for i, ins in enumerate(instructions):
        if getattr(ins, "starts_line", None) is not None:
            # CALL itself often carries no line; remember the line of
            # the statement it belongs to.
            current_line = ins.starts_line
        if ins.is_jump_target and i != 0:
            stack = []
        op = ins.opname
        arg = ins.arg if ins.arg is not None else 0
        if op == "PUSH_NULL":
            stack.append("<null>")
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_GLOBAL":
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_FAST", "LOAD_DEREF", "LOAD_NAME", "LOAD_CLOSURE"):
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_CONST":
            stack.append("<const>")
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_ATTR", "LOAD_METHOD"):
            base = stack.pop() if stack else "<obj>"
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_SUPER_ATTR":
            base = "<super>"
            if stack:
                stack.pop()
            if stack:
                base = stack.pop()
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "COPY":
            if stack and 1 <= arg <= len(stack):
                stack.append(stack[-arg])
            else:
                stack.append("<expr>")
            continue
        if op == "SWAP":
            if len(stack) >= arg and arg >= 2:
                stack[-1], stack[-arg] = stack[-arg], stack[-1]
            continue
        if op.startswith("CALL"):
            if op == "CALL_FUNCTION_EX":
                popped = 4 if (arg & 1) else 3
                callee = stack[-3] if len(stack) >= 3 else None
                if callee is not None:
                    out.append((i, callee, current_line))
                stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
                stack.append("<call>")
                continue
            argc = arg
            callee = None
            if len(stack) >= argc + 1:
                callee = stack[-argc - 1]
            if callee is not None and callee not in ("<null>", "<const>",
                                                     "<expr>", "<call>"):
                out.append((i, callee, current_line))
            popped = argc + 2
            stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
            stack.append("<call>")
            continue
        try:
            effect = dis.stack_effect(ins.opcode, ins.arg)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            stack = []
            continue
        if effect < 0:
            pops = -effect
            stack = stack[:-pops] if pops <= len(stack) else []
        elif effect > 0:
            stack.extend("<expr>" for _ in range(effect))
    return out


def _called_names(code: types.CodeType) -> List[str]:
    """Best-effort callee names for one code object, via _call_sites."""
    instructions = list(dis.get_instructions(code))
    return [name for _, name, _ in _call_sites(instructions)]


def _walk_code_objects(code: types.CodeType):
    yield code
    for const in code.co_consts:
        if isinstance(const, types.CodeType):
            yield from _walk_code_objects(const)


class CallGraph:
    """Call graph of a whole module: caller qualname -> callee names,
    plus which callees resolve to functions defined in the same module."""

    def __init__(self, path: str | Path) -> None:
        path = Path(path)
        self.path = path
        code = compile(path.read_text(), str(path), "exec")
        self.nodes: Dict[str, types.CodeType] = {}
        for obj in _walk_code_objects(code):
            self.nodes[obj.co_qualname] = obj
        self.edges: Dict[str, List[str]] = {
            name: _called_names(obj) for name, obj in self.nodes.items()
        }

    def internal_edges(self) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
        """Edges where the callee is defined in this same module."""
        defined = set(self.nodes)
        return {caller: [c for c in callees if c in defined]
                for caller, callees in self.edges.items()}

    def external_calls(self) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
        defined = set(self.nodes)
        return {caller: [c for c in callees if c not in defined]
                for caller, callees in self.edges.items()}

    def callers_of(self, name: str) -> List[str]:
        return [caller for caller, callees in self.edges.items()
                if name in callees]

    def roots(self) -> List[str]:
        """Functions nothing in the module calls (entry points)."""
        called = {c for callees in self.edges.values() for c in callees}
        return [n for n in self.nodes if n not in called]

    def render(self) -> str:
        lines = ["call graph of %s (%d functions)"
                 % (self.path.name, len(self.nodes))]
        for caller in sorted(self.edges):
            callees = self.edges[caller]
            internal = [c for c in callees if c in self.nodes]
            lines.append("  %s -> %s" % (
                caller,
                ", ".join("%s*" % c if c in internal else c
                          for c in callees) or "(none)"))
        lines.append("  (* = defined in this module)")
        return "\n".join(lines)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo targets (real code, analysed by the demo below)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def demo_straight(x):
    """No branches at all: a single basic block."""
    y = x * 2 + 1
    return y


def demo_branch(x):
    """One if/else: three blocks, complexity 2."""
    if x > 0:
        return "pos"
    return "non-pos"


def demo_loop(n):
    """A for loop with an inner conditional: two loops? No -- one loop,
    but the inner if adds a branch inside the loop body."""
    total = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if i % 3 == 0:
            total += i
        elif i % 3 == 1:
            total += 2 * i
        else:
            total += 3
    return total


def demo_while(n):
    """A while loop with a break: one loop, one exit edge."""
    total = 0
    i = 0
    while i < n:
        if i == 5:
            break
        total += i
        i += 1
    return total


def demo_nested(n):
    """Two nested loops: an outer loop and an inner loop."""
    total = 0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i):
            total += j
    return total


def demo_early_return(x):
    """Early return inside a loop: the loop still has one back edge."""
    for i in range(10):
        if i == x:
            return i
    return -1


def demo_try(x):
    """try/except adds an exception-handling edge."""
    try:
        return 100 // x
    except ZeroDivisionError:
        return 0


def demo_helper_a():
    return demo_helper_b() + 1


def demo_helper_b():
    return 41


def demo_caller():
    return demo_helper_a() + demo_helper_b()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _run_demo() -> int:
    failures: List[str] = []

    def check(condition: bool, message: str) -> None:
        print(("  ok  " if condition else " FAIL ") + message)
        if not condition:
            failures.append(message)

    print("== reversing_flow demo ==")

    # --- straight-line code: one block, complexity 1 ----------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_straight)
    print(cfg.render())
    check(cfg.block_count == 1, "demo_straight has 1 block")
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() == 1,
          "demo_straight complexity is 1")
    check(cfg.loops() == [], "demo_straight has no loops")
    check(cfg.unreachable_blocks() == [], "demo_straight has no dead blocks")

    # --- one branch: 3 blocks, complexity 2 -------------------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_branch)
    check(cfg.block_count == 3, "demo_branch has 3 blocks (got %d)"
          % cfg.block_count)
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() == 2, "demo_branch complexity is 2")
    check(cfg.loops() == [], "demo_branch has no loops")

    # --- for loop with inner if: one loop, complexity 4 -------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_loop)
    print()
    print(cfg.render())
    loops = cfg.loops()
    check(len(loops) == 1, "demo_loop has exactly 1 loop (got %d)"
          % len(loops))
    if loops:
        check(loops[0]["body_size"] >= 3,
              "demo_loop loop body spans >= 3 blocks (got %d)"
              % loops[0]["body_size"])
        check(loops[0]["header_line"] is not None,
              "demo_loop loop header has a source line")
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() == 4,
          "demo_loop complexity is 4 (got %d)" % cfg.cyclomatic_complexity())

    # --- while + break ------------------------------------------------------
    # Note: the compiler duplicates `return total` on the break path, so
    # the bytecode graph has TWO exit blocks.  Graph-theoretic McCabe
    # counts the extra exit: 1 (base) + 1 (while) + 1 (if) + 1 (extra
    # exit) = 4, one more than a source-level count of 3.
    cfg = CFG(demo_while)
    check(len(cfg.loops()) == 1, "demo_while has exactly 1 loop")
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() == 4,
          "demo_while complexity is 4 (got %d)" % cfg.cyclomatic_complexity())

    # --- nested loops: two loops, inner header dominated by outer ---------
    cfg = CFG(demo_nested)
    loops = cfg.loops()
    check(len(loops) == 2, "demo_nested has exactly 2 loops (got %d)"
          % len(loops))
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() == 3,
          "demo_nested complexity is 3 (got %d)" % cfg.cyclomatic_complexity())
    dom = cfg.dominators()
    if len(loops) == 2:
        headers = sorted(loop["header_block"] for loop in loops)
        outer, inner = headers[0], headers[1]
        check(outer in dom[inner],
              "outer loop header dominates inner loop header")

    # --- early return: still one loop ---------------------------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_early_return)
    check(len(cfg.loops()) == 1, "demo_early_return has 1 loop")

    # --- try/except: at least one extra edge, no crash ----------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_try)
    check(cfg.block_count >= 3,
          "demo_try has >= 3 blocks (got %d)" % cfg.block_count)
    check(cfg.cyclomatic_complexity() >= 2,
          "demo_try complexity >= 2")

    # --- dominators: entry dominates everything ------------------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_loop)
    dom = cfg.dominators()
    check(all(0 in dom[b] for b in dom),
          "entry block dominates every block in demo_loop")
    check(dom[0] == {0}, "entry block's dominator set is just itself")

    # --- reachability: every block reachable in these demos -----------------
    for fn in (demo_straight, demo_branch, demo_loop, demo_while,
               demo_nested, demo_early_return, demo_try):
        c = CFG(fn)
        check(c.unreachable_blocks() == [],
              "%s has no unreachable blocks" % fn.__name__)

    # --- the analyses agree with execution -----------------------------------
    check(demo_loop(6) == 0 + 0 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 10,
          "demo_loop(6) == 19 (sanity: analysed code runs)")
    check(demo_nested(3) == 0 + 0 + 1,
          "demo_nested(3) == 1")
    check(demo_while(10) == 0 + 1 + 2 + 3 + 4,
          "demo_while(10) == 10 (break at i == 5)")

    # --- call graph of this file ----------------------------------------------
    here = Path(__file__)
    graph = CallGraph(here)
    print()
    print(graph.render())
    check("demo_caller" in graph.edges, "call graph has demo_caller")
    check("demo_helper_a" in graph.edges["demo_caller"],
          "demo_caller calls demo_helper_a")
    check("demo_helper_b" in graph.edges["demo_caller"],
          "demo_caller calls demo_helper_b")
    check("demo_helper_b" in graph.edges["demo_helper_a"],
          "demo_helper_a calls demo_helper_b")
    check("demo_helper_b" in graph.callers_of("demo_helper_b")
          or "demo_caller" in graph.callers_of("demo_helper_b"),
          "callers_of(demo_helper_b) is non-empty")
    check(set(graph.callers_of("demo_helper_b"))
          == {"demo_helper_a", "demo_caller"},
          "exactly demo_helper_a and demo_caller call demo_helper_b")
    internal = graph.internal_edges()
    check("demo_helper_a" in internal["demo_caller"],
          "demo_helper_a is an internal edge of demo_caller")

    # --- call graph of a real repo module --------------------------------------
    repo = here.resolve().parents[2]
    planning = repo / "mini_ai" / "planning.py"
    if planning.exists():
        pgraph = CallGraph(planning)
        print()
        print(pgraph.render())
        check(len(pgraph.nodes) >= 3,
              "planning.py call graph has >= 3 nodes (got %d)"
              % len(pgraph.nodes))
        check(all(isinstance(v, list) for v in pgraph.edges.values()),
              "planning.py edges are lists")
        # Every edge target is a string name.
        check(all(isinstance(c, str) for cs in pgraph.edges.values()
                  for c in cs), "planning.py callee names are strings")
    else:
        print("  (mini_ai/planning.py not found; repo check skipped)")

    # --- DOT output and loop nesting -----------------------------------------
    cfg = CFG(demo_nested)
    check(cfg.loop_nesting_depth() == 2,
          "demo_nested loop nesting depth is 2 (got %d)"
          % cfg.loop_nesting_depth())
    cfg = CFG(demo_loop)
    check(cfg.loop_nesting_depth() == 1,
          "demo_loop loop nesting depth is 1")
    cfg = CFG(demo_straight)
    check(cfg.loop_nesting_depth() == 0,
          "demo_straight loop nesting depth is 0")
    dot = CFG(demo_loop).to_dot()
    check(dot.startswith("digraph"), "DOT output starts with digraph")
    check("B0 -> B1" in dot, "DOT output contains the B0 -> B1 edge")
    check("FOR_ITER" in dot, "DOT output names the FOR_ITER instruction")
    check("complexity 4" in dot, "DOT label carries the complexity")
    check(CFG(demo_loop).reachable_from(0)
          == set(range(CFG(demo_loop).block_count)),
          "every block is reachable from the entry block of demo_loop")

    # --- CFG of every function in this file: no crashes, sane numbers ---------
    for name, obj in graph.nodes.items():
        c = CFG(obj)
        # Generator expressions can legitimately compute complexity 0:
        # they are resumed via SEND, an edge this CFG does not model.
        check(c.block_count >= 1 and c.cyclomatic_complexity() >= 0,
              "CFG of %s is sane (%d blocks, complexity %d)"
              % (name, c.block_count, c.cyclomatic_complexity()))

    if failures:
        print("DEMO FAILED: %d check(s)" % len(failures))
        return 1
    print("DEMO PASSED: all checks green")
    return 0


def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) == 1:
        return _run_demo()
    path = Path(argv[1])
    print(CallGraph(path).render())
    print()
    code = compile(path.read_text(), str(path), "exec")
    for obj in _walk_code_objects(code):
        print(CFG(obj).render())
        print()
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

--- SOURCE: python_samples/reversing_scanner.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
reversing_scanner.py -- A small static-analysis scanner for Python code.

Walks the bytecode of a module (or a whole directory of modules) and
flags patterns a reverse engineer cares about:

  * DANGEROUS_EXEC  -- eval(), exec(), compile() of dynamic input
  * CODEGEN         -- __import__, importlib.import_module, getattr tricks
  * NETWORK         -- socket creation, urllib / http.client / requests use
  * PROCESS         -- subprocess, os.system, os.popen, os.exec*
  * FILESYSTEM      -- open(), os.remove/unlink, shutil.rmtree, Path.unlink
  * ENV             -- os.environ / os.getenv / os.putenv reads and writes
  * SERIALISATION   -- pickle / marshal loads (arbitrary code on load)
  * CRYPTO_WEAK     -- hashlib.md5 / sha1, random (not secrets), DES
  * INTROSPECTION   -- globals()/locals()/vars(), inspect, __class__ tricks
  * SUSPICIOUS_STRING -- string constants that look like URLs, IP
    addresses, shell paths, cloud keys, or long encoded tokens

Each finding carries a severity (info / low / medium / high), the
function and source line it was found in, and the evidence (the exact
instruction that triggered it).  A module gets a risk score from its
findings, so modules can be ranked.

Detection is done on bytecode (LOAD_GLOBAL / LOAD_METHOD / LOAD_ATTR
followed by CALL), which works even when the source is unavailable --
only a .pyc is needed.  A parallel source-level check (ast) is included
so the two can be cross-checked.

Only the standard library is used.  The file is self-contained; its
__main__ demo scans a planted test sample (which must trigger every
rule) and real files from this repo, and asserts concrete findings:
the planted eval is found at the right line, mini_ai/data_fetch.py is
flagged for network use, mini_ai/self_edit.py is flagged for
exec-family calls, and so on.

Usage:
    python3 reversing_scanner.py                    # run the demo
    python3 reversing_scanner.py path/to/file.py    # scan one file
    python3 reversing_scanner.py path/to/dir        # scan a directory
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import dis
import sys
import types
from collections import Counter
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Iterable, List, Optional, Set, Tuple

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rule table
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Each rule: (rule id, severity, description, set of callee names that
# trigger it).  A callee name is the dotted name as it appears at the
# call site, e.g. "os.system" or "socket.socket".  Matching is by
# suffix: "socket" matches "socket.socket" and "socket.create_connection".

SEVERITY_WEIGHTS = {"info": 0, "low": 1, "medium": 3, "high": 5}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Rule:
    rule_id: str
    severity: str
    description: str
    names: frozenset


RULES: List[Rule] = [
    Rule("DANGEROUS_EXEC", "high",
         "dynamic code execution (eval / exec / compile of runtime input)",
         frozenset({"eval", "exec", "compile"})),
    Rule("CODEGEN", "medium",
         "dynamic import or attribute access that can load arbitrary code",
         frozenset({"__import__", "import_module", "getattr", "setattr",
                    "globals", "locals", "vars", "eval", "exec"})),
    Rule("NETWORK", "medium",
         "network access (socket, urllib, http, requests)",
         frozenset({"socket", "create_connection", "urlopen", "request",
                    "getaddrinfo", "connect", "send", "recv", "listen",
                    "bind", "accept", "HTTPConnection", "HTTPSConnection",
                    "urlretrieve", "Session"})),
    Rule("PROCESS", "high",
         "process creation or shell execution",
         frozenset({"Popen", "run", "call", "check_output", "check_call",
                    "system", "popen", "execv", "execve", "execl",
                    "execlp", "spawnl", "spawnlp", "fork"})),
    Rule("FILESYSTEM", "low",
         "file open / delete / destructive filesystem operation",
         frozenset({"open", "remove", "unlink", "rmtree", "rename",
                    "chmod", "chown", "mkdir", "makedirs", "write_bytes",
                    "write_text", "read_bytes", "read_text"})),
    Rule("ENV", "low",
         "environment variable read or write",
         frozenset({"environ", "getenv", "getenvb", "putenv", "unsetenv",
                    "setdefault"})),
    Rule("SERIALISATION", "high",
         "deserialisation that can execute arbitrary code (pickle/marshal)",
         frozenset({"loads", "load", "Unpickler", "dumps"})),
    Rule("CRYPTO_WEAK", "medium",
         "weak hash or non-cryptographic randomness",
         frozenset({"md5", "sha1", "random", "randint", "randrange",
                    "choice", "seed"})),
    Rule("INTROSPECTION", "info",
         "runtime introspection (inspect, __class__, __subclasses__)",
         frozenset({"getmembers", "getsource", "getfile", "__subclasses__",
                    "mro", "__class__", "getattr"})),
]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Findings
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class Finding:
    rule_id: str
    severity: str
    function: str
    line: Optional[int]
    evidence: str
    description: str

    def weight(self) -> int:
        return SEVERITY_WEIGHTS[self.severity]

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        line = "line %s" % self.line if self.line is not None else "line ?"
        return "[%s/%s] %s (%s): %s -- %s" % (
            self.severity.upper(), self.rule_id, self.function, line,
            self.evidence, self.description)


@dataclass
class ScanReport:
    path: str
    findings: List[Finding] = field(default_factory=list)

    @property
    def score(self) -> int:
        return sum(f.weight() for f in self.findings)

    @property
    def risk(self) -> str:
        if self.score >= 15:
            return "HIGH"
        if self.score >= 6:
            return "MEDIUM"
        if self.score >= 1:
            return "LOW"
        return "CLEAN"

    def by_rule(self) -> Dict[str, List[Finding]]:
        out: Dict[str, List[Finding]] = {}
        for f in self.findings:
            out.setdefault(f.rule_id, []).append(f)
        return out

    def by_severity(self) -> Dict[str, List[Finding]]:
        out: Dict[str, List[Finding]] = {}
        for f in self.findings:
            out.setdefault(f.severity, []).append(f)
        return out

    def has_rule(self, rule_id: str) -> bool:
        return any(f.rule_id == rule_id for f in self.findings)

    def findings_in(self, function: str) -> List[Finding]:
        return [f for f in self.findings if f.function == function]

    def summary(self) -> str:
        counts = Counter(f.rule_id for f in self.findings)
        lines = ["%s: score %d (%s), %d finding(s)"
                 % (self.path, self.score, self.risk, len(self.findings))]
        for rule_id, n in sorted(counts.items()):
            lines.append("  %-16s %d" % (rule_id, n))
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def full_report(self) -> str:
        lines = [self.summary(), ""]
        for f in sorted(self.findings,
                        key=lambda f: (-f.weight(), f.function or "")):
            lines.append("  " + str(f))
        return "\n".join(lines)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Bytecode-level scanning
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _walk_code_objects(code: types.CodeType):
    yield code
    for const in code.co_consts:
        if isinstance(const, types.CodeType):
