            yield from _walk_code_objects(const)


def _call_sites(instructions: List[Any]
                ) -> List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]]:
    """Origin-tracking stack simulation: for every CALL instruction,
    return (index, dotted callee name, source line).

    The stack holds the *origin* of each value: LOAD_GLOBAL os pushes
    "os", LOAD_ATTR system turns the top origin into "os.system", and
    so on.  A CALL with n arguments finds its callable at stack
    position -(n+1), because CPython 3.12 lays the call out as
    [NULL, callable, arg1, ..., argn] (the NULL comes from LOAD_GLOBAL
    with its null-bit set, from PUSH_NULL, or from LOAD_ATTR with its
    null-bit set).  The simulation is linear: at any jump target the
    stack is reset, so calls that span a branch are missed rather than
    misattributed.
    """
    stack: List[str] = []
    out: List[Tuple[int, str, Optional[int]]] = []
    current_line: Optional[int] = None
    for i, ins in enumerate(instructions):
        if getattr(ins, "starts_line", None) is not None:
            # CALL itself often carries no line; remember the line of
            # the statement it belongs to.
            current_line = ins.starts_line
        if getattr(ins, "is_jump_target", False) and i != 0:
            stack = []
        op = ins.opname
        arg = ins.arg if ins.arg is not None else 0
        if op == "PUSH_NULL":
            stack.append("<null>")
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_GLOBAL":
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_FAST", "LOAD_DEREF", "LOAD_NAME", "LOAD_CLOSURE"):
            stack.append(str(ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_CONST":
            stack.append("<const>")
            continue
        if op in ("LOAD_ATTR", "LOAD_METHOD"):
            base = stack.pop() if stack else "<obj>"
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "LOAD_SUPER_ATTR":
            base = "<super>"
            if stack:
                stack.pop()
            if stack:
                base = stack.pop()
            if arg & 1:
                stack.append("<null>")
            stack.append("%s.%s" % (base, ins.argval))
            continue
        if op == "COPY":
            if stack and 1 <= arg <= len(stack):
                stack.append(stack[-arg])
            else:
                stack.append("<expr>")
            continue
        if op == "SWAP":
            if len(stack) >= arg and arg >= 2:
                stack[-1], stack[-arg] = stack[-arg], stack[-1]
            continue
        if op.startswith("CALL"):
            if op == "CALL_FUNCTION_EX":
                # Stack: [NULL, callable, args, (kwargs)].
                popped = 4 if (arg & 1) else 3
                callee = stack[-3] if len(stack) >= 3 else None
                if callee is not None:
                    out.append((i, callee, current_line))
                stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
                stack.append("<call>")
                continue
            argc = arg
            callee = None
            if len(stack) >= argc + 1:
                callee = stack[-argc - 1]
            if callee is not None and callee not in ("<null>", "<const>",
                                                     "<expr>", "<call>"):
                out.append((i, callee, current_line))
            elif callee is not None:
                # Callable came from a computed value (e.g. a subscript
                # or a call result); record it as unknown, not as a name.
                pass
            popped = argc + 2
            stack = stack[:-popped] if popped <= len(stack) else []
            stack.append("<call>")
            continue
        try:
            effect = dis.stack_effect(ins.opcode, ins.arg)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            stack = []
            continue
        if effect < 0:
            pops = -effect
            stack = stack[:-pops] if pops <= len(stack) else []
        elif effect > 0:
            stack.extend("<expr>" for _ in range(effect))
    return out


def _called_names_with_lines(code: types.CodeType
                             ) -> List[Tuple[str, Optional[int], str]]:
    """For one code object, return (dotted callee name, source line,
    evidence string) for every CALL instruction, via _call_sites."""
    instructions = list(dis.get_instructions(code))
    out: List[Tuple[str, Optional[int], str]] = []
    for index, dotted, line in _call_sites(instructions):
        ins = instructions[index]
        out.append((dotted, line, "%s(...) at offset %d"
                    % (dotted, ins.offset)))
    return out


def _matches(name: str, trigger: str) -> bool:
    """True if a dotted callee name triggers a rule name: the trigger
    matches any dotted component (os.system matches 'system',
    os.environ.get matches 'environ', socket.socket matches 'socket').
    Component equality, not substring matching, so 'random' does not
    match 'randomize'."""
    return trigger in name.split(".")


def scan_code(code: types.CodeType, label: str) -> List[Finding]:
    """Scan one compiled module (and every nested code object)."""
    findings: List[Finding] = []
    for obj in _walk_code_objects(code):
        for dotted, line, evidence in _called_names_with_lines(obj):
            for rule in RULES:
                if any(_matches(dotted, trigger) for trigger in rule.names):
                    findings.append(Finding(
                        rule.rule_id, rule.severity, obj.co_qualname,
                        line, evidence, rule.description))
    findings.extend(scan_string_constants(code))
    return findings


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# String-constant scanning
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Suspicious shapes inside string constants.  Each entry is
# (pattern, severity, label).  Patterns are matched against the whole
# constant; a constant can produce several findings.

import re

STRING_PATTERNS: List[Tuple[Any, str, str]] = [
    (re.compile(r"https?://[^\s\"'<>]+"), "low", "URL"),
    (re.compile(r"\b(?:\d{1,3}\.){3}\d{1,3}\b"), "low", "IPv4 address"),
    (re.compile(r"/(?:bin|usr/bin)/(?:sh|bash|zsh|dash)\b"), "medium",
     "shell path"),
    (re.compile(r"/etc/(?:passwd|shadow|sudoers)\b"), "medium",
     "sensitive system file"),
    (re.compile(r"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}"), "high", "possible AWS access key"),
    (re.compile(r"(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}={0,2})"), "low",
     "long base64-looking token"),
    (re.compile(r"\b[0-9a-f]{64}\b"), "low", "long hex token (sha256-size)"),
]


def _is_valid_ipv4(match: str) -> bool:
    return all(0 <= int(part) <= 255 for part in match.split("."))


def scan_string_constants(code: types.CodeType) -> List[Finding]:
    """Flag suspicious shapes inside string constants: URLs, IPs,
    shell paths, cloud keys, long encoded tokens.  String constants
    carry no line number, so the finding is attributed to the code
    object that owns the constant."""
    findings: List[Finding] = []
    for obj in _walk_code_objects(code):
        for const in obj.co_consts:
            if not isinstance(const, str):
                continue
            for pattern, severity, label in STRING_PATTERNS:
                for match in pattern.finditer(const):
                    text = match.group(0)
                    if label == "IPv4 address" and not _is_valid_ipv4(text):
                        continue
                    if label == "long base64-looking token" and len(text) < 40:
                        continue
                    findings.append(Finding(
                        "SUSPICIOUS_STRING", severity, obj.co_qualname,
                        None,
                        'string contains %s %r' % (label, text[:80]),
                        "suspicious %s in a string constant" % label))
    return findings


def scan_source(path: str | Path) -> ScanReport:
    """Scan a .py source file by compiling and scanning its bytecode."""
    path = Path(path)
    code = compile(path.read_text(), str(path), "exec")
    return ScanReport(str(path), scan_code(code, str(path)))


def scan_pyc(path: str | Path) -> ScanReport:
    """Scan a .pyc file: no source needed, bytecode only."""
    import importlib.util
    import marshal
    data = Path(path).read_bytes()
    magic = importlib.util.MAGIC_NUMBER
    if data[:4] != magic:
        raise ValueError("not a .pyc for this interpreter: %s" % path)
    code = marshal.loads(data[16:])
    return ScanReport(str(path), scan_code(code, str(path)))


def scan_directory(root: str | Path, suffix: str = ".py") -> List[ScanReport]:
    """Scan every matching file under a directory, highest risk first."""
    root = Path(root)
    reports = []
    for path in sorted(root.rglob("*" + suffix)):
        if "__pycache__" in path.parts:
            continue
        try:
            reports.append(scan_source(path))
        except (SyntaxError, OSError) as exc:
            reports.append(ScanReport(
                str(path), [Finding("SCAN_ERROR", "info", "<module>", None,
                                    "could not scan: %s" % exc,
                                    "file could not be compiled")]))
    reports.sort(key=lambda r: -r.score)
    return reports


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Source-level cross-check (ast)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def scan_source_ast(path: str | Path) -> List[Finding]:
    """Same rules, applied to the AST instead of the bytecode.  Used to
    cross-check the bytecode scanner: the two should agree on direct
    calls.  The AST version also sees attribute chains exactly."""
    path = Path(path)
    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(), filename=str(path))
    findings: List[Finding] = []

    def dotted_name(node: ast.AST) -> Optional[str]:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
            return node.id
        if isinstance(node, ast.Attribute):
            base = dotted_name(node.value)
            return "%s.%s" % (base, node.attr) if base else node.attr
        return None

    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            continue
        name = dotted_name(node.func)
        if name is None:
            continue
        # Find the enclosing function for context.
        func = "<module>"
        for parent in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(parent, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                                   ast.ClassDef)):
                if (parent.lineno <= node.lineno
                        and node.lineno <= (parent.end_lineno or node.lineno)):
                    func = parent.name
        for rule in RULES:
            if any(_matches(name, trigger) for trigger in rule.names):
                findings.append(Finding(
                    rule.rule_id, rule.severity, func, node.lineno,
                    "%s(...)" % name, rule.description))
    return findings


def cross_check(path: str | Path) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Compare bytecode and AST findings for one file: report the
    findings each method found that the other missed."""
    bytecode = scan_source(path).findings
    ast_findings = scan_source_ast(path)
    key = lambda f: (f.rule_id, f.function, f.line)
    bc_keys = {key(f) for f in bytecode}
    ast_keys = {key(f) for f in ast_findings}
    return {
        "bytecode_only": sorted(bc_keys - ast_keys),
        "ast_only": sorted(ast_keys - bc_keys),
        "common": sorted(bc_keys & ast_keys),
        "bytecode_count": len(bytecode),
        "ast_count": len(ast_findings),
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Planted test sample (deliberately suspicious code)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

PLANTED_SAMPLE = '''\
"""Deliberately suspicious module, used to test the scanner."""

import os
import socket
import subprocess
import pickle
import hashlib


def run_user_code(text):
    return eval(text)          # DANGEROUS_EXEC


def launch(cmd):
    return subprocess.Popen(cmd, shell=True)   # PROCESS


def phone_home(host):
    s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM)  # NETWORK
    s.connect((host, 80))
    return s


def read_secret():
    return os.environ.get("API_KEY")   # ENV


def load_blob(data):
    return pickle.loads(data)   # SERIALISATION


def weak_hash(data):
    return hashlib.md5(data).hexdigest()   # CRYPTO_WEAK


def delete_it(path):
    os.remove(path)   # FILESYSTEM


CALLBACK_URL = "http://evil.example.com/collect"
SHELL = "/bin/sh"
LONG_TOKEN = "QUJDREVGR0hJSktMTU5PUFFSU1RVVldYWVphYmNkZWZnaGlqa2xtbm9wcXJzdHV2d3h5ejAxMjM0NTY3ODk="


def clean():
    return "nothing suspicious here"
'''


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _run_demo() -> int:
    failures: List[str] = []

    def check(condition: bool, message: str) -> None:
        print(("  ok  " if condition else " FAIL ") + message)
        if not condition:
            failures.append(message)

    print("== reversing_scanner demo ==")
    import tempfile

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        sample = Path(tmp) / "planted.py"
        sample.write_text(PLANTED_SAMPLE)
        report = scan_source(sample)
        print(report.full_report())
        print()

        check(report.has_rule("DANGEROUS_EXEC"),
              "planted eval() triggers DANGEROUS_EXEC")
        check(report.has_rule("PROCESS"), "planted Popen triggers PROCESS")
        check(report.has_rule("NETWORK"), "planted socket triggers NETWORK")
        check(report.has_rule("ENV"), "planted environ triggers ENV")
        check(report.has_rule("SERIALISATION"),
              "planted pickle.loads triggers SERIALISATION")
        check(report.has_rule("CRYPTO_WEAK"),
              "planted md5 triggers CRYPTO_WEAK")
        check(report.has_rule("FILESYSTEM"),
              "planted os.remove triggers FILESYSTEM")
        check(report.risk == "HIGH",
              "planted sample risk is HIGH (score %d)" % report.score)

        eval_findings = [f for f in report.findings
                         if f.rule_id == "DANGEROUS_EXEC"]
        check(len(eval_findings) == 1,
              "exactly one DANGEROUS_EXEC finding (got %d)"
              % len(eval_findings))
        if eval_findings:
            check(eval_findings[0].function == "run_user_code",
                  "eval finding is in run_user_code")
            check(eval_findings[0].line == 11,
                  "eval finding is on line 11 (got %s)"
                  % eval_findings[0].line)
            check(eval_findings[0].severity == "high",
                  "eval finding severity is high")

        clean = report.findings_in("clean")
        check(clean == [], "clean() produces no findings")
        check(report.findings_in("launch")[0].rule_id == "PROCESS",
              "launch() finding is PROCESS")

        # Suspicious string constants in the planted sample.
        check(report.has_rule("SUSPICIOUS_STRING"),
              "planted URL / shell path trigger SUSPICIOUS_STRING")
        string_hits = [f for f in report.findings
                       if f.rule_id == "SUSPICIOUS_STRING"]
        check(any("evil.example.com" in f.evidence for f in string_hits),
              "planted callback URL found in a string constant")
        check(any("/bin/sh" in f.evidence for f in string_hits),
              "planted /bin/sh found in a string constant")
        check(any("base64" in f.evidence for f in string_hits),
              "planted long base64 token found in a string constant")

        # AST cross-check: the two methods must agree on the planted file.
        cross = cross_check(sample)
        check(cross["bytecode_count"] >= 7,
              "bytecode scanner found >= 7 findings (got %d)"
              % cross["bytecode_count"])
        check(cross["ast_count"] >= 7,
              "AST scanner found >= 7 findings (got %d)" % cross["ast_count"])
        check(len(cross["common"]) >= 7,
              "bytecode and AST agree on >= 7 findings (got %d)"
              % len(cross["common"]))

        # The scanner also works from a .pyc with no source at all.
        import py_compile
        pyc = Path(tmp) / "planted.pyc"
        py_compile.compile(str(sample), cfile=str(pyc), doraise=True)
        pyc_report = scan_pyc(pyc)
        check(pyc_report.has_rule("DANGEROUS_EXEC"),
              ".pyc scan finds the planted eval without source")
        check(pyc_report.has_rule("NETWORK"),
              ".pyc scan finds the planted socket without source")

    # --- real repo files -----------------------------------------------------
    repo = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]

    data_fetch = repo / "mini_ai" / "data_fetch.py"
    if data_fetch.exists():
        rep = scan_source(data_fetch)
        print(rep.summary())
        check(rep.has_rule("NETWORK"),
              "mini_ai/data_fetch.py flagged NETWORK (urllib)")
        check(rep.has_rule("FILESYSTEM"),
              "mini_ai/data_fetch.py flagged FILESYSTEM (writes files)")
        print("  data_fetch.py findings: %d, score %d, risk %s"
              % (len(rep.findings), rep.score, rep.risk))

    self_edit = repo / "mini_ai" / "self_edit.py"
    if self_edit.exists():
        rep = scan_source(self_edit)
        print(rep.summary())
        check(rep.has_rule("DANGEROUS_EXEC") or rep.has_rule("CODEGEN"),
              "mini_ai/self_edit.py flagged for exec-family/codegen use")
        print("  self_edit.py findings: %d, score %d, risk %s"
              % (len(rep.findings), rep.score, rep.risk))

    # --- scan this file itself ----------------------------------------------
    # The planted sample lives in a string literal here, so it must NOT
    # be flagged (string literals are not calls).  But this file really
    # does call compile() in scan_source(), so the scanner must honestly
    # flag itself for that.
    here = Path(__file__)
    self_rep = scan_source(here)
    exec_findings = [f for f in self_rep.findings
                     if f.rule_id == "DANGEROUS_EXEC"]
    check(all("compile" in f.evidence for f in exec_findings),
          "self-scan DANGEROUS_EXEC findings are all compile() calls, "
          "not the string literal (%d finding(s))" % len(exec_findings))
    check(any(f.function == "scan_source" for f in exec_findings),
          "self-scan flags compile() in scan_source")
    check(self_rep.has_rule("NETWORK") is False,
          "self-scan has no NETWORK findings (no socket code here)")
    print(self_rep.summary())

    if failures:
        print("DEMO FAILED: %d check(s)" % len(failures))
        return 1
    print("DEMO PASSED: all checks green")
    return 0


def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) == 1:
        return _run_demo()
    target = Path(argv[1])
    if target.is_dir():
        for rep in scan_directory(target):
            print(rep.full_report())
            print()
    else:
        print(scan_source(target).full_report())
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

--- SOURCE: python_samples/reversing_elf.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
reversing_elf.py -- A pure-stdlib ELF header parser.

Parses the ELF (Executable and Linkable Format) headers of a real
binary -- the same format the Linux loader reads -- using only the
struct module:

  * the ELF identification bytes (class, data encoding, version, ABI),
  * the ELF header (type, machine, version, entry point, header
    offsets, header sizes and counts),
  * the program header table (segment types, permissions, file/memory
    sizes, alignment),
  * the section header table, resolved through the section-name string
    table (.text, .data, .symtab, ...),
  * the symbol table (.symtab / .dynsym): names, binding, type,
    defining section and value,
  * the dynamic section (.dynamic): NEEDED libraries, SONAME, RPATH,
  * the note section (.note.ABI-tag): the kernel ABI the binary wants.

Everything is validated as it is read: magic bytes, truncated files,
and inconsistent offsets raise ElfError instead of producing garbage.
The parser handles both 32-bit and 64-bit files and both byte orders,
and reports which it found.

Only the standard library is used.  The file is self-contained; its
__main__ demo parses /bin/ls (and itself, via /usr/bin/python3) and
asserts concrete facts: the machine is x86-64, the entry point is
non-zero, there is a PT_LOAD segment and a PT_INTERP segment naming
the dynamic loader, a .text section exists, and the symbol table
contains 'main'.

Usage:
    python3 reversing_elf.py                    # run the demo
    python3 reversing_elf.py /bin/ls            # full report for a binary
    python3 reversing_elf.py /bin/ls --symbols  # also list symbols
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import struct
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

ELF_MAGIC = b"\x7fELF"

# e_ident indexes.
EI_CLASS = 4       # 1 = 32-bit, 2 = 64-bit
EI_DATA = 5        # 1 = little-endian, 2 = big-endian
EI_VERSION = 6
EI_OSABI = 7
EI_ABIVERSION = 8

ELFCLASS32 = 1
ELFCLASS64 = 2
ELFDATA2LSB = 1
ELFDATA2MSB = 2

CLASSES = {ELFCLASS32: "ELF32", ELFCLASS64: "ELF64"}
DATA_ENCODINGS = {ELFDATA2LSB: "little-endian", ELFDATA2MSB: "big-endian"}
OSABI_NAMES = {
    0: "System V", 1: "HP-UX", 2: "NetBSD", 3: "Linux", 4: "GNU Hurd",
    6: "Solaris", 7: "AIX", 8: "IRIX", 9: "FreeBSD", 10: "Tru64",
    11: "Novell Modesto", 12: "OpenBSD", 13: "OpenVMS", 14: "HP Non-Stop",
    15: "AROS", 16: "FenixOS", 17: "Nuxi CloudABI", 18: "Stratus OpenVOS",
    53: "ARM", 64: "ARM", 97: "ARM", 255: "Standalone",
}

# e_type values.
ET_NAMES = {0: "NONE (no file type)", 1: "REL (relocatable)",
            2: "EXEC (executable)", 3: "DYN (shared object / PIE)",
            4: "CORE (core file)", 0xFE00: "LOOS", 0xFEFF: "HIOS",
            0xFF00: "LOPROC", 0xFFFF: "HIPROC"}

# e_machine values (the common ones; the full table is long).
MACHINE_NAMES = {
    0: "No machine", 2: "SPARC", 3: "x86 (i386)", 8: "MIPS",
    20: "PowerPC", 21: "PowerPC64", 22: "S390", 40: "ARM",
    42: "SH", 43: "SPARC V9", 50: "IA-64", 62: "x86-64",
    75: "VAX", 183: "AArch64", 243: "RISC-V", 247: "BPF",
}

# p_type values.
PT_NAMES = {0: "NULL", 1: "LOAD", 2: "DYNAMIC", 3: "INTERP",
            4: "NOTE", 5: "SHLIB", 6: "PHDR", 7: "TLS",
            0x6474E550: "GNU_EH_FRAME", 0x6474E551: "GNU_STACK",
            0x6474E552: "GNU_RELRO", 0x6474E553: "GNU_PROPERTY",
            0x6474E559: "GNU_SFRAME"}

# p_flags bits.
PF_X = 0x1
PF_W = 0x2
PF_R = 0x4


def segment_flags(flags: int) -> str:
    out = ""
    out += "R" if flags & PF_R else "-"
    out += "W" if flags & PF_W else "-"
    out += "X" if flags & PF_X else "-"
    return out


# sh_type values.
SHT_NAMES = {0: "NULL", 1: "PROGBITS", 2: "SYMTAB", 3: "STRTAB",
             4: "RELA", 5: "HASH", 6: "DYNAMIC", 7: "NOTE",
             8: "NOBITS", 9: "REL", 10: "SHLIB", 11: "DYNSYM",
             14: "INIT_ARRAY", 15: "FINI_ARRAY", 16: "PREINIT_ARRAY",
             17: "GROUP", 18: "SYMTAB_SHNDX", 19: "RELR",
             0x6FFFFFF6: "GNU_HASH", 0x6FFFFFF7: "VERDEF",
             0x6FFFFFF8: "VERNEED", 0x6FFFFFF9: "VERSYM",
             0x70000000: "LOPROC", 0x7FFFFFFF: "HIPROC",
             0x80000000: "LOUSER", 0xFFFFFFFF: "HIUSER"}

# sh_flags bits.
SHF_WRITE = 0x1
SHF_ALLOC = 0x2
SHF_EXECINSTR = 0x4
SHF_MERGE = 0x10
SHF_STRINGS = 0x20
SHF_INFO_LINK = 0x40
SHF_LINK_ORDER = 0x80
SHF_TLS = 0x400


def section_flags(flags: int) -> str:
    bits = []
    if flags & SHF_WRITE:
        bits.append("W")
    if flags & SHF_ALLOC:
        bits.append("A")
    if flags & SHF_EXECINSTR:
        bits.append("X")
    if flags & SHF_MERGE:
        bits.append("M")
    if flags & SHF_STRINGS:
        bits.append("S")
    if flags & SHF_TLS:
        bits.append("T")
    return "".join(bits) or "-"


# Symbol binding and type (st_info: bind << 4 | type).
STB_NAMES = {0: "LOCAL", 1: "GLOBAL", 2: "WEAK", 10: "GNU_UNIQUE"}
STT_NAMES = {0: "NOTYPE", 1: "OBJECT", 2: "FUNC", 3: "SECTION",
             4: "FILE", 5: "COMMON", 6: "TLS", 10: "GNU_IFUNC"}

# d_tag values for the dynamic section.
DT_NAMES = {0: "NULL", 1: "NEEDED", 2: "PLTRELSZ", 3: "PLTGOT",
            4: "HASH", 5: "STRTAB", 6: "SYMTAB", 7: "RELA",
            8: "RELASZ", 9: "RELAENT", 10: "STRSZ", 11: "SYMENT",
            12: "INIT", 13: "FINI", 14: "SONAME", 15: "RPATH",
            16: "SYMBOLIC", 17: "REL", 18: "RELSZ", 19: "RELENT",
            20: "PLTREL", 21: "DEBUG", 22: "TEXTREL", 23: "JMPREL",
            24: "BIND_NOW", 25: "INIT_ARRAY", 26: "FINI_ARRAY",
            27: "INIT_ARRAYSZ", 28: "FINI_ARRAYSZ", 29: "RUNPATH",
            30: "FLAGS", 32: "PREINIT_ARRAY", 33: "PREINIT_ARRAYSZ",
            0x6FFFFFFA: "FLAGS_1", 0x6FFFFFFB: "VERNEED",
            0x6FFFFFFC: "VERNEEDNUM", 0x6FFFFFFD: "VERSYM",
            0x6FFFFFF0: "VERSYM", 0x6FFFFDF5: "GNU_HASH",
            0x6FFFFDF6: "TLSDESC_PLT", 0x6FFFFDF7: "TLSDESC_GOT",
            0x6FFFFDF8: "GNU_CONFLICT", 0x6FFFFDF9: "CONFIG",
            0x6FFFFDFA: "DEPAUDIT", 0x6FFFFDFB: "AUDIT",
            0x6FFFFDFC: "PLTPAD", 0x6FFFFDFD: "MOVETAB",
            0x6FFFFDFE: "SYMINFO", 0x6FFFFEFF: "RELACOUNT",
            0x6FFFFEFE: "RELCOUNT", 0x6FFFFDF6 + 1: "?"}


class ElfError(ValueError):
    """Raised when a file is not a parseable ELF file."""


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data classes
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class ElfHeader:
    elf_class: int
    data_encoding: int
    osabi: int
    abi_version: int
    e_type: int
    machine: int
    version: int
    entry: int
    phoff: int
    shoff: int
    flags: int
    ehsize: int
    phentsize: int
    phnum: int
    shentsize: int
    shnum: int
    shstrndx: int

    @property
    def is_64bit(self) -> bool:
        return self.elf_class == ELFCLASS64

    @property
    def is_little_endian(self) -> bool:
        return self.data_encoding == ELFDATA2LSB

    @property
    def class_name(self) -> str:
        return CLASSES.get(self.elf_class, "unknown class %d" % self.elf_class)

    @property
    def machine_name(self) -> str:
        return MACHINE_NAMES.get(self.machine,
                                 "unknown machine %d" % self.machine)

    @property
    def type_name(self) -> str:
        return ET_NAMES.get(self.e_type, "unknown type %d" % self.e_type)

    def describe(self) -> List[str]:
        return [
            "class:        %s" % self.class_name,
            "encoding:     %s" % DATA_ENCODINGS.get(
                self.data_encoding, "unknown %d" % self.data_encoding),
            "OS/ABI:       %s (ABI version %d)" % (
                OSABI_NAMES.get(self.osabi, "unknown %d" % self.osabi),
                self.abi_version),
            "type:         %s" % self.type_name,
            "machine:      %s" % self.machine_name,
            "version:      %d" % self.version,
            "entry point:  0x%x" % self.entry,
            "phoff:        %d (program headers)" % self.phoff,
            "shoff:        %d (section headers)" % self.shoff,
            "flags:        0x%x" % self.flags,
            "header size:  %d bytes" % self.ehsize,
            "program headers: %d x %d bytes" % (self.phnum, self.phentsize),
            "section headers: %d x %d bytes" % (self.shnum, self.shentsize),
            "section-name string table index: %d" % self.shstrndx,
        ]


@dataclass
class ProgramHeader:
    p_type: int
    flags: int
    offset: int
    vaddr: int
    paddr: int
    filesz: int
    memsz: int
    align: int

    @property
    def type_name(self) -> str:
        return PT_NAMES.get(self.p_type, "unknown 0x%x" % self.p_type)

    def describe(self) -> str:
        return ("%-12s flags %s  off 0x%06x  vaddr 0x%08x  "
                "filesz %7d  memsz %7d  align 0x%x"
                % (self.type_name, segment_flags(self.flags),
                   self.offset, self.vaddr, self.filesz, self.memsz,
                   self.align))


@dataclass
class SectionHeader:
    index: int
    name_offset: int
    sh_type: int
    flags: int
    addr: int
    offset: int
    size: int
    link: int
    info: int
    align: int
    entsize: int
    name: str = ""

    @property
    def type_name(self) -> str:
        return SHT_NAMES.get(self.sh_type, "unknown 0x%x" % self.sh_type)

    def describe(self) -> str:
        return ("[%2d] %-20s %-10s flags %-6s addr 0x%08x  "
                "off 0x%06x  size %7d"
                % (self.index, self.name, self.type_name,
                   section_flags(self.flags), self.addr, self.offset,
                   self.size))


@dataclass
class Symbol:
    index: int
    name: str
    value: int
    size: int
    info: int
    other: int
    shndx: int

    @property
    def bind(self) -> str:
        return STB_NAMES.get(self.info >> 4,
                             "unknown %d" % (self.info >> 4))

    @property
    def type(self) -> str:
        return STT_NAMES.get(self.info & 0xF,
                             "unknown %d" % (self.info & 0xF))

    def describe(self) -> str:
        return ("[%4d] %-32s %-6s %-8s value 0x%08x  size %6d  shndx %d"
                % (self.index, self.name, self.bind, self.type,
                   self.value, self.size, self.shndx))


@dataclass
class DynamicEntry:
    tag: int
    value: int

    @property
    def tag_name(self) -> str:
        return DT_NAMES.get(self.tag, "unknown 0x%x" % self.tag)


@dataclass
class NoteEntry:
    """One ELF note: a name, a type, and a descriptor payload."""
    name: str
    note_type: int
    desc: bytes
    section: str = ""

    @property
    def type_name(self) -> str:
        if self.name == "GNU":
            return {1: "NT_GNU_ABI_TAG", 2: "NT_GNU_HWCAP",
                    3: "NT_GNU_BUILD_ID", 4: "NT_GNU_GOLD_VERSION",
                    5: "NT_GNU_PROPERTY_TYPE_0"}.get(
                        self.note_type, "NT_GNU_%d" % self.note_type)
        return "type %d" % self.note_type

    def abi_tag(self) -> Optional[Tuple[str, int, int, int]]:
        """Interpret an NT_GNU_ABI_TAG descriptor: (OS name, major,
        minor, subminor).  The descriptor is 4 x 32-bit words: OS,
        major, minor, subminor."""
        if self.note_type != 1 or len(self.desc) < 16:
            return None
        os_id, major, minor, sub = struct.unpack("<IIII", self.desc[:16])
        os_names = {0: "Linux", 1: "Hurd", 2: "Solaris", 3: "FreeBSD",
                    4: "NetBSD", 5: "Syllable", 6: "NaCl"}
        return (os_names.get(os_id, "OS %d" % os_id), major, minor, sub)

    def build_id(self) -> Optional[str]:
        """Hex build ID for NT_GNU_BUILD_ID notes."""
        if self.note_type != 3:
            return None
        return self.desc.hex()

    def describe(self) -> str:
        extra = ""
        tag = self.abi_tag()
        if tag is not None:
            extra = " -> OS %s, ABI %d.%d.%d" % tag
        elif self.build_id() is not None:
            extra = " -> build ID %s" % self.build_id()
        return "%s in %s: %s (%d bytes desc)%s" % (
            self.type_name, self.section, self.name, len(self.desc), extra)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Parser
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class ElfFile:
    """A parsed ELF file: header, program headers, section headers,
    symbols, and dynamic entries."""

    def __init__(self, path: str | Path) -> None:
        self.path = Path(path)
        self.data = self.path.read_bytes()
        self.header = self._parse_header()
        self._endian = "<" if self.header.is_little_endian else ">"
        self.program_headers = self._parse_program_headers()
        self.sections = self._parse_section_headers()
        self.symbols: List[Symbol] = []
        self.dynamic: List[DynamicEntry] = []
        self.notes: List[NoteEntry] = []
        self.interpreter: Optional[str] = None
        self._parse_interpreter()
        self._parse_symbols()
        self._parse_dynamic()
        self._parse_notes()

    # -- low-level helpers --------------------------------------------------
    def _check(self, offset: int, size: int, what: str) -> None:
        if offset < 0 or offset + size > len(self.data):
            raise ElfError(
                "%s at offset %d (size %d) runs past end of file "
                "(%d bytes)" % (what, offset, size, len(self.data)))

    def _read(self, fmt: str, offset: int) -> Tuple[Any, ...]:
        size = struct.calcsize(fmt)
        self._check(offset, size, "read %s" % fmt)
        return struct.unpack_from(fmt, self.data, offset)

    def _cstring(self, table: bytes, offset: int) -> str:
        if offset < 0 or offset >= len(table):
            return ""
        end = table.find(b"\x00", offset)
        if end == -1:
            end = len(table)
        return table[offset:end].decode("utf-8", "replace")

    # -- header ---------------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_header(self) -> ElfHeader:
        if len(self.data) < 16:
            raise ElfError("file too small for an ELF header (%d bytes)"
                           % len(self.data))
        if self.data[:4] != ELF_MAGIC:
            raise ElfError("bad magic: %r (not an ELF file)"
                           % self.data[:4])
        elf_class = self.data[EI_CLASS]
        data_encoding = self.data[EI_DATA]
        if elf_class not in (ELFCLASS32, ELFCLASS64):
            raise ElfError("unknown ELF class %d" % elf_class)
        if data_encoding not in (ELFDATA2LSB, ELFDATA2MSB):
            raise ElfError("unknown data encoding %d" % data_encoding)
        endian = "<" if data_encoding == ELFDATA2LSB else ">"
        # The fixed part of the header is 64 bytes (ELF64) or 52 (ELF32).
        min_size = 64 if elf_class == ELFCLASS64 else 52
        if len(self.data) < min_size:
            raise ElfError("file too small for a %s header (%d bytes)"
                           % (CLASSES[elf_class], len(self.data)))
        if elf_class == ELFCLASS64:
            fmt = endian + "HHIQQQIHHHHHH"
            values = struct.unpack_from(fmt, self.data, 16)
            (e_type, machine, version, entry, phoff, shoff, flags,
             ehsize, phentsize, phnum, shentsize, shnum,
             shstrndx) = values
        else:
            fmt = endian + "HHIIIIIHHHHHH"
            values = struct.unpack_from(fmt, self.data, 16)
            (e_type, machine, version, entry, phoff, shoff, flags,
             ehsize, phentsize, phnum, shentsize, shnum,
             shstrndx) = values
        return ElfHeader(elf_class, data_encoding, self.data[EI_OSABI],
                         self.data[EI_ABIVERSION], e_type, machine,
                         version, entry, phoff, shoff, flags, ehsize,
                         phentsize, phnum, shentsize, shnum, shstrndx)

    # -- program headers --------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_program_headers(self) -> List[ProgramHeader]:
        headers: List[ProgramHeader] = []
        h = self.header
        if h.phnum == 0:
            return headers
        for i in range(h.phnum):
            off = h.phoff + i * h.phentsize
            if h.is_64bit:
                (p_type, flags, offset, vaddr, paddr, filesz, memsz,
                 align) = self._read(self._endian + "IIQQQQQQ", off)
            else:
                (p_type, offset, vaddr, paddr, filesz, memsz, flags,
                 align) = self._read(self._endian + "IIIIIIII", off)
            headers.append(ProgramHeader(p_type, flags, offset, vaddr,
                                         paddr, filesz, memsz, align))
        return headers

    # -- section headers ---------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_section_headers(self) -> List[SectionHeader]:
        headers: List[SectionHeader] = []
        h = self.header
        if h.shnum == 0:
            return headers
        for i in range(h.shnum):
            off = h.shoff + i * h.shentsize
            if h.is_64bit:
                (name, sh_type, flags, addr, offset, size, link, info,
                 align, entsize) = self._read(
                     self._endian + "IIQQQQIIQQ", off)
            else:
                (name, sh_type, flags, addr, offset, size, link, info,
                 align, entsize) = self._read(
                     self._endian + "IIIIIIIIII", off)
            headers.append(SectionHeader(i, name, sh_type, flags, addr,
                                         offset, size, link, info, align,
                                         entsize))
        # Resolve names through the section-name string table.
        if 0 <= h.shstrndx < len(headers):
            strtab = headers[h.shstrndx]
            self._check(strtab.offset, strtab.size, "shstrtab")
            table = self.data[strtab.offset:strtab.offset + strtab.size]
            for section in headers:
                section.name = self._cstring(table, section.name_offset)
        return headers

    def section_by_name(self, name: str) -> Optional[SectionHeader]:
        for section in self.sections:
            if section.name == name:
                return section
        return None

    def section_data(self, section: SectionHeader) -> bytes:
        if section.sh_type == 8:  # SHT_NOBITS: occupies no file bytes.
            return b""
        self._check(section.offset, section.size, "section %s" % section.name)
        return self.data[section.offset:section.offset + section.size]

    # -- interpreter ---------------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_interpreter(self) -> None:
        for phdr in self.program_headers:
            if phdr.p_type == 3:  # PT_INTERP
                self._check(phdr.offset, phdr.filesz, "PT_INTERP")
                raw = self.data[phdr.offset:phdr.offset + phdr.filesz]
                self.interpreter = raw.rstrip(b"\x00").decode(
                    "utf-8", "replace")
                return

    # -- symbols ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_symbol_table(self, section: SectionHeader) -> List[Symbol]:
        strtab = self.sections[section.link] if section.link < len(self.sections) else None
        strings = b""
        if strtab is not None:
            strings = self.section_data(strtab)
        out: List[Symbol] = []
        if section.entsize == 0:
            return out
        count = section.size // section.entsize
        for i in range(count):
            off = section.offset + i * section.entsize
            if self.header.is_64bit:
                (name_off, info, other, shndx, value, size) = self._read(
                    self._endian + "IBBHQQ", off)
            else:
                (name_off, value, size, info, other, shndx) = self._read(
                    self._endian + "IIIBBH", off)
            out.append(Symbol(i, self._cstring(strings, name_off), value,
                              size, info, other, shndx))
        return out

    def _parse_symbols(self) -> None:
        for section in self.sections:
            if section.sh_type in (2, 11):  # SHT_SYMTAB, SHT_DYNSYM
                self.symbols.extend(self._parse_symbol_table(section))

    def find_symbol(self, name: str) -> Optional[Symbol]:
        for symbol in self.symbols:
            if symbol.name == name:
                return symbol
        return None

    def functions(self) -> List[Symbol]:
        return [s for s in self.symbols if s.type == "FUNC" and s.name]

    # -- dynamic section ---------------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_dynamic(self) -> None:
        section = self.section_by_name(".dynamic")
        if section is None:
            return
        data = self.section_data(section)
        entry_size = 16 if self.header.is_64bit else 8
        fmt = self._endian + ("qQ" if self.header.is_64bit else "iI")
        for i in range(0, len(data) - entry_size + 1, entry_size):
            tag, value = struct.unpack_from(fmt, data, i)
            self.dynamic.append(DynamicEntry(tag, value))
            if tag == 0:
                break

    def needed_libraries(self) -> List[str]:
        """DT_NEEDED library names, resolved through .dynstr."""
        dynstr = self.section_by_name(".dynstr")
        if dynstr is None:
            return []
        strings = self.section_data(dynstr)
        return [self._cstring(strings, entry.value)
                for entry in self.dynamic if entry.tag == 1]

    # -- notes -------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _parse_notes(self) -> None:
        """Parse every SHT_NOTE section.  Each note is three 32-bit
        words (namesz, descsz, type), then the name (padded to 4),
        then the descriptor (padded to 4)."""
        for section in self.sections:
            if section.sh_type != 7:  # SHT_NOTE
                continue
            data = self.section_data(section)
            offset = 0
            while offset + 12 <= len(data):
                namesz, descsz, note_type = struct.unpack_from(
                    self._endian + "III", data, offset)
                offset += 12
                name_raw = data[offset:offset + namesz]
                name = name_raw.rstrip(b"\x00").decode("utf-8", "replace")
                offset += (namesz + 3) & ~3
                desc = data[offset:offset + descsz]
                offset += (descsz + 3) & ~3
                self.notes.append(NoteEntry(name, note_type, desc,
                                            section.name))

    def abi_tag(self) -> Optional[Tuple[str, int, int, int]]:
        """The GNU ABI tag, if the file carries one."""
        for note in self.notes:
            tag = note.abi_tag()
            if tag is not None:
                return tag
        return None

    def build_id(self) -> Optional[str]:
        for note in self.notes:
            build_id = note.build_id()
            if build_id is not None:
                return build_id
        return None

    # -- derived facts ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def load_segments(self) -> List[ProgramHeader]:
        return [p for p in self.program_headers if p.p_type == 1]

    def total_mapped_size(self) -> int:
        """Span of virtual addresses covered by PT_LOAD segments."""
        loads = self.load_segments()
        if not loads:
            return 0
        lo = min(p.vaddr for p in loads)
        hi = max(p.vaddr + p.memsz for p in loads)
        return hi - lo

    def executable_segments(self) -> List[ProgramHeader]:
        return [p for p in self.load_segments() if p.flags & PF_X]

    def writable_segments(self) -> List[ProgramHeader]:
        return [p for p in self.load_segments() if p.flags & PF_W]

    def section_sizes_by_type(self) -> Dict[str, int]:
        totals: Dict[str, int] = {}
        for section in self.sections:
            totals[section.type_name] = (totals.get(section.type_name, 0)
                                         + section.size)
        return totals

    def check_entry_in_executable_segment(self) -> bool:
        """True if the entry point lies inside an executable PT_LOAD."""
        for seg in self.executable_segments():
            if seg.vaddr <= self.header.entry < seg.vaddr + seg.memsz:
                return True
        return False

    # -- strings and entropy ---------------------------------------------------
    def extract_strings(self, min_length: int = 4
                        ) -> List[Tuple[int, str]]:
        """The classic `strings` utility: every run of printable ASCII
        (0x20-0x7E) of at least min_length bytes, with its file offset.
        The fastest first look at what a binary contains."""
        out: List[Tuple[int, str]] = []
        start: Optional[int] = None
        for i, byte in enumerate(self.data):
            if 0x20 <= byte <= 0x7E:
                if start is None:
                    start = i
            else:
                if start is not None and i - start >= min_length:
                    out.append((start,
                                self.data[start:i].decode("ascii")))
                start = None
        if start is not None and len(self.data) - start >= min_length:
            out.append((start, self.data[start:].decode("ascii")))
        return out

    def strings_containing(self, needle: str,
                           min_length: int = 4) -> List[Tuple[int, str]]:
        """All extracted strings that contain `needle`."""
        return [(off, s) for off, s in self.extract_strings(min_length)
                if needle in s]

    @staticmethod
    def shannon_entropy(data: bytes) -> float:
        """Shannon entropy of a byte string, in bits per byte (0-8).
        ~0 for uniform data, ~8 for random/encrypted data; packed or
        encrypted sections stand out at 7.5+ while code is usually
        5-7."""
        import math
        if not data:
            return 0.0
        counts = [0] * 256
        for byte in data:
            counts[byte] += 1
        entropy = 0.0
        for count in counts:
            if count:
                p = count / len(data)
                entropy -= p * math.log2(p)
        return entropy

    def section_entropy(self, section: SectionHeader) -> float:
        """Entropy of one section's file bytes."""
        return self.shannon_entropy(self.section_data(section))

    def entropy_report(self) -> List[Tuple[str, float, int]]:
        """(section name, entropy, size) for every section with file
        bytes, most entropic first."""
        rows = [(s.name, self.section_entropy(s), s.size)
                for s in self.sections if s.size > 0 and s.sh_type != 8]
        rows.sort(key=lambda row: -row[1])
        return rows

    # -- reporting ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    def report(self, show_symbols: bool = False) -> str:
        lines = ["== %s ==" % self.path, ""]
        lines.append("-- ELF header --")
        lines.extend(self.header.describe())
        lines.append("")
        lines.append("-- program headers (%d) --" % len(self.program_headers))
        for phdr in self.program_headers:
            lines.append("  " + phdr.describe())
        if self.interpreter:
            lines.append("  interpreter: %s" % self.interpreter)
        lines.append("")
        lines.append("-- sections (%d) --" % len(self.sections))
        for section in self.sections:
            lines.append("  " + section.describe())
        lines.append("")
        needed = self.needed_libraries()
        if needed:
            lines.append("-- needed libraries (%d) --" % len(needed))
            for lib in needed:
                lines.append("  %s" % lib)
            lines.append("")
        lines.append("-- derived facts --")
        lines.append("  load segments: %d, mapped size: %d bytes (%.1f KiB)"
                     % (len(self.load_segments()), self.total_mapped_size(),
                        self.total_mapped_size() / 1024))
        lines.append("  executable segments: %d, writable segments: %d"
                     % (len(self.executable_segments()),
                        len(self.writable_segments())))
        lines.append("  entry point in an executable segment: %s"
                     % self.check_entry_in_executable_segment())
        lines.append("  symbols parsed: %d (%d functions)"
                     % (len(self.symbols), len(self.functions())))
        if self.notes:
            lines.append("  notes: %d" % len(self.notes))
            for note in self.notes:
                lines.append("    " + note.describe())
            tag = self.abi_tag()
            if tag is not None:
                lines.append("  ABI tag: OS %s, version %d.%d.%d" % tag)
        if show_symbols:
            lines.append("")
            lines.append("-- symbols --")
            for symbol in self.symbols:
                if symbol.name:
                    lines.append("  " + symbol.describe())
        return "\n".join(lines)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo / self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _run_demo() -> int:
    failures: List[str] = []

    def check(condition: bool, message: str) -> None:
        print(("  ok  " if condition else " FAIL ") + message)
        if not condition:
            failures.append(message)

    print("== reversing_elf demo ==")

    # --- /bin/ls: a real dynamically linked x86-64 executable -------------
    ls_path = Path("/bin/ls")
    if not ls_path.exists():
        print("  /bin/ls not found; demo cannot run")
        return 1
    elf = ElfFile(ls_path)
    print("\n".join("  " + line for line in elf.header.describe()))
    check(elf.header.is_64bit, "/bin/ls is a 64-bit ELF")
    check(elf.header.is_little_endian, "/bin/ls is little-endian")
    check(elf.header.machine == 62, "/bin/ls machine is x86-64 (62)")
    check(elf.header.machine_name == "x86-64",
          "machine name is x86-64")
    check(elf.header.e_type in (2, 3),
          "/bin/ls is EXEC or DYN (PIE) (got %s)" % elf.header.type_name)
    check(elf.header.entry != 0, "/bin/ls entry point is non-zero: 0x%x"
          % elf.header.entry)
    check(elf.header.phnum >= 4, "/bin/ls has >= 4 program headers (got %d)"
          % elf.header.phnum)
    check(elf.header.shnum >= 10, "/bin/ls has >= 10 sections (got %d)"
          % elf.header.shnum)
    check(elf.check_entry_in_executable_segment(),
          "entry point lies in an executable PT_LOAD segment")
    check(any(p.p_type == 1 for p in elf.program_headers),
          "/bin/ls has a PT_LOAD segment")
    check(any(p.p_type == 3 for p in elf.program_headers),
          "/bin/ls has a PT_INTERP segment")
    check(elf.interpreter is not None
          and "ld-linux" in elf.interpreter,
          "interpreter is the dynamic loader: %s" % elf.interpreter)
    check(len(elf.load_segments()) >= 2,
          "/bin/ls has >= 2 PT_LOAD segments")
    check(len(elf.executable_segments()) >= 1,
          "/bin/ls has >= 1 executable segment")
    check(len(elf.writable_segments()) >= 1,
          "/bin/ls has >= 1 writable segment")
    check(elf.total_mapped_size() > 100_000,
          "mapped size > 100 KiB (got %d)" % elf.total_mapped_size())

    text = elf.section_by_name(".text")
    check(text is not None, "/bin/ls has a .text section")
    if text is not None:
        check(text.type_name == "PROGBITS", ".text is PROGBITS")
        check(bool(text.flags & SHF_EXECINSTR), ".text is executable")
        check(text.size > 10_000, ".text is > 10 KiB (got %d)" % text.size)
    check(elf.section_by_name(".symtab") is not None
          or elf.section_by_name(".dynsym") is not None,
          "/bin/ls has a symbol table")
    check(len(elf.symbols) > 0, "/bin/ls has symbols parsed")
    needed = elf.needed_libraries()
    print("  needed libraries: %s" % needed)
    check(any("libc.so" in lib for lib in needed),
          "/bin/ls needs libc")

    # Symbols: /bin/ls is usually stripped of .symtab but keeps .dynsym.
    main = elf.find_symbol("main")
    if main is not None:
        check(main.type == "FUNC", "main is a FUNC symbol")
        print("  main: %s" % main.describe())
    else:
        # A stripped binary may not export main; the dynamic symbols
        # must still contain well-known libc imports (ls is built with
        # _FORTIFY_SOURCE, so printf appears as __printf_chk).
        printf = elf.find_symbol("__printf_chk") or elf.find_symbol("snprintf")
        check(printf is not None,
              "dynamic symbols contain __printf_chk or snprintf "
              "(main not exported)")
        print("  (main not in symbol table; %s found instead)"
              % (printf.name if printf else "nothing"))

    # --- /usr/bin/python3: the interpreter running this demo ---------------
    python = Path(sys.executable)
    py_elf = ElfFile(python)
    check(py_elf.header.machine == 62, "python3 is x86-64")
    check(py_elf.header.is_64bit, "python3 is 64-bit")
    check(py_elf.section_by_name(".text") is not None,
          "python3 has a .text section")
    check(py_elf.find_symbol("Py_BytesMain") is not None
          or py_elf.find_symbol("main") is not None,
          "python3 exports main or Py_BytesMain")

    # --- error paths ----------------------------------------------------------
    import tempfile
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        tmpdir = Path(tmp)
        not_elf = tmpdir / "plain.txt"
        not_elf.write_text("this is not an ELF file at all")
        try:
            ElfFile(not_elf)
            check(False, "non-ELF file raises ElfError")
        except ElfError:
            check(True, "non-ELF file raises ElfError")
        truncated = tmpdir / "trunc.elf"
        truncated.write_bytes(ls_path.read_bytes()[:20])
        try:
            ElfFile(truncated)
            check(False, "truncated ELF raises ElfError")
        except ElfError:
            check(True, "truncated ELF raises ElfError")
        empty = tmpdir / "empty"
        empty.write_bytes(b"")
        try:
            ElfFile(empty)
            check(False, "empty file raises ElfError")
        except ElfError:
            check(True, "empty file raises ElfError")

    # --- notes ----------------------------------------------------------------
    check(len(elf.notes) >= 3,
          "/bin/ls has >= 3 note entries (got %d)" % len(elf.notes))
    tag = elf.abi_tag()
    check(tag is not None, "/bin/ls carries a GNU ABI tag")
    if tag is not None:
        check(tag[0] == "Linux", "ABI tag OS is Linux (got %s)" % tag[0])
        print("  ABI tag: OS %s, version %d.%d.%d" % tag)
    check(elf.build_id() is not None,
          "/bin/ls carries a GNU build ID")
    if elf.build_id():
        check(len(elf.build_id()) == 40,
              "build ID is 20 bytes (40 hex chars)")

    # --- strings and entropy --------------------------------------------------
    strings = elf.extract_strings()
    check(len(strings) > 100,
          "/bin/ls yields > 100 printable strings (got %d)" % len(strings))
    check(any("libc.so.6" in s for _, s in strings),
          "strings extraction finds 'libc.so.6'")
    check(any("ld-linux" in s for _, s in strings),
          "strings extraction finds the dynamic loader path")
    hits = elf.strings_containing("libc.so.6")
    check(len(hits) >= 1, "strings_containing finds libc.so.6")
    check(all(off >= 0 for off, _ in hits),
          "string offsets are non-negative")
    text_entropy = elf.section_entropy(text) if text is not None else 0.0
    print("  .text entropy: %.2f bits/byte" % text_entropy)
    check(4.0 < text_entropy < 8.0,
          ".text entropy is in the code-like range (got %.2f)"
          % text_entropy)
    check(ElfFile.shannon_entropy(b"\x00" * 100) == 0.0,
          "entropy of uniform bytes is 0")
    check(abs(ElfFile.shannon_entropy(bytes(range(256))) - 8.0) < 0.01,
          "entropy of all 256 byte values is 8")
    ent_report = elf.entropy_report()
    check(len(ent_report) > 0, "entropy report is non-empty")
    check(ent_report[0][1] >= ent_report[-1][1],
          "entropy report is sorted most-entropic first")

    # --- section size accounting is consistent --------------------------------
    totals = elf.section_sizes_by_type()
    check("PROGBITS" in totals, "section sizes by type has PROGBITS")
    check(sum(totals.values()) > 0, "section sizes sum to > 0")

    if failures:
        print("DEMO FAILED: %d check(s)" % len(failures))
        return 1
    print("DEMO PASSED: all checks green")
    return 0


def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) == 1:
        return _run_demo()
    path = Path(argv[1])
    show_symbols = "--symbols" in argv[2:]
    print(ElfFile(path).report(show_symbols=show_symbols))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv))

--- SOURCE: python_samples/transform_folding.py ---
"""Constant folding: evaluate constant expressions at compile time.

This module implements a source-to-source AST transformer that replaces
expressions whose value is fully known at compile time with the resulting
literal.  It folds arithmetic, string operations, boolean logic,
comparisons, conditional expressions, constant subscripts, ``len()`` of a
literal, and whole ``if`` / ``while`` statements guarded by a constant.

Safety rules (the transformer never changes observable behaviour):

* An expression is folded only when every operand is a literal constant
  (checked with ``ast.literal_eval``) and evaluating it raises no error.
  Division by zero, huge powers, oversized string repetitions and the
  like are simply left unfolded.
* ``is`` / ``is not`` comparisons are never folded: identity of equal
  constants is an implementation detail, not a language guarantee.
* Boolean ``and`` / ``or`` use real short-circuit *value* semantics, so
  ``0 or "fallback"`` folds to ``"fallback"``, not to ``True``.
* Folding a ``while False:`` body away is safe because the body can
  never execute; folding ``if True:`` keeps only the taken branch.

Every demonstration program in this file is executed twice -- once in its
original form and once after folding -- and the two runs must produce
byte-identical standard output.  Run this file directly to see the
transformer at work and to run its unit tests::

    python3 transform_folding.py
"""

import ast
import contextlib
import io
import operator
import sys
import unittest

__all__ = ["ConstantFolder", "fold_source", "run_source", "check_preserved"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small shared helpers (duplicated in each transform_* file so every file
# runs standalone).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_source(source, extra_globals=None):
    """Execute *source* and return ``(stdout, namespace)``.

    The program runs in a fresh namespace with stdout captured, so the
    caller can compare the observable behaviour of two versions of a
    program without either one polluting the other.
    """
    namespace = {} if extra_globals is None else dict(extra_globals)
    buffer = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buffer):
        exec(compile(source, "<demo>", "exec"), namespace)
    return buffer.getvalue(), namespace


def check_preserved(source, transformer, label=""):
    """Transform *source* and assert identical observable behaviour.

    Returns ``(new_source, original_output, new_output)``.  Raises
    ``AssertionError`` when the transformed program prints anything
    different from the original, or when the transformed source does not
    even compile.
    """
    original_out, _ = run_source(source)
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    new_tree = transformer().visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(new_tree)
    new_source = ast.unparse(new_tree)
    compile(new_source, "<transformed>", "exec")  # must compile cleanly
    new_out, _ = run_source(new_source)
    assert original_out == new_out, (
        "behaviour changed%s:\noriginal: %r\ntransformed: %r\n%s"
        % (" for " + label if label else "", original_out, new_out, new_source)
    )
    return new_source, original_out, new_out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The transformer.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
    ast.LShift: operator.lshift,
    ast.RShift: operator.rshift,
    ast.BitOr: operator.or_,
    ast.BitXor: operator.xor,
    ast.BitAnd: operator.and_,
}

_UNOPS = {
    ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    ast.USub: operator.neg,
    ast.Invert: operator.invert,
    ast.Not: operator.not_,
}

_COMPARATORS = {
    ast.Eq: operator.eq,
    ast.NotEq: operator.ne,
    ast.Lt: operator.lt,
    ast.LtE: operator.le,
    ast.Gt: operator.gt,
    ast.GtE: operator.ge,
    ast.In: lambda a, b: a in b,
    ast.NotIn: lambda a, b: a not in b,
    # ast.Is / ast.IsNot deliberately absent: identity is not a value.
}


def _is_plain_constant(value):
    """True when *value* can safely be written back as an ast.Constant."""
    if value is None or isinstance(value, (bool, int, float, complex, str, bytes)):
        return True
    if isinstance(value, tuple):
        return all(_is_plain_constant(item) for item in value)
    if isinstance(value, frozenset):
        return all(_is_plain_constant(item) for item in value)
    return False


def _literal(node):
    """Return the literal value of *node*, or a sentinel on failure."""
    try:
        return ast.literal_eval(node)
    except (ValueError, TypeError, SyntaxError, MemoryError, RecursionError):
        return _MISSING


_MISSING = object()


def _foldable_result(value):
    """Reject results that would be unsafe or absurd to materialise."""
    if not _is_plain_constant(value):
        return False
    if isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool):
        if value.bit_length() > 4096:  # e.g. 10 ** 10000
            return False
    if isinstance(value, (str, bytes)) and len(value) > 100_000:
        return False
    if isinstance(value, float) and value != value:
        return False  # never fold a NaN into being; keep the expression
    return True


class ConstantFolder(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Replace constant expressions with their computed value.

    The transformer keeps a count of performed folds in ``self.folds``
    so callers (and the demo) can report how much work was done.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.folds = 0

    # -- generic helpers -------------------------------------------------

    def _constant_node(self, value, template):
        """Build a Constant node carrying *value*, positioned like *template*."""
        node = ast.Constant(value=value)
        return ast.copy_location(node, template)

    # -- expression folding ----------------------------------------------

    def visit_BinOp(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        left = _literal(node.left)
        right = _literal(node.right)
        if left is _MISSING or right is _MISSING:
            return node
        func = _BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if func is None:
            return node
        # Guard against absurd powers before evaluating them.
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if isinstance(right, (int, float)) and abs(right) > 64:
                return node
            if isinstance(left, int) and abs(left) > 10 and isinstance(right, int) and right > 10:
                return node
        try:
            value = func(left, right)
        except Exception:
            return node  # e.g. ZeroDivisionError: leave the expression alone
        if not _foldable_result(value):
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return self._constant_node(value, node)

    def visit_UnaryOp(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        operand = _literal(node.operand)
        if operand is _MISSING:
            return node
        func = _UNOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if func is None:
            return node
        try:
            value = func(operand)
        except Exception:
            return node
        if not _foldable_result(value):
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return self._constant_node(value, node)

    def visit_BoolOp(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        values = [_literal(v) for v in node.values]
        if any(v is _MISSING for v in values):
            return node
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.And):
            result = values[0]
            for value in values[1:]:
                if not result:
                    break
                result = value
        elif isinstance(node.op, ast.Or):
            result = values[0]
            for value in values[1:]:
                if result:
                    break
                result = value
        else:
            return node
        if not _foldable_result(result):
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return self._constant_node(result, node)

    def visit_Compare(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        left = _literal(node.left)
        if left is _MISSING:
            return node
        comparators = [_literal(c) for c in node.comparators]
        if any(c is _MISSING for c in comparators):
            return node
        current = left
        try:
            for op, nxt in zip(node.ops, comparators):
                func = _COMPARATORS.get(type(op))
                if func is None:
                    return node
                if not func(current, nxt):
                    self.folds += 1
                    return self._constant_node(False, node)
                current = nxt
        except Exception:
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return self._constant_node(True, node)

    def visit_IfExp(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        test = _literal(node.test)
        if test is _MISSING:
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return node.body if test else node.orelse

    def visit_Subscript(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        value = _literal(node.value)
        if value is _MISSING or not isinstance(value, (str, bytes, tuple)):
            return node
        index = node.slice
        if isinstance(index, ast.Constant):
            key = _literal(index)
            if key is _MISSING or not isinstance(key, int) or isinstance(key, bool):
                return node
            try:
                result = value[key]
            except Exception:
                return node
        elif isinstance(index, ast.Slice):
            bounds = [_literal(b) if b is not None else None for b in
                      (index.lower, index.upper, index.step)]
            if any(b is _MISSING for b in bounds):
                return node
            try:
                result = value[slice(*bounds)]
            except Exception:
                return node
        else:
            return node
        if not _foldable_result(result):
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return self._constant_node(result, node)

    def visit_Call(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        # Only len() of a literal is folded: it is pure and total.
        if (
            isinstance(node.func, ast.Name)
            and node.func.id == "len"
            and len(node.args) == 1
            and not node.keywords
        ):
            arg = _literal(node.args[0])
            if arg is not _MISSING:
                try:
                    value = len(arg)
                except Exception:
                    return node
                self.folds += 1
                return self._constant_node(value, node)
        return node

    # -- statement folding -------------------------------------------------

    def visit_If(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        test = _literal(node.test)
        if test is _MISSING:
            return node
        self.folds += 1
        return node.body if test else node.orelse

    def visit_While(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        test = _literal(node.test)
        if test is _MISSING:
            return node
        if not test:
            self.folds += 1
            return []  # a loop that can never run is gone entirely
        return node  # while True: stays; it is a deliberate infinite loop


def fold_source(source):
    """Fold the constants in *source* and return ``(new_source, folds)``."""
    folder = ConstantFolder()
    tree = folder.visit(ast.parse(source))
    ast.fix_missing_locations(tree)
    return ast.unparse(tree), folder.folds


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demonstration programs.  Each one is run before and after folding and
# the outputs must match exactly.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

DEMO_PROGRAMS = {
    "arithmetic": '''
def area(radius):
    return 3.14159 * radius * radius

def total(price, count):
    return price * count + 2 * 5 - 10 / 2

print("area(3) =", round(area(3), 2))
print("total(7, 4) =", total(7, 4))
print("2 ** 10 =", 2 ** 10)
print("17 % 5 =", 17 % 5)
print("1 << 8 =", 1 << 8)
print("-(-42) =", -(-42))
print("~5 =", ~5)
''',
    "strings_and_collections": '''
GREETING = "Hello, " + "world" + "!"
SEPARATOR = "-" * 10
WORDS = ("alpha", "beta", "gamma")
print(GREETING)
print(SEPARATOR)
print("second word:", WORDS[1])
print("letters:", len("constant"))
print("items:", len([10, 20, 30, 40]))
print("fallback:", "" or "default")
print("chosen:", 0 and "never" or "picked")
''',
    "logic_and_branches": '''
DEBUG = False
LIMIT = 100

def classify(score):
    if score >= 90 and score <= 100:
        return "A"
    if score < 0 or score > 100:
        return "out of range"
    return "other"

if DEBUG:
    print("this line must disappear")
else:
    print("debug is off")

print(classify(95))
print(classify(150))
print(classify(50))
print("in range:", 1 < 2 < 3)
print("word in text:", "ell" in "hello")
print("pick:", "yes" if 3 > 2 else "no")
count = 0
while False:
    count += 1
print("count after dead loop:", count)
''',
    "geometry_with_constants": '''
import math

def circle_stats(radius):
    circumference = 2 * math.pi * radius
    area = math.pi * radius ** 2
    return circumference, area

def describe(n):
    label = "small" if n < 10 else "large"
    doubled = n * 2 + 0
    return label, doubled

c, a = circle_stats(5)
print("circumference:", round(c, 3))
print("area:", round(a, 3))
print(describe(4))
print(describe(25))
print("halved:", 100 // 8, 100 / 8)
''',
}


def demo():
    """Run every demo program through the folder and report the results."""
    print("=" * 70)
    print("Constant folding demo")
    print("=" * 70)
    total_folds = 0
    for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
        folded, folds = fold_source(source)
        total_folds += folds
        # Behaviour preservation: identical stdout, and it must compile.
        check_preserved(source, ConstantFolder, label=name)
        print("\n--- %s: %d fold(s) ---" % (name, folds))
        print(folded.rstrip())
    print("\nTotal folds across all demos: %d" % total_folds)
    # A compact before/after showcase.
    sample = "x = (2 + 3) * (10 - 4)\ny = 'ab' * 3 + '!'\nprint(x, y)\n"
    folded, folds = fold_source(sample)
    print("\nShowcase: %r -> %r (%d folds)" % (sample, folded, folds))
    assert "x = 30" in folded and "y = 'ababab!'" in folded
    print("\nAll behaviour-preservation checks passed.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Unit tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class TestConstantFolder(unittest.TestCase):
    def _fold(self, source):
        return fold_source(source)[0]

    def test_binop_arithmetic(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 2 + 3 * 4\n"), "x = 14")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 2 ** 10\n"), "x = 1024")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 7 // 2\n"), "x = 3")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 7 % 3\n"), "x = 1")

    def test_binop_strings(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'foo' + 'bar'\n"), "x = 'foobar'")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'ab' * 3\n"), "x = 'ababab'")

    def test_no_fold_on_unknown_names(self):
        folded = self._fold("x = n + 1\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "x = n + 1")

    def test_no_fold_division_by_zero(self):
        folded = self._fold("x = 1 / 0\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "x = 1 / 0")

    def test_no_fold_huge_power(self):
        folded = self._fold("x = 2 ** 1000\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "x = 2 ** 1000")

    def test_unary_ops(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = -5\n"), "x = -5")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = not True\n"), "x = False")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = ~0\n"), "x = -1")

    def test_boolop_value_semantics(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 0 or 'fallback'\n"), "x = 'fallback'")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'a' and 'b'\n"), "x = 'b'")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = '' and 'b'\n"), "x = ''")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = True and False\n"), "x = False")

    def test_comparisons(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 3 < 4\n"), "x = True")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 3 == 4\n"), "x = False")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'a' in 'abc'\n"), "x = True")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 1 < 2 < 3\n"), "x = True")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 1 < 2 > 3\n"), "x = False")

    def test_no_fold_identity_comparison(self):
        # `is` must never be folded: identity is not a value property.
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 1 is 1\n"), "x = 1 is 1")

    def test_ifexp_and_if_statement(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'a' if True else 'b'\n"), "x = 'a'")
        folded = self._fold("if False:\n    x = 1\nelse:\n    x = 2\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "x = 2")

    def test_while_false_removed(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("while False:\n    x = 1\n"), "")

    def test_subscript_and_len(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = (10, 20, 30)[2]\n"), "x = 30")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = 'hello'[1]\n"), "x = 'e'")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = len('abcd')\n"), "x = 4")
        self.assertEqual(self._fold("x = len([1, 2, 3])\n"), "x = 3")

    def test_preserves_runtime_values(self):
        for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
            with self.subTest(program=name):
                check_preserved(source, ConstantFolder, label=name)

    def test_fold_count_reported(self):
        _, folds = fold_source("x = 1 + 2\ny = 3 * 4\n")
        self.assertEqual(folds, 2)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()
    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("Unit tests")
    print("=" * 70)
    unittest.main(argv=[sys.argv[0], "-v"], exit=True)

--- SOURCE: python_samples/transform_deadcode.py ---
"""Dead-code elimination: remove code that can never run.

This module implements a source-to-source AST transformer that deletes
statements which are provably unreachable, and simplifies branches whose
condition is a compile-time constant:

* anything after ``return``, ``raise``, ``break``, ``continue`` or an
  ``assert False`` in the same block;
* anything after an ``if`` / ``try`` whose every path terminates;
* anything after a ``while True:`` loop whose body contains no ``break``;
* an ``if True:`` / ``if False:`` (or any constant condition) is
  replaced by the branch that will actually run;
* a ``while False:`` loop is deleted outright;
* a call to ``sys.exit(...)`` ends a block, but only when ``sys`` was
  really imported as a module in this file -- a local variable that
  merely happens to be called ``sys`` is never treated as terminating.

The transformer runs to a fixpoint: deleting one dead branch can expose
another (for example folding ``if True:`` can leave a ``return`` as the
last statement of a block, which then kills everything after the ``if``).

Safety rules:

* Code inside ``try`` / ``finally`` is handled conservatively.  A
  statement after a ``return`` inside a ``try`` body is dead (the
  ``finally`` still runs, because the ``return`` itself is kept), and a
  ``try`` only counts as terminating when its body *and* every handler
  terminate.
* ``sys.exit`` is recognised only via a real ``import sys``.
* Nothing is ever deleted from a block that might still be reached:
  when in doubt the code is kept.

Every demonstration program is executed before and after elimination
and the two runs must produce byte-identical output.  Run directly::

    python3 transform_deadcode.py
"""

import ast
import contextlib
import io
import sys
import unittest

__all__ = ["DeadCodeEliminator", "eliminate_dead_code", "run_source",
           "check_preserved"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small shared helpers (duplicated in each transform_* file so every file
# runs standalone).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_source(source, extra_globals=None):
    """Execute *source* and return ``(stdout, namespace)``."""
    namespace = {} if extra_globals is None else dict(extra_globals)
    buffer = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buffer):
        exec(compile(source, "<demo>", "exec"), namespace)
    return buffer.getvalue(), namespace


def check_preserved(source, transformer, label=""):
    """Transform *source* and assert identical observable behaviour.

    Returns ``(new_source, original_output, new_output)``.
    """
    original_out, _ = run_source(source)
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    new_tree = transformer().visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(new_tree)
    new_source = ast.unparse(new_tree)
    compile(new_source, "<transformed>", "exec")
    new_out, _ = run_source(new_source)
    assert original_out == new_out, (
        "behaviour changed%s:\noriginal: %r\ntransformed: %r\n%s"
        % (" for " + label if label else "", original_out, new_out, new_source)
    )
    return new_source, original_out, new_out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The transformer.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _literal(node):
    try:
        return ast.literal_eval(node)
    except (ValueError, TypeError, SyntaxError, MemoryError, RecursionError):
        return _MISSING


_MISSING = object()


def _walk_no_nested(node, stop_types):
    """Yield nodes under *node* without descending into *stop_types*.

    Used to look for ``break`` / ``return`` inside a block while ignoring
    nested loops and nested functions, whose control flow belongs to
    themselves.
    """
    stack = [node]
    while stack:
        current = stack.pop()
        if isinstance(current, stop_types) and current is not node:
            continue
        yield current
        stack.extend(ast.iter_child_nodes(current))


class DeadCodeEliminator(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Delete unreachable statements and simplify constant branches."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.removed = 0          # statements deleted
        self.simplified = 0       # constant branches resolved
        self._sys_imported = False
