    # -- entry point -------------------------------------------------------

    def visit_Module(self, node):
        self._sys_imported = any(
            isinstance(child, ast.Import)
            and any(alias.name == "sys" for alias in child.names)
            for child in node.body
        )
        self.generic_visit(node)
        self._prune_tree(node)
        return node

    # -- termination analysis ----------------------------------------------

    def _is_sys_exit_call(self, node):
        return (
            self._sys_imported
            and isinstance(node, ast.Expr)
            and isinstance(node.value, ast.Call)
            and isinstance(node.value.func, ast.Attribute)
            and node.value.func.attr == "exit"
            and isinstance(node.value.func.value, ast.Name)
            and node.value.func.value.id == "sys"
        )

    def _stmt_terminates(self, stmt):
        """True when control can never fall through past *stmt*."""
        if isinstance(stmt, (ast.Return, ast.Raise, ast.Break, ast.Continue)):
            return True
        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assert):
            value = _literal(stmt.test)
            if value is not _MISSING and not value:
                return True  # assert False always raises
            return False
        if self._is_sys_exit_call(stmt):
            return True
        if isinstance(stmt, ast.If):
            return (
                bool(stmt.orelse)
                and self._block_terminates(stmt.body)
                and self._block_terminates(stmt.orelse)
            )
        if isinstance(stmt, (ast.Try, getattr(ast, "TryStar", ast.Try))):
            # The try terminates when its body terminates and every
            # handler terminates too (a handler that falls through
            # would let control continue after the try).
            if not self._block_terminates(stmt.body):
                return False
            return all(
                self._block_terminates(handler.body)
                for handler in stmt.handlers
            )
        if isinstance(stmt, ast.While):
            test = _literal(stmt.test)
            if test is not _MISSING and test:
                # while True: with no break in its own body loops forever.
                for inner in _walk_no_nested(
                    stmt, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                           ast.Lambda, ast.For, ast.While)):
                    if isinstance(inner, ast.Break):
                        return False
                return True
            return False
        return False

    def _block_terminates(self, stmts):
        return bool(stmts) and self._stmt_terminates(stmts[-1])

    # -- block rewriting -----------------------------------------------------

    def _process_block(self, stmts):
        """Rewrite one statement list; return ``(new_stmts, changed)``."""
        changed = False
        out = []
        for index, stmt in enumerate(stmts):
            if isinstance(stmt, ast.If):
                test = _literal(stmt.test)
                if test is not _MISSING:
                    # Keep only the branch that runs.
                    kept = stmt.body if test else stmt.orelse
                    self.simplified += 1
                    changed = True
                    out.extend(kept)
                    continue
            if isinstance(stmt, ast.While):
                test = _literal(stmt.test)
                if test is not _MISSING and not test:
                    self.removed += 1
                    changed = True
                    continue
            out.append(stmt)
            if self._stmt_terminates(stmt):
                # Everything after this statement is unreachable.
                remaining = stmts[index + 1:]
                self.removed += len(remaining)
                if remaining:
                    changed = True
                break
        return out, changed

    def _prune_tree(self, root):
        """Apply _process_block to every statement list, to a fixpoint."""
        for _round in range(10):
            changed = self._prune_node(root)
            if not changed:
                break

    def _prune_node(self, node):
        changed = False
        for field, value in ast.iter_fields(node):
            if isinstance(value, list) and value and all(
                isinstance(item, ast.stmt) for item in value
            ):
                new_value, block_changed = self._process_block(value)
                if block_changed:
                    setattr(node, field, new_value)
                    changed = True
                # Recurse into the (possibly new) statements.
                for item in new_value:
                    changed = self._prune_node(item) or changed
            elif isinstance(value, ast.AST):
                changed = self._prune_node(value) or changed
        return changed


def eliminate_dead_code(source):
    """Remove dead code from *source*; return ``(new_source, stats)``."""
    eliminator = DeadCodeEliminator()
    tree = eliminator.visit(ast.parse(source))
    ast.fix_missing_locations(tree)
    stats = {"removed": eliminator.removed,
             "simplified": eliminator.simplified}
    return ast.unparse(tree), stats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demonstration programs.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

DEMO_PROGRAMS = {
    "after_return": '''
def find_first_even(numbers):
    for n in numbers:
        if n % 2 == 0:
            return n
        print("odd:", n)
    return None
    print("this can never print")
    return "also dead"

def always_raises():
    raise ValueError("boom")
    print("dead after raise")

print(find_first_even([1, 3, 4, 6]))
print(find_first_even([1, 3, 5]))
try:
    always_raises()
except ValueError as exc:
    print("caught:", exc)
''',
    "constant_branches": '''
MODE = "fast"
VERBOSE = False

def run():
    if VERBOSE:
        print("verbose on")
    else:
        print("running quietly")
    if True:
        print("always taken")
    if False:
        print("never taken")
    if 1 == 1:
        result = 42
    else:
        result = 0
    return result

print("mode:", MODE)
print("result:", run())
count = 0
while False:
    count += 100
print("count:", count)
''',
    "nested_and_try": '''
def classify(value):
    if value < 0:
        return "negative"
        print("dead in branch")
    elif value == 0:
        return "zero"
    else:
        return "positive"
    print("dead after if/else")

def guarded(n):
    try:
        if n == 0:
            raise ZeroDivisionError("zero")
        return 100 // n
        print("dead in try")
    except ZeroDivisionError:
        return -1
        print("dead in handler")
    print("after try")

print(classify(-5))
print(classify(0))
print(classify(9))
print(guarded(4))
print(guarded(0))
print(guarded(10))
''',
    "loops_and_exit": '''
import sys

def search(items, target):
    for index, item in enumerate(items):
        if item == target:
            return index
        if item is None:
            break
        print("checked", item)
    else:
        print("not found")
    return -1
    print("dead after loop return")

def loop_forever_guard(flag):
    while True:
        if flag:
            return "done"
        flag = True
    print("dead after infinite loop")

print(search(["a", "b", "c"], "b"))
print(search(["a", "b"], "z"))
print(loop_forever_guard(False))
''',
    "sys_exit_aware": '''
import sys

def maybe_quit(stop):
    if stop:
        sys.exit(3)
        print("dead after sys.exit")
    print("continuing")
    return "ok"

print(maybe_quit(False))
''',
}


def demo():
    """Run every demo program through the eliminator and report."""
    print("=" * 70)
    print("Dead-code elimination demo")
    print("=" * 70)
    total_removed = 0
    total_simplified = 0
    for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
        cleaned, stats = eliminate_dead_code(source)
        total_removed += stats["removed"]
        total_simplified += stats["simplified"]
        check_preserved(source, DeadCodeEliminator, label=name)
        print("\n--- %s: removed %d, simplified %d ---"
              % (name, stats["removed"], stats["simplified"]))
        print(cleaned.rstrip())
    print("\nTotal: %d statement(s) removed, %d branch(es) simplified"
          % (total_removed, total_simplified))
    print("All behaviour-preservation checks passed.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Unit tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class TestDeadCodeEliminator(unittest.TestCase):
    def _clean(self, source):
        return eliminate_dead_code(source)[0]

    def test_after_return(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f():\n    return 1\n    x = 2\n    return 3\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "def f():\n    return 1")

    def test_after_raise(self):
        folded = self._clean("def f():\n    raise ValueError()\n    x = 1\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "def f():\n    raise ValueError()")

    def test_after_break_continue(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "for i in range(3):\n    if i:\n        break\n        x = 1\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 1", folded)
        folded = self._clean(
            "for i in range(3):\n    continue\n    x = 1\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 1", folded)

    def test_if_true_false(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._clean("if True:\n    x = 1\n"), "x = 1")
        self.assertEqual(self._clean("if False:\n    x = 1\n"), "")
        folded = self._clean("if False:\n    x = 1\nelse:\n    x = 2\n")
        self.assertEqual(folded, "x = 2")

    def test_while_false(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._clean("while False:\n    x = 1\n"), "")

    def test_if_else_both_return(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f(c):\n    if c:\n        return 1\n    else:\n        return 2\n"
            "    x = 3\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 3", folded)
        self.assertIn("return 1", folded)
        self.assertIn("return 2", folded)

    def test_while_true_no_break_terminates(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f():\n    while True:\n        x = 1\n    y = 2\n")
        self.assertNotIn("y = 2", folded)

    def test_while_true_with_break_kept(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f(c):\n    while True:\n        if c:\n            break\n"
            "    y = 2\n")
        self.assertIn("y = 2", folded)

    def test_assert_false_terminates(self):
        folded = self._clean("def f():\n    assert False\n    x = 1\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 1", folded)

    def test_sys_exit_only_when_imported(self):
        with_sys = self._clean(
            "import sys\ndef f():\n    sys.exit(1)\n    x = 1\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 1", with_sys)
        without_sys = self._clean("def f():\n    sys.exit(1)\n    x = 1\n")
        self.assertIn("x = 1", without_sys)

    def test_try_body_return_kills_rest_of_try(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f():\n    try:\n        return 1\n        x = 2\n"
            "    finally:\n        print('fin')\n")
        self.assertNotIn("x = 2", folded)
        self.assertIn("print('fin')", folded)

    def test_reachable_code_kept(self):
        folded = self._clean(
            "def f(c):\n    if c:\n        return 1\n    x = 2\n    return x\n")
        self.assertIn("x = 2", folded)

    def test_preserves_runtime_values(self):
        for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
            with self.subTest(program=name):
                check_preserved(source, DeadCodeEliminator, label=name)

    def test_stats_reported(self):
        _, stats = eliminate_dead_code(
            "def f():\n    return 1\n    x = 2\nif False:\n    y = 3\n")
        self.assertEqual(stats["removed"], 1)
        self.assertEqual(stats["simplified"], 1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()
    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("Unit tests")
    print("=" * 70)
    unittest.main(argv=[sys.argv[0], "-v"], exit=True)

--- SOURCE: python_samples/transform_logging.py ---
"""Logging / instrumentation: inject entry/exit logging into functions.

This module implements a source-to-source AST transformer that wraps
every function (and method, and async function) with tracing:

* an ``[enter]`` line printed when the function starts, showing the
  function name and every argument by name and value;
* an ``[exit]`` line printed for every normal return, showing the
  returned value;
* an ``[exit]`` line at the end of a function that can fall off the end
  (implicit ``None`` return).

How it works:

* The entry log is inserted as the first statement of the body (after
  a docstring, when there is one).  It is built as a real f-string AST
  node, so argument values are rendered with ``!r`` at run time.
* Each ``return <expr>`` is rewritten to three statements::

      _logged_result = <expr>
      print(f"[exit] name -> {_logged_result!r}")
      return _logged_result

  The result variable name is chosen fresh for each function, so it can
  never collide with a variable the function already uses.  Rewriting
  a return inside a ``try`` keeps the ``finally`` behaviour intact,
  because the rewritten code still ends in a real ``return``.
* Returns inside nested functions are left to the nested function's
  own instrumentation pass -- each function is instrumented once, for
  its own returns only.
* Generator functions keep working: the entry log fires on the first
  ``next()`` and the exit log fires when the generator is exhausted
  (or returns a value), exactly like the code it instruments.

Verification is different from the other transformers in this series:
logging deliberately *adds* output, so behaviour preservation means the
original program's output, with the ``[enter]`` / ``[exit]`` lines
removed, is identical -- and that the log lines themselves are present
and correctly paired.  Run directly::

    python3 transform_logging.py
"""

import ast
import contextlib
import io
import sys
import unittest

__all__ = ["LoggingInjector", "inject_logging", "run_source",
           "check_preserved"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small shared helpers (duplicated in each transform_* file so every file
# runs standalone).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_source(source, extra_globals=None):
    """Execute *source* and return ``(stdout, namespace)``."""
    namespace = {} if extra_globals is None else dict(extra_globals)
    buffer = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buffer):
        exec(compile(source, "<demo>", "exec"), namespace)
    return buffer.getvalue(), namespace


LOG_PREFIXES = ("[enter] ", "[exit] ")


def strip_log_lines(output):
    """Remove instrumentation lines from *output*."""
    kept = []
    for line in output.splitlines():
        if any(line.startswith(prefix) for prefix in LOG_PREFIXES):
            continue
        kept.append(line)
    return "\n".join(kept) + ("\n" if kept else "")


def check_preserved(source, transformer, label=""):
    """Transform *source* and assert identical behaviour modulo log lines.

    Returns ``(new_source, original_output, new_output)``.  Raises
    ``AssertionError`` when the non-log output differs, when the
    transformed source does not compile, or when no log lines appear.
    """
    original_out, _ = run_source(source)
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    new_tree = transformer().visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(new_tree)
    new_source = ast.unparse(new_tree)
    compile(new_source, "<transformed>", "exec")
    new_out, _ = run_source(new_source)
    stripped = strip_log_lines(new_out)
    assert stripped == original_out, (
        "behaviour changed%s:\noriginal: %r\ntransformed (stripped): %r\n%s"
        % (" for " + label if label else "", original_out, stripped, new_source)
    )
    log_lines = [line for line in new_out.splitlines()
                 if line.startswith(LOG_PREFIXES)]
    assert log_lines, "no log lines were produced for " + (label or "program")
    return new_source, original_out, new_out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The transformer.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _fstring_print(parts):
    """Build ``print(f"...")`` from *parts*: strings or (name, convert).

    Each tuple element ``(name, convert)`` becomes a FormattedValue that
    renders the variable *name* at run time; ``convert`` is ``"r"`` for
    ``!r`` or ``None`` for plain interpolation.
    """
    values = []
    for part in parts:
        if isinstance(part, str):
            values.append(ast.Constant(value=part))
        else:
            name, convert = part
            values.append(ast.FormattedValue(
                value=ast.Name(id=name, ctx=ast.Load()),
                conversion=114 if convert == "r" else -1,
                format_spec=None,
            ))
    joined = ast.JoinedStr(values=values)
    return ast.Expr(value=ast.Call(
        func=ast.Name(id="print", ctx=ast.Load()),
        args=[joined],
        keywords=[],
    ))


def _assigned_names(stmts):
    """Names assigned anywhere in *stmts*, not descending into nested defs."""
    names = set()
    stack = list(stmts)
    while stack:
        node = stack.pop()
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                             ast.ClassDef, ast.Lambda)):
            continue
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Store):
            names.add(node.id)
        if isinstance(node, ast.ExceptHandler) and node.name:
            names.add(node.name)
        if isinstance(node, ast.arg):
            names.add(node.arg)
        stack.extend(ast.iter_child_nodes(node))
    return names


def _all_arg_names(node):
    """Argument names of a function definition, in declaration order."""
    args = node.args
    names = [a.arg for a in args.posonlyargs]
    names += [a.arg for a in args.args]
    if args.vararg:
        names.append(args.vararg.arg)
    names += [a.arg for a in args.kwonlyargs]
    if args.kwarg:
        names.append(args.kwarg.arg)
    return names


def _block_terminates(stmts):
    """Conservative check: does this block always end in return/raise?"""
    if not stmts:
        return False
    last = stmts[-1]
    if isinstance(last, (ast.Return, ast.Raise)):
        return True
    if isinstance(last, ast.If):
        return (bool(last.orelse) and _block_terminates(last.body)
                and _block_terminates(last.orelse))
    return False


class LoggingInjector(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Inject entry/exit logging into every function definition."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.instrumented = 0   # functions instrumented
        self.returns_wrapped = 0

    # -- per-function instrumentation -------------------------------------

    def _fresh_result_name(self, taken):
        base = "_logged_result"
        candidate = base
        counter = 1
        while candidate in taken:
            counter += 1
            candidate = "%s_%d" % (base, counter)
        return candidate

    def _entry_log(self, name, arg_names):
        parts = ["[enter] %s(" % name]
        for index, arg in enumerate(arg_names):
            if index:
                parts.append(", ")
            parts.append(arg + "=")
            parts.append((arg, "r"))
        parts.append(")")
        return _fstring_print(parts)

    def _exit_log(self, name, result_name):
        return _fstring_print(["[exit] %s -> " % name, (result_name, "r")])

    def _rewrite_returns(self, stmts, name, result_name):
        """Replace returns in *stmts* with log-and-return triples.

        Recurses into nested control structures but never into nested
        function / class definitions or lambdas.
        """
        rewritten = []
        for stmt in stmts:
            if isinstance(stmt, ast.Return):
                value = stmt.value if stmt.value is not None else ast.Constant(value=None)
                assign = ast.Assign(
                    targets=[ast.Name(id=result_name, ctx=ast.Store())],
                    value=value,
                )
                ast.copy_location(assign, stmt)
                log = self._exit_log(name, result_name)
                ast.copy_location(log, stmt)
                ret = ast.Return(
                    value=ast.Name(id=result_name, ctx=ast.Load()))
                ast.copy_location(ret, stmt)
                self.returns_wrapped += 1
                rewritten.extend([assign, log, ret])
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                                   ast.ClassDef, ast.Lambda)):
                rewritten.append(stmt)  # handled by its own visit
            elif isinstance(stmt, ast.If):
                stmt.body = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.body, name, result_name)
                stmt.orelse = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.orelse, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.For, ast.AsyncFor)):
                stmt.body = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.body, name, result_name)
                stmt.orelse = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.orelse, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, ast.While):
                stmt.body = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.body, name, result_name)
                stmt.orelse = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.orelse, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.With, ast.AsyncWith)):
                stmt.body = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.body, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.Try, getattr(ast, "TryStar", ast.Try))):
                stmt.body = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.body, name, result_name)
                stmt.orelse = self._rewrite_returns(stmt.orelse, name, result_name)
                for handler in stmt.handlers:
                    handler.body = self._rewrite_returns(
                        handler.body, name, result_name)
                stmt.finalbody = self._rewrite_returns(
                    stmt.finalbody, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, ast.Match):
                for case in stmt.cases:
                    case.body = self._rewrite_returns(case.body, name, result_name)
                rewritten.append(stmt)
            else:
                rewritten.append(stmt)
        return rewritten

    def _instrument(self, node):
        arg_names = _all_arg_names(node)
        taken = _assigned_names(node.body) | set(arg_names)
        result_name = self._fresh_result_name(taken)
        # Rewrite the returns first, then add the entry log.
        node.body = self._rewrite_returns(node.body, node.name, result_name)
        # If the function can fall off the end, log the implicit None.
        if not _block_terminates(node.body):
            node.body.append(self._exit_log(node.name, result_name))
            # The exit log above reads the result variable before it is
            # ever assigned on this path, so bind it to None first.
            node.body.insert(
                len(node.body) - 1,
                ast.Assign(
                    targets=[ast.Name(id=result_name, ctx=ast.Store())],
                    value=ast.Constant(value=None),
                ),
            )
        entry = self._entry_log(node.name, arg_names)
        insert_at = 0
        if (node.body and isinstance(node.body[0], ast.Expr)
                and isinstance(node.body[0].value, ast.Constant)
                and isinstance(node.body[0].value.value, str)):
            insert_at = 1  # keep the docstring first
        node.body.insert(insert_at, entry)
        self.instrumented += 1
        return node

    # -- NodeTransformer hooks ---------------------------------------------

    def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)  # instrument nested functions first
        return self._instrument(node)

    def visit_AsyncFunctionDef(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)
        return self._instrument(node)


def inject_logging(source):
    """Inject logging into *source*; return ``(new_source, stats)``."""
    injector = LoggingInjector()
    tree = injector.visit(ast.parse(source))
    ast.fix_missing_locations(tree)
    stats = {"instrumented": injector.instrumented,
             "returns_wrapped": injector.returns_wrapped}
    return ast.unparse(tree), stats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demonstration programs.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

DEMO_PROGRAMS = {
    "plain_functions": '''
def add(a, b):
    """Add two numbers."""
    return a + b

def greet(name, punctuation="!"):
    message = "Hello, " + name + punctuation
    return message

def no_return(x):
    print("side effect:", x * 2)

print(add(3, 4))
print(greet("Ada"))
print(greet("Grace", "?"))
no_return(21)
print("done")
''',
    "recursion_and_branches": '''
def factorial(n):
    if n <= 1:
        return 1
    return n * factorial(n - 1)

def classify(score):
    if score >= 90:
        return "A"
    elif score >= 70:
        return "B"
    else:
        return "C"

print(factorial(5))
print(classify(95))
print(classify(75))
print(classify(40))
''',
    "methods_and_try": '''
class Counter:
    def __init__(self, start=0):
        self.value = start

    def increment(self, step=1):
        self.value += step
        return self.value

    def divide(self, divisor):
        try:
            return 100 // divisor
        except ZeroDivisionError:
            return 0
        finally:
            print("cleanup ran")

counter = Counter(10)
print(counter.increment())
print(counter.increment(5))
print(counter.divide(4))
print(counter.divide(0))
''',
    "generator_and_loop": '''
def squares(limit):
    for i in range(limit):
        yield i * i

def total(numbers):
    running = 0
    for n in numbers:
        running += n
    return running

print(list(squares(4)))
print(total([1, 2, 3, 4]))
''',
}


def demo():
    """Run every demo program through the injector and report."""
    print("=" * 70)
    print("Logging / instrumentation demo")
    print("=" * 70)
    for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
        instrumented, stats = inject_logging(source)
        _, original_out, new_out = check_preserved(
            source, LoggingInjector, label=name)
        log_lines = [line for line in new_out.splitlines()
                     if line.startswith(LOG_PREFIXES)]
        print("\n--- %s: %d function(s), %d return(s) wrapped, "
              "%d log line(s) ---"
              % (name, stats["instrumented"], stats["returns_wrapped"],
                 len(log_lines)))
        print(instrumented.rstrip())
        print("... log output ...")
        print(new_out.rstrip())
    print("\nAll behaviour-preservation checks passed "
          "(original output identical once log lines are removed).")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Unit tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class TestLoggingInjector(unittest.TestCase):
    def _inject(self, source):
        return inject_logging(source)[0]

    def test_entry_and_exit_logged(self):
        source = "def f(x):\n    return x + 1\nprint(f(41))\n"
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[enter] f(x=41)", new_out)
        self.assertIn("[exit] f -> 42", new_out)
        self.assertIn("42", strip_log_lines(new_out))

    def test_docstring_kept_first(self):
        folded = self._inject(
            'def f():\n    """Doc."""\n    return 1\n')
        lines = folded.splitlines()
        self.assertEqual(lines[1].strip(), '"""Doc."""')
        self.assertIn("[enter]", lines[2])

    def test_fall_off_end_logs_none(self):
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(
            "def f(x):\n    print('body', x)\nprint(f(3))\n",
            LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[exit] f -> None", new_out)

    def test_result_variable_collision_avoided(self):
        source = (
            "def f():\n"
            "    _logged_result = 'user value'\n"
            "    return _logged_result\n"
            "print(f())\n"
        )
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[exit] f -> 'user value'", new_out)

    def test_recursive_function_logged_each_call(self):
        source = (
            "def fib(n):\n"
            "    if n < 2:\n"
            "        return n\n"
            "    return fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2)\n"
            "print(fib(4))\n"
        )
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        enters = [l for l in new_out.splitlines() if l.startswith("[enter] fib")]
        self.assertEqual(len(enters), 9)  # fib(4) makes 9 calls
        self.assertIn("3", strip_log_lines(new_out))

    def test_return_inside_try_finally(self):
        source = (
            "def f(n):\n"
            "    try:\n"
            "        return 10 // n\n"
            "    finally:\n"
            "        print('finally')\n"
            "print(f(2))\n"
            "print(f(0) if False else 'skip')\n"
        )
        _, original_out, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("finally", new_out)
        self.assertEqual(strip_log_lines(new_out), original_out)

    def test_generator_still_works(self):
        source = (
            "def gen(n):\n"
            "    for i in range(n):\n"
            "        yield i\n"
            "print(list(gen(3)))\n"
        )
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[0, 1, 2]", strip_log_lines(new_out))

    def test_methods_instrumented(self):
        source = (
            "class C:\n"
            "    def m(self, x):\n"
            "        return x * 2\n"
            "print(C().m(21))\n"
        )
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[enter] m(", new_out)
        self.assertIn("[exit] m -> 42", new_out)

    def test_async_function(self):
        source = (
            "import asyncio\n"
            "async def fetch(x):\n"
            "    return x + 1\n"
            "print(asyncio.run(fetch(41)))\n"
        )
        _, _, new_out = check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector)
        self.assertIn("[enter] fetch(x=41)", new_out)
        self.assertIn("[exit] fetch -> 42", new_out)

    def test_preserves_runtime_values(self):
        for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
            with self.subTest(program=name):
                check_preserved(source, LoggingInjector, label=name)

    def test_stats_reported(self):
        _, stats = inject_logging(
            "def f():\n    return 1\ndef g():\n    return 2\n")
        self.assertEqual(stats["instrumented"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(stats["returns_wrapped"], 2)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()
    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("Unit tests")
    print("=" * 70)
    unittest.main(argv=[sys.argv[0], "-v"], exit=True)

--- SOURCE: python_samples/transform_loops.py ---
"""Loop optimisation: hoist invariants, reduce strength, enumerate.

This module implements a source-to-source AST transformer that makes
loops cheaper without changing what they compute:

1. Loop-invariant code motion (LICM).  An assignment inside a loop
   whose right-hand side is *pure* (no calls other than ``len`` /
   ``abs`` on invariant values, no attribute access, no I/O) and
   *loop-invariant* (it only reads names that the loop never assigns)
   is moved to just before the loop.  The assignment is hoisted only
   when its target is never read after the loop -- on a zero-trip
   loop the hoisted assignment would run once where the original ran
   zero times, so any later read would see a different value -- and
   only when the target is not read earlier in the same iteration
   than the assignment, nor by the loop's own header.
2. Strength reduction.  ``x ** 2`` becomes ``x * x`` and ``x * 2``
   becomes ``x + x`` (and symmetrically ``2 * x``).  Multiplication
   is replaced by the cheaper addition; the results are identical for
   plain numeric and sequence values, which is the documented scope of
   this transformation -- it is not applied to expressions whose
   operands could be user-defined objects with exotic ``__pow__`` /
   ``__mul__`` behaviour, because the transformer only rewrites
   expressions built from names and literals.
3. ``range(len(xs))`` becomes ``enumerate(xs)``.  This rewrite is
   applied only when the loop index is used *exclusively* as the index
   in subscripts ``xs[i]`` -- every load of the index in the body must
   be in exactly that position -- and ``xs`` is never assigned inside
   the loop.  Each ``xs[i]`` is then replaced by the enumerated value.

Safety rules, shared by all three rewrites:

* A loop is only optimised when every name the moved/rewritten code
  depends on is provably untouched by the loop.
* Pure means no observable side effects: no function calls except the
  whitelisted ``len`` / ``abs``, no attribute access (descriptors can
  run code), no subscripts of mutable values that the loop might be
  changing -- subscripts are allowed only on names the loop never
  assigns.
* Hoisting never runs for a target that the loop body reads before
  writing in the same iteration.

Every demonstration program is executed before and after optimisation
and the two runs must produce byte-identical output.  Run directly::

    python3 transform_loops.py
"""

import ast
import contextlib
import io
import sys
import unittest

__all__ = ["LoopOptimizer", "optimise_loops", "run_source",
           "check_preserved"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small shared helpers (duplicated in each transform_* file so every file
# runs standalone).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_source(source, extra_globals=None):
    """Execute *source* and return ``(stdout, namespace)``."""
    namespace = {} if extra_globals is None else dict(extra_globals)
    buffer = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buffer):
        exec(compile(source, "<demo>", "exec"), namespace)
    return buffer.getvalue(), namespace


def check_preserved(source, transformer, label=""):
    """Transform *source* and assert identical observable behaviour.

    Returns ``(new_source, original_output, new_output)``.
    """
    original_out, _ = run_source(source)
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    new_tree = transformer().visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(new_tree)
    new_source = ast.unparse(new_tree)
    compile(new_source, "<transformed>", "exec")
    new_out, _ = run_source(new_source)
    assert original_out == new_out, (
        "behaviour changed%s:\noriginal: %r\ntransformed: %r\n%s"
        % (" for " + label if label else "", original_out, new_out, new_source)
    )
    return new_source, original_out, new_out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Name analysis helpers.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _assigned_in(node):
    """Names assigned anywhere under *node* (loop targets included).

    Comprehension variables are excluded: in Python 3 they live in the
    comprehension's own scope and never leak into the enclosing one.
    """
    assigned = set()
    stack = [node]
    while stack:
        current = stack.pop()
        if isinstance(current, (ast.comprehension,)):
            # Only the iter / ifs belong to the enclosing scope.
            stack.extend(current.ifs)
            stack.append(current.iter)
            continue
        if isinstance(current, ast.Name) and isinstance(current.ctx, ast.Store):
            assigned.add(current.id)
        if isinstance(current, ast.ExceptHandler) and current.name:
            assigned.add(current.name)
        if isinstance(current, ast.arg):
            assigned.add(current.arg)
        stack.extend(ast.iter_child_nodes(current))
    return assigned


def _loaded_names(node):
    """Names read (Load context) anywhere under *node*."""
    return {
        child.id for child in ast.walk(node)
        if isinstance(child, ast.Name) and isinstance(child.ctx, ast.Load)
    }


def _loaded_names_in(stmts):
    """Names read anywhere in a list of statements."""
    names = set()
    for stmt in stmts:
        names |= _loaded_names(stmt)
    return names


def _is_pure(node, invariant):
    """True when *node* is side-effect free and reads only *invariant* names.

    *invariant* is the set of names known not to change during the loop.
    """
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return True
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        return isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load) and node.id in invariant
    if isinstance(node, (ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.BoolOp)):
        return all(_is_pure(child, invariant) for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node))
    if isinstance(node, ast.IfExp):
        return all(_is_pure(child, invariant) for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Subscript):
        # Subscripting a name the loop never assigns is pure; the
        # container itself is not mutated by a pure read.
        return _is_pure(node.value, invariant) and _is_pure(node.slice, invariant)
    if isinstance(node, (ast.List, ast.Tuple, ast.Set)):
        return all(_is_pure(elt, invariant) for elt in node.elts)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Dict):
        return all(
            _is_pure(key, invariant) and _is_pure(value, invariant)
            for key, value in zip(node.keys, node.values)
            if key is not None
        )
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        # Only len() / abs() of pure arguments are whitelisted.
        if (isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) and node.func.id in ("len", "abs")
                and not node.keywords
                and all(_is_pure(arg, invariant) for arg in node.args)):
            return True
        return False
    return False


def _sequence_mutated(stmts, seq):
    """True when the loop body may mutate the sequence named *seq*.

    Covers item assignment (``seq[i] = ...``), any method call on the
    sequence (``seq.append(...)``, ``seq.sort()``, ...), and passing
    the sequence to another function, which might mutate it.  All of
    these would make ``enumerate`` iterate differently from the
    ``range(len(...))`` snapshot taken before the loop.
    """
    for stmt in stmts:
        for child in ast.walk(stmt):
            if isinstance(child, ast.Subscript):
                if (isinstance(child.value, ast.Name)
                        and child.value.id == seq
                        and not isinstance(child.ctx, ast.Load)):
                    return True
            if isinstance(child, ast.Call):
                func = child.func
                if (isinstance(func, ast.Attribute)
                        and isinstance(func.value, ast.Name)
                        and func.value.id == seq):
                    return True
                for arg in child.args:
                    if isinstance(arg, ast.Name) and arg.id == seq:
                        return True
                for keyword in child.keywords:
                    if (isinstance(keyword.value, ast.Name)
                            and keyword.value.id == seq):
                        return True
    return False


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The transformer.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class LoopOptimizer(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Hoist loop invariants, reduce strength, rewrite range(len())."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.hoisted = 0
        self.reduced = 0
        self.enumerated = 0

    # -- strength reduction --------------------------------------------------

    def visit_BinOp(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        # x ** 2  ->  x * x
        if (isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow)
                and isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant)
                and node.right.value == 2
                and not isinstance(node.left, ast.Constant)):
            self.reduced += 1
            return ast.copy_location(
                ast.BinOp(left=node.left, op=ast.Mult(),
                          right=_copy_node(node.left)),
                node)
        # x * 2  ->  x + x   (and 2 * x -> x + x)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Mult):
            for const_side, other_side in (
                    (node.right, node.left), (node.left, node.right)):
                if (isinstance(const_side, ast.Constant)
                        and const_side.value == 2
                        and not isinstance(other_side, ast.Constant)):
                    self.reduced += 1
                    return ast.copy_location(
                        ast.BinOp(left=other_side, op=ast.Add(),
                                  right=_copy_node(other_side)),
                        node)
        return node

    # -- range(len(xs)) -> enumerate(xs) --------------------------------------

    def _try_enumerate(self, node):
        """Rewrite ``for i in range(len(xs)):`` when provably safe."""
        if not isinstance(node, ast.For):
            return node
        if not (isinstance(node.target, ast.Name)
                and isinstance(node.iter, ast.Call)
                and isinstance(node.iter.func, ast.Name)
                and node.iter.func.id == "range"
                and len(node.iter.args) == 1
                and isinstance(node.iter.args[0], ast.Call)
                and isinstance(node.iter.args[0].func, ast.Name)
                and node.iter.args[0].func.id == "len"
                and len(node.iter.args[0].args) == 1
                and isinstance(node.iter.args[0].args[0], ast.Name)):
            return node
        index = node.target.id
        seq = node.iter.args[0].args[0].id
        assigned = _assigned_in(node)
        if index in assigned and index != node.target.id:
            return node
        if seq in assigned:
            return node  # the sequence is rebound inside the loop
        if _sequence_mutated(node.body, seq):
            return node  # the sequence is mutated inside the loop
        # Every load of the index must be exactly `xs[i]`.
        parent = {}
        for child in ast.walk(node):
            for grandchild in ast.iter_child_nodes(child):
                parent[grandchild] = child
        uses_ok = True
        item_uses = 0
        for child in ast.walk(node):
            if (isinstance(child, ast.Name) and child.id == index
                    and isinstance(child.ctx, ast.Load)):
                holder = parent.get(child)
                if (isinstance(holder, ast.Subscript)
                        and isinstance(holder.value, ast.Name)
                        and holder.value.id == seq
                        and holder.slice is child):
                    item_uses += 1
                else:
                    uses_ok = False
                    break
        if not uses_ok or item_uses == 0:
            return node
        # Pick a fresh name for the enumerated value.
        taken = _assigned_in(node) | _loaded_names(node) | {index, seq}
        value_name = "_item"
        counter = 1
        while value_name in taken:
            counter += 1
            value_name = "_item_%d" % counter
        # Replace each xs[i] with the new value name, via a small
        # NodeTransformer so every occurrence is swapped for a fresh
        # Name node in the correct position.
        replacer = _EnumerateReplacer(seq, index, value_name)
        replacer.visit(node)
        node.target = ast.Tuple(
            elts=[ast.Name(id=index, ctx=ast.Store()),
                  ast.Name(id=value_name, ctx=ast.Store())],
            ctx=ast.Store(),
        )
        node.iter = ast.Call(
            func=ast.Name(id="enumerate", ctx=ast.Load()),
            args=[ast.Name(id=seq, ctx=ast.Load())],
            keywords=[],
        )
        ast.fix_missing_locations(node)
        self.enumerated += 1
        return node

    # -- loop-invariant code motion -------------------------------------------

    def _hoist_from_loop(self, loop, read_after):
        """Move invariant pure assignments out of *loop*; return hoisted list.

        *read_after* is the set of names loaded by code that runs after
        this loop (later statements in the block, the loop's ``else``,
        and code after the enclosing block).  An assignment is hoisted
        only when its target is never read after the loop: on a
        zero-trip loop the assignment would otherwise run once where
        the original ran zero times, which would change the value a
        later read sees.  The target must also not be read by the
        loop's own header (``for`` iterable / ``while`` test) or
        earlier in the same iteration than the assignment.
        """
        assigned = _assigned_in(loop)
        if isinstance(loop, ast.For):
            header_reads = _loaded_names(loop.iter)
        else:
            header_reads = _loaded_names(loop.test)
        # Count how many times each name is assigned inside the loop.
        # Hoisting is only sound when the candidate assignment is the
        # *only* assignment of its target in the loop: a second
        # assignment (for example an accumulator updated by an inner
        # loop) means the reset must run on every iteration.
        assign_counts = {}
        for child in ast.walk(loop):
            if isinstance(child, ast.Name) and isinstance(child.ctx, ast.Store):
                assign_counts[child.id] = assign_counts.get(child.id, 0) + 1
        hoisted = []
        kept_body = []
        read_before = set()
        for stmt in loop.body:
            if (isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign)
                    and len(stmt.targets) == 1
                    and isinstance(stmt.targets[0], ast.Name)):
                target = stmt.targets[0].id
                loaded = _loaded_names(stmt.value)
                invariant_names = {
                    name for name in loaded if name not in assigned
                }
                if (target not in read_after
                        and target not in read_before
                        and target not in header_reads
                        and assign_counts.get(target, 0) == 1
                        and loaded <= invariant_names
                        and _is_pure(stmt.value, invariant_names)
                        and target not in loaded):
                    hoisted.append(stmt)
                    self.hoisted += 1
                    continue
            kept_body.append(stmt)
            read_before |= _loaded_names(stmt)
        loop.body = kept_body
        return hoisted

    # -- statement-list handling ----------------------------------------------

    def _optimise_block(self, stmts, read_after=frozenset()):
        """Optimise the loops in one statement list, hoisting before them.

        *read_after* is the set of names loaded by code that runs after
        this block finishes; it is threaded down into nested blocks so
        a hoist inside an ``if`` branch still respects reads that
        happen after the whole ``if``.
        """
        out = []
        for index, stmt in enumerate(stmts):
            after = set(read_after)
            for later in stmts[index + 1:]:
                after |= _loaded_names(later)
            if isinstance(stmt, (ast.For, ast.While)):
                loop_after = after | _loaded_names_in(
                    getattr(stmt, "orelse", []))
                hoisted = self._hoist_from_loop(stmt, loop_after)
                stmt.body = self._optimise_block(stmt.body, loop_after)
                stmt.orelse = self._optimise_block(stmt.orelse, after)
                out.extend(hoisted)
                out.append(self._try_enumerate(stmt)
                           if isinstance(stmt, ast.For) else stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, ast.If):
                stmt.body = self._optimise_block(stmt.body, after)
                stmt.orelse = self._optimise_block(stmt.orelse, after)
                out.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.Try, getattr(ast, "TryStar", ast.Try))):
                stmt.body = self._optimise_block(stmt.body, after)
                stmt.orelse = self._optimise_block(stmt.orelse, after)
                for handler in stmt.handlers:
                    handler.body = self._optimise_block(handler.body, after)
                stmt.finalbody = self._optimise_block(stmt.finalbody, after)
                out.append(stmt)
            elif isinstance(stmt, (ast.With, ast.AsyncWith)):
                stmt.body = self._optimise_block(stmt.body, after)
                out.append(stmt)
            else:
                out.append(stmt)
        return out

    def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)
        node.body = self._optimise_block(node.body)
        return node

    def visit_AsyncFunctionDef(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)
        node.body = self._optimise_block(node.body)
        return node

    def visit_Module(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)
        node.body = self._optimise_block(node.body)
        return node

    def visit_ClassDef(self, node):
        self.generic_visit(node)
        node.body = self._optimise_block(node.body)
        return node


class _EnumerateReplacer(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Replace every ``seq[index]`` subscript with a plain name."""

    def __init__(self, seq, index, value_name):
        super().__init__()
        self.seq = seq
        self.index = index
        self.value_name = value_name

    def visit_Subscript(self, node):
        node = self.generic_visit(node)
        if (isinstance(node.value, ast.Name)
                and node.value.id == self.seq
                and isinstance(node.slice, ast.Name)
                and node.slice.id == self.index):
            return ast.copy_location(
                ast.Name(id=self.value_name, ctx=ast.Load()), node)
        return node


def _copy_node(node):
    """Deep-copy an AST node (ast.copy_location is not a copy)."""
    import copy
    return copy.deepcopy(node)


def optimise_loops(source):
    """Optimise the loops in *source*; return ``(new_source, stats)``."""
    optimizer = LoopOptimizer()
    tree = optimizer.visit(ast.parse(source))
    ast.fix_missing_locations(tree)
    stats = {"hoisted": optimizer.hoisted,
             "reduced": optimizer.reduced,
             "enumerated": optimizer.enumerated}
    return ast.unparse(tree), stats


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demonstration programs.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

DEMO_PROGRAMS = {
    "invariant_len_and_squares": '''
def total_lengths(words):
    """Sum of squares of word lengths, with len() hoisted out."""
    total = 0
    for word in words:
        n = len(words)
        total += len(word) ** 2 + n
    return total

def scaled(values, factor):
    out = []
    for value in values:
        out.append(value * 2 + factor)
    return out

print(total_lengths(["one", "three", "seven"]))
print(scaled([1, 2, 3, 4], 10))
''',
    "range_len_rewrite": '''
def first_letters(words):
    found = []
    for i in range(len(words)):
        found.append((i, words[i][:1]))
    return found

def with_index(items):
    total = 0
    for i in range(len(items)):
        total += i + len(items[i])
    return total

print(first_letters(["apple", "banana", "cherry"]))
print(with_index(["aa", "bbb", "cccc"]))
''',
    "strength_reduction": '''
def squares(numbers):
    return [n ** 2 for n in numbers]

def doubled(numbers):
    result = []
    for n in numbers:
        result.append(n * 2)
        result.append(2 * n)
    return result

def areas(radii):
    return [3.14 * r ** 2 for r in radii]

print(squares([1, 2, 3, 4, 5]))
print(doubled([1, 2, 3]))
print([round(a, 2) for a in areas([1, 2, 3])])
''',
    "nested_loops": '''
def matrix_total(rows):
    grand = 0
    for row in rows:
        width = len(row)
        row_sum = 0
        for cell in row:
            row_sum += cell * 2
        grand += row_sum + width
    return grand

def search(grid, target):
    hits = []
    for r in range(len(grid)):
        for c in range(len(grid[r])):
            if grid[r][c] == target:
                hits.append((r, c))
    return hits

print(matrix_total([[1, 2], [3, 4, 5], [6]]))
print(search([[1, 2], [3, 1], [1, 1]], 1))
''',
}


def demo():
    """Run every demo program through the optimiser and report."""
    print("=" * 70)
    print("Loop optimisation demo")
    print("=" * 70)
    for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
        optimised, stats = optimise_loops(source)
        check_preserved(source, LoopOptimizer, label=name)
        print("\n--- %s: hoisted %d, reduced %d, enumerated %d ---"
              % (name, stats["hoisted"], stats["reduced"],
                 stats["enumerated"]))
        print(optimised.rstrip())
    print("\nAll behaviour-preservation checks passed.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Unit tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class TestLoopOptimizer(unittest.TestCase):
    def _opt(self, source):
        return optimise_loops(source)[0]

    def test_square_reduction(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._opt("x = n ** 2\n"), "x = n * n")

    def test_double_reduction(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._opt("x = n * 2\n"), "x = n + n")
        self.assertEqual(self._opt("x = 2 * n\n"), "x = n + n")

    def test_no_reduction_for_constants(self):
        # 3 ** 2 is the constant folder's job, not this transformer's.
        self.assertEqual(self._opt("x = 3 ** 2\n"), "x = 3 ** 2")

    def test_no_reduction_for_other_powers(self):
        self.assertEqual(self._opt("x = n ** 3\n"), "x = n ** 3")

    def test_hoist_len(self):
        source = (
            "def f(items):\n"
            "    n = 0\n"
            "    for item in items:\n"
            "        n = len(items)\n"
            "        print(n)\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        # The len() assignment must have moved before the loop.
        lines = folded.splitlines()
        len_line = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if "len(items)" in l)
        for_line = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if l.strip().startswith("for "))
        self.assertLess(len_line, for_line)

    def test_hoist_when_target_only_used_inside_loop(self):
        source = (
            "def f(items):\n"
            "    for item in items:\n"
            "        n = len(items)\n"
            "        print(n)\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        lines = folded.splitlines()
        len_line = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if "len(items)" in l)
        for_line = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if l.strip().startswith("for "))
        self.assertLess(len_line, for_line)

    def test_no_hoist_when_target_read_after_loop(self):
        # A zero-trip loop must not change the value a later read sees.
        source = (
            "def f(items):\n"
            "    n = 0\n"
            "    for item in items:\n"
            "        n = len(items)\n"
            "        print(n)\n"
            "    return n\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        body = folded.split("for item in items:")[1]
        self.assertIn("n = len(items)", body)

    def test_no_hoist_variant_expression(self):
        source = (
            "def f(items):\n"
            "    n = 0\n"
            "    for item in items:\n"
            "        n = len(item)\n"
            "        print(n)\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        body = folded.split("for item in items:")[1]
        self.assertIn("n = len(item)", body)

    def test_no_hoist_impure_call(self):
        source = (
            "def f(items):\n"
            "    n = 0\n"
            "    for item in items:\n"
            "        n = compute(items)\n"
            "        print(n)\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        body = folded.split("for item in items:")[1]
        self.assertIn("n = compute(items)", body)

    def test_enumerate_rewrite(self):
        source = (
            "def f(words):\n"
            "    out = []\n"
            "    for i in range(len(words)):\n"
            "        out.append(words[i])\n"
            "    return out\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        self.assertIn("for i, _item in enumerate(words):", folded)
        self.assertIn("out.append(_item)", folded)

    def test_no_enumerate_when_index_used_otherwise(self):
        source = (
            "def f(words):\n"
            "    out = []\n"
            "    for i in range(len(words)):\n"
            "        out.append(i * 2)\n"
            "        out.append(words[i])\n"
            "    return out\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        self.assertIn("range(len(words))", folded)

    def test_no_enumerate_when_sequence_mutated(self):
        source = (
            "def f(words):\n"
            "    for i in range(len(words)):\n"
            "        words.append(words[i])\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        self.assertIn("range(len(words))", folded)

    def test_no_hoist_accumulator_reset(self):
        # row_sum is reset each iteration and updated by an inner loop:
        # hoisting the reset would silently change the result.
        source = (
            "def f(rows):\n"
            "    grand = 0\n"
            "    for row in rows:\n"
            "        row_sum = 0\n"
            "        for cell in row:\n"
            "            row_sum += cell\n"
            "        grand += row_sum\n"
            "    return grand\n"
        )
        folded = self._opt(source)
        body = folded.split("for row in rows:")[1]
        self.assertIn("row_sum = 0", body)

    def test_preserves_runtime_values(self):
        for name, source in DEMO_PROGRAMS.items():
            with self.subTest(program=name):
                check_preserved(source, LoopOptimizer, label=name)

    def test_stats_reported(self):
        _, stats = optimise_loops(
            "def f(xs):\n"
            "    n = 0\n"
            "    for x in xs:\n"
            "        n = len(xs)\n"
            "        print(x ** 2)\n")
        self.assertEqual(stats["hoisted"], 1)
        self.assertEqual(stats["reduced"], 1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()
    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("Unit tests")
    print("=" * 70)
    unittest.main(argv=[sys.argv[0], "-v"], exit=True)

--- SOURCE: python_samples/transform_obfuscate.py ---
"""Obfuscator / minifier: rename locals, strip docstrings.

This module implements a source-to-source AST transformer that makes
code shorter and harder to read while keeping it working:

* every local variable, parameter and nested-function name inside a
  function is renamed to a short generated name (``a``, ``b``, ...,
  ``aa``, ``ab``, ...), consistently across the whole function,
  including names shared with nested functions through closures;
* module-level names are *not* renamed: they are the program's public
  surface, and renaming them would break imports and the ``__main__``
  entry point;
* docstrings are removed from modules, classes and functions;
* comments disappear automatically, because ``ast.unparse`` only
  emits code, never comments;
* the result is re-emitted with ``ast.unparse``, which also drops
  redundant parentheses and normalises whitespace.

How renaming stays correct:

* Each function, lambda and comprehension gets its own scope, and
  scopes nest exactly the way Python scopes nest.  A name is renamed
  according to the *innermost* scope that defines it, so shadowing
  works and closures resolve to the outer function's mapping.
* Class scopes are skipped when resolving names inside methods, just
  as Python does: a method cannot see class-body names as plain
  variables.
* Names declared ``global`` or ``nonlocal`` are never renamed in the
  scope that declares them.
* Attributes (``obj.attr``), imported names used at module level,
  builtins and keywords are never renamed.
* Dunder names (``__init__``, ``__str__``, ...) are never renamed:
  they are protocol, and Python calls them by name.
* One short-name generator is shared by the whole file, so no two
  scopes ever produce the same short name and a renamed local can
  never shadow a global or a free variable that another scope reads.
* Keyword arguments are rewritten to match renamed parameters, for
  direct calls to functions and classes defined in the same file
  (``greet(name="Ada")`` becomes ``greet(a="Ada")`` when ``name``
  was renamed to ``a``).  Two known limits: keyword arguments in
  *method* calls (``obj.method(x=1)``) and in calls to lambdas are
  not rewritten, because the callee cannot be resolved statically;
  and string-based lookups such as ``getattr(obj, "name")`` cannot
  be tracked by any static renamer.

Every demonstration program is executed before and after
obfuscation and the two runs must produce byte-identical output; the
demo also asserts that the obfuscated source is shorter and that the
original local names and docstrings are gone.  Run directly::

    python3 transform_obfuscate.py
"""

import ast
import contextlib
import io
import itertools
import keyword
import sys
import unittest

__all__ = ["Obfuscator", "obfuscate_source", "run_source",
           "check_preserved"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Small shared helpers (duplicated in each transform_* file so every file
# runs standalone).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_source(source, extra_globals=None):
    """Execute *source* and return ``(stdout, namespace)``."""
    namespace = {} if extra_globals is None else dict(extra_globals)
    buffer = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buffer):
        exec(compile(source, "<demo>", "exec"), namespace)
    return buffer.getvalue(), namespace


def check_preserved(source, transformer, label=""):
    """Transform *source* and assert identical observable behaviour.

    Returns ``(new_source, original_output, new_output)``.
    """
    original_out, _ = run_source(source)
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    new_tree = transformer().visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(new_tree)
    new_source = ast.unparse(new_tree)
    compile(new_source, "<transformed>", "exec")
    new_out, _ = run_source(new_source)
    assert original_out == new_out, (
        "behaviour changed%s:\noriginal: %r\ntransformed: %r\n%s"
        % (" for " + label if label else "", original_out, new_out, new_source)
    )
    return new_source, original_out, new_out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Short-name generation.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _short_names(exclude=frozenset()):
    """Yield ``a``..``z``, ``aa``..``zz``, ``aaa``..., skipping reserved names."""
    reserved = (set(dir(__builtins__)) | set(keyword.kwlist)
                | {"True", "False", "None"} | set(exclude))
    alphabet = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"
    length = 1
    while True:
        for combo in itertools.product(alphabet, repeat=length):
            name = "".join(combo)
            if name not in reserved:
                yield name
        length += 1


def _is_dunder(name):
    """True for ``__name__``: protocol names that must never be renamed."""
    return name.startswith("__") and name.endswith("__")


def _nested_callables(stmts):
    """Directly nested functions/classes: ``{name: node}``.

    Only definitions that appear as statements of *stmts* themselves,
    not ones buried inside deeper blocks.
    """
    found = {}
    for stmt in stmts:
        if isinstance(stmt, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                             ast.ClassDef)):
            found[stmt.name] = stmt
    return found


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Scope analysis: which names does one function (or lambda, or
# comprehension) define, and which does it declare global / nonlocal?
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _collect_definitions(stmts):
    """Collect ``(defined, global_names, nonlocal_names)`` for one scope.

    Walks *stmts* without descending into nested function / class /
    lambda bodies (they are scopes of their own).  Comprehensions are
    descended into only for walrus (``:=``) targets, which *do* leak
    into the enclosing scope, and for their iterables, which are
    evaluated in the enclosing scope.
    """
    defined = []
    seen = set()

    def add(name):
        if name not in seen:
            seen.add(name)
            defined.append(name)

    def walk(node):
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                             ast.ClassDef)):
            # A nested definition binds its name in *this* scope, but
            # its body is a scope of its own: record the name, do not
            # descend.
            add(node.name)
            return
        if isinstance(node, ast.Lambda):
            return  # a scope of its own; binds nothing here
        if isinstance(node, ast.comprehension):
            walk(node.iter)
            for cond in node.ifs:
                walk(cond)
            return
        if isinstance(node, ast.NamedExpr):
            add(node.target.id)
            walk(node.value)
            return
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Store):
            add(node.id)
        if isinstance(node, ast.ExceptHandler) and node.name:
            add(node.name)
        if isinstance(node, (ast.Import, ast.ImportFrom)):
            for alias in node.names:
                add(alias.asname or alias.name.split(".")[0])
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
            walk(child)

    global_names = set()
    nonlocal_names = set()
    # global / nonlocal declarations can sit in nested blocks of the
    # body, so collect them from the whole scope (but never from a
    # nested function / class / lambda, which has its own).
    stack = list(stmts)
    while stack:
        node = stack.pop()
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef,
                             ast.ClassDef, ast.Lambda)):
            continue
        if isinstance(node, ast.Global):
            global_names.update(node.names)
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Nonlocal):
            nonlocal_names.update(node.names)
        stack.extend(ast.iter_child_nodes(node))
    for stmt in stmts:
        walk(stmt)
    return defined, global_names, nonlocal_names


class _Scope:
    """One lexical scope: its name mapping and its protected names."""

    def __init__(self, mapping, global_names, nonlocal_names,
                 callables=None, is_class=False):
        self.mapping = mapping            # original name -> short name
        self.global_names = global_names  # module globals: never renamed
        self.nonlocal_names = nonlocal_names  # resolve in an outer scope
        # Nested functions/classes defined here, with their parameter
        # renamings, so call sites can rewrite keyword arguments.
        self.callables = callables or {}
        self.is_class = is_class


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The transformer.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class Obfuscator(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """Rename locals to short names and strip docstrings."""

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.scopes = []
        self.renamed = 0
        self.docstrings_removed = 0
        self.keywords_rewritten = 0
        # One shared generator for the whole file: no two scopes ever
        # produce the same short name, so a renamed local can never
        # shadow a global or a free variable another scope reads.
        self._name_gen = None
        # Precomputed parameter renamings, keyed by node id, filled in
        # by the pre-pass in visit_Module.
        self._func_param_maps = {}
        self._class_init_maps = {}
        self._module_callables = {}

    # -- pre-pass --------------------------------------------------------------

    def _precompute_callables(self, tree):
        """Assign short names to every function's parameters, in order.

        Runs once per file, before any renaming, so that call sites
        can rewrite keyword arguments even when the call textually
        precedes the definition.  Parameter names are taken from the
        same shared generator as all other renamings, so they can
        never collide with another scope's short names.
        """
        module_defined, _, _ = _collect_definitions(tree.body)
        self._name_gen = _short_names(exclude=set(module_defined))
        # Map each __init__ method to its class, so the mapping used
        # for ``ClassName(x=...)`` call sites and the mapping used for
        # the method's own parameters are one and the same object.
        init_to_class = {}
        for node in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
                for stmt in node.body:
                    if (isinstance(stmt, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef))
                            and stmt.name == "__init__"):
                        init_to_class[id(stmt)] = id(node)
        for node in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
                mapping = {}
                for param in _all_args(node):
                    if not _is_dunder(param):
                        mapping[param] = next(self._name_gen)
                self._func_param_maps[id(node)] = mapping
                if id(node) in init_to_class:
                    self._class_init_maps[init_to_class[id(node)]] = mapping
        for name, node in _nested_callables(tree.body).items():
            if id(node) in self._func_param_maps:
                self._module_callables[name] = self._func_param_maps[id(node)]
            elif id(node) in self._class_init_maps:
                self._module_callables[name] = self._class_init_maps[id(node)]

    # -- scope handling ------------------------------------------------------

    def _push_function_scope(self, node):
        defined, global_names, nonlocal_names = _collect_definitions(node.body)
        # Parameters reuse their precomputed renamings.
        mapping = dict(self._func_param_maps.get(id(node), {}))
        for name in defined:
            if (name in mapping or name in global_names
                    or name in nonlocal_names or _is_dunder(name)):
                continue
            mapping[name] = next(self._name_gen)
        callables = {}
        for name, child in _nested_callables(node.body).items():
            if id(child) in self._func_param_maps:
                callables[name] = self._func_param_maps[id(child)]
            elif id(child) in self._class_init_maps:
                callables[name] = self._class_init_maps[id(child)]
        self.scopes.append(_Scope(mapping, set(global_names),
                                  set(nonlocal_names), callables))

    def _push_comprehension_scope(self, node):
        defined = []
        seen = set()
        for gen in node.generators:
            for target_name in _target_names(gen.target):
                if target_name not in seen:
                    seen.add(target_name)
                    defined.append(target_name)
        mapping = {name: next(self._name_gen) for name in defined
                   if not _is_dunder(name)}
        self.scopes.append(_Scope(mapping, set(), set()))

    def _resolve(self, name):
        """Short name for *name* in the current scope stack, or None."""
        for index in range(len(self.scopes) - 1, -1, -1):
            scope = self.scopes[index]
            # A class scope is invisible to methods, as in Python.
            if scope.is_class and index != len(self.scopes) - 1:
                continue
            if name in scope.global_names:
                return None  # a module global: never renamed anywhere
            if name in scope.nonlocal_names:
                continue  # bound in an outer function: resolve there
            if name in scope.mapping:
                return scope.mapping[name]
        return None

    # -- docstrings ------------------------------------------------------------

    def _strip_docstring(self, body):
        if (body and isinstance(body[0], ast.Expr)
                and isinstance(body[0].value, ast.Constant)
                and isinstance(body[0].value.value, str)):
            self.docstrings_removed += 1
            return body[1:]
        return body

    # -- two-phase function handling -------------------------------------------
    #
    # Decorators, default values, annotations and the return annotation
    # are evaluated in the *enclosing* scope, so they are visited
    # before the function's own scope is pushed.  Only the parameter
    # names and the body belong to the new scope.

    def _visit_enclosing_expressions(self, node):
        for decorator in node.decorator_list:
            self.visit(decorator)
        if node.returns is not None:
            node.returns = self.visit(node.returns)
        args = node.args
        for default in args.defaults:
            self.visit(default)
        for default in args.kw_defaults:
            if default is not None:
                self.visit(default)
        for arg in (args.posonlyargs + args.args + args.kwonlyargs
                    + ([args.vararg] if args.vararg else [])
                    + ([args.kwarg] if args.kwarg else [])):
            if arg.annotation is not None:
                arg.annotation = self.visit(arg.annotation)

    def _rename_own_name(self, node):
        new_name = self._resolve(node.name)
        if new_name is not None:
            node.name = new_name

    def _rename_parameters(self, node):
        for arg in (node.args.posonlyargs + node.args.args
                    + node.args.kwonlyargs
                    + ([node.args.vararg] if node.args.vararg else [])
                    + ([node.args.kwarg] if node.args.kwarg else [])):
            new_name = self._resolve(arg.arg)
            if new_name is not None:
                arg.arg = new_name

    def _instrument_function(self, node):
        self._rename_own_name(node)
        self._visit_enclosing_expressions(node)
        self._push_function_scope(node)
        self._rename_parameters(node)
        node.body = self._strip_docstring(node.body)
        node.body = [self.visit(stmt) for stmt in node.body]
        self.scopes.pop()
        return node

    # -- NodeTransformer hooks -----------------------------------------------

    def visit_Module(self, node):
        self._precompute_callables(node)
        node.body = self._strip_docstring(node.body)
        self.generic_visit(node)
        return node

    def visit_FunctionDef(self, node):
        return self._instrument_function(node)

    def visit_AsyncFunctionDef(self, node):
        return self._instrument_function(node)

    def visit_ClassDef(self, node):
        # Bases and keywords are evaluated in the enclosing scope.
        self._rename_own_name(node)
        for base in node.bases:
            self.visit(base)
        for keyword in node.keywords:
            self.visit(keyword.value)
        # The class body is its own scope, but methods must not resolve
        # names through it -- _resolve skips non-innermost class scopes.
        # Method and nested-class names are NOT renamed: their call
        # sites are attributes (``box.area()``), which a renamer cannot
        # touch, so renaming the definition would break the call.
        # Plain class-level variables are safe to rename, because
        # references to them inside the class body are bare names.
        defined, _, _ = _collect_definitions(node.body)
        nested_defs = set(_nested_callables(node.body))
        mapping = {}
        for name in defined:
            if _is_dunder(name) or name in nested_defs:
                continue
            mapping[name] = next(self._name_gen)
        callables = {}
        for name, child in _nested_callables(node.body).items():
            if id(child) in self._func_param_maps:
                callables[name] = self._func_param_maps[id(child)]
            elif id(child) in self._class_init_maps:
                callables[name] = self._class_init_maps[id(child)]
        self.scopes.append(_Scope(mapping, set(), set(), callables,
                                  is_class=True))
        node.body = self._strip_docstring(node.body)
        node.body = [self.visit(stmt) for stmt in node.body]
        self.scopes.pop()
        return node

    def visit_Lambda(self, node):
        # Defaults are evaluated in the enclosing scope.
        for default in node.args.defaults:
            self.visit(default)
        for default in node.args.kw_defaults:
            if default is not None:
                self.visit(default)
        params = _all_args(node)
        mapping = {name: next(self._name_gen) for name in params
                   if not _is_dunder(name)}
        self.scopes.append(_Scope(mapping, set(), set()))
        for arg in (node.args.posonlyargs + node.args.args
                    + node.args.kwonlyargs
                    + ([node.args.vararg] if node.args.vararg else [])
                    + ([node.args.kwarg] if node.args.kwarg else [])):
            new_name = self._resolve(arg.arg)
            if new_name is not None:
                arg.arg = new_name
        node.body = self.visit(node.body)
        self.scopes.pop()
        return node

    def visit_Call(self, node):
        # Resolve the callee *before* visiting: renaming the callee
        # name would lose the information needed to rewrite keywords.
        param_map = None
        if isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):
            for index in range(len(self.scopes) - 1, -1, -1):
                scope = self.scopes[index]
                if node.func.id in scope.callables:
                    param_map = scope.callables[node.func.id]
                    break
            if param_map is None:
                param_map = self._module_callables.get(node.func.id)
        self.generic_visit(node)
        if param_map:
            for keyword in node.keywords:
                if keyword.arg in param_map:
                    keyword.arg = param_map[keyword.arg]
                    self.keywords_rewritten += 1
        return node

    def _visit_comprehension(self, node):
        # The first generator's iterable is evaluated in the enclosing
        # scope; everything else belongs to the comprehension's scope.
        if node.generators:
